     info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
         .filter(|index| match &read.index {
             Some(name) => &index.name == name,
@@ -17,7 +18,7 @@ pub(super) fn candidates<'a>(info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, types: &[(Ty,
             let expected = if index.computed.contains_key(column) { Ty::Int }
                 else if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(read.table.clone()) }
                 else { info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) };
-            ty.comparable(&expected) || (*literal && expected.is_identity())
+            ty.comparable(&expected) || (!ty.is_optional() && matches!((ty.required(),expected.required()),(Ty::Row(a)|Ty::WriteRow(a),Ty::Ref(b)) if a==b)) || (*literal && expected.is_identity())
         })).collect()
 }
 
diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..3d68f47a6a
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/write_affordances.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,114 @@
+//! Advisory mismatches between a program's caller gate and its table rule.
+use super::catalog::Catalog;
+use crate::{ast::*, Diagnostic};
+use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, RuleExpr, RuleTerm};
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+fn seat(expr: &Expr) -> Option<&str> {
+    let Expr::Binary { op,left,right,.. }=expr else { return None; };
+    if op == "and" { return seat(left).or_else(|| seat(right)); }
+    if op != "=" { return None; }
+    let name = |e: &Expr| matches!(e, Expr::Name { name,.. } if name == "viewer");
+    let value = if name(left) { right.as_ref() } else if name(right) { left.as_ref() } else { return None; };
+    match value { Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(s),.. } if s.starts_with("service:") => Some(s), _=>None }
+}
+fn fields(rule: &RuleExpr) -> Vec<String> {
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Field(f))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Field(f),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>vec![f.clone()],
+        RuleExpr::And(rs)|RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().flat_map(fields).collect(),
+        _=>vec![]
+    }
+}
+fn required_owners(rule:&RuleExpr)->Vec<String> {
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::And(parts)=>parts.iter().flat_map(required_owners).collect(),
+        RuleExpr::Or(parts)=>{
+            let Some(first)=parts.first() else {return vec![]};
+            let mut common=required_owners(first);
+            for part in &parts[1..] {let have=required_owners(part);common.retain(|f|have.contains(f));}
+            common
+        }
+        other=>fields(other),
+    }
+}
+fn admits_seat(rule: &RuleExpr, seat: &str) -> bool {
+    match rule {
+        RuleExpr::Allow|RuleExpr::Public { .. }=>true,
+        RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer,RuleTerm::Literal(v))|RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Literal(v),RuleTerm::Viewer)=>v.as_str().ok()==Some(seat),
+        RuleExpr::Or(rs)=>rs.iter().any(|r|admits_seat(r,seat)),
+        _=>false
+    }
+}
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#7 — these notes state conditional argument requirements, never an impossibility theorem over dynamic inputs.
+pub(super) fn notes(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
+    let mut out=Vec::new();
+    for doc in &catalog.documents {
+        for table in &doc.tables {
+            for column in &table.columns {
+                if column.type_.kind==TypeKind::Principal && let Some(Expr::Literal {value:Literal::Str(value),..})=&column.default
+                    && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p|p==value) {
+                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(&column.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{}.{} defaults to bare persona '{value}', whose dev principal is 'dev:{value}'",table.name,column.name),format!("use `default 'dev:{value}'` for development, or supply the authenticated principal in the mutation for production")).guide("personas"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        for program in &doc.programs {
+            if program.kind == ProgramKind::Query || program.native { continue; }
+            body(catalog,schema,program,&program.body,None,&mut out);
+        }
+    }
+    out
+}
+fn body(catalog:&Catalog,schema:&Schema,program:&ProgramDecl,statements:&[Stmt], mut gate:Option<String>,out:&mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
+    let mut lookups: BTreeMap<String,(String,Vec<String>)>=BTreeMap::new();
+    for statement in statements {
+        if let Stmt::Require { condition,code,site,.. }=statement {
+            if let Some(seat)=seat(condition) { gate=Some(seat.into()); }
+            for (alias,(table,owners)) in &lookups {
+                let mut uses_owner=false; let mut copy=condition.clone();
+                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy,&mut |e| if let Expr::Member { base,name,.. }=e && matches!(base.as_ref(),Expr::Name { name,.. } if name==alias) && owners.contains(name) { uses_owner=true; });
+                if uses_owner && code != "NOT_FOUND" {
+                    out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN",format!("{}: callers who cannot read {table} get NOT_FOUND at `{alias}`'s lookup, never {code} from this require",program.name),
+                        format!("put a caller authorization check before the lookup if {code} must win; otherwise keep NOT_FOUND as the privacy-preserving answer and test it (guide rules)")).guide("rules"));
+                }
+            }
+        }
+        if let Stmt::Bind { names,value:Expr::Assert { value,code,.. },.. }=statement && code=="NOT_FOUND" && let Expr::Read(read)=value.as_ref() {
+            if let [name]=names.as_slice() && let Some(table)=schema.table(&read.table) {
+                let owners=required_owners(&table.rules.read);
+                if !owners.is_empty() { lookups.insert(name.clone(),(read.table.clone(),owners)); }
+            }
+        }
+        let mut copy=vec![statement.clone()];
+        // Inspect only this statement's expressions; nested control flow receives its own gate below.
+        if !matches!(statement,Stmt::If { .. }|Stmt::For { .. }|Stmt::As { .. }) {
+            super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy,&mut |expr| {
+                let Expr::Write { op,table,fields:payload,site,.. }=expr else { return; };
+                let Some(def)=schema.table(table) else { return; };
+                for field in payload.iter() {
+                    let principal=catalog.tables.get(table).and_then(|t|t.columns.get(&field.name)).is_some_and(|t|t.kind==TypeKind::Principal);
+                    if principal && let Some(Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(value),.. })=&field.value
+                        && catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|d|&d.personas).any(|p| p==value) {
+                        out.push(Diagnostic::new(&field.site,"N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL",format!("{table}.{} stores bare persona '{value}', but its dev viewer is 'dev:{value}'",field.name),format!("write 'dev:{value}' for development, or an authenticated account principal in production; personas are names, not principals")).guide("personas"));
+                    }
+                }
+                let Some(seat)=gate.as_deref() else { return; };
+                let effect=match op { WriteOp::Insert=>"insert",WriteOp::Update=>"update", _=>return };
+                let rule=if effect=="insert" { &def.rules.insert } else { &def.rules.update };
+                if admits_seat(rule,seat) { return; }
+                let others=required_owners(rule).into_iter().filter(|owner| payload.iter().find(|f|&f.name==owner).map_or(effect=="update",|f| {
+                    let value=f.value.clone().unwrap_or_else(||Expr::Name { name:f.name.clone(),site:f.site.clone() });
+                    !matches!(value,Expr::Name { ref name,.. } if name=="viewer") && !matches!(value,Expr::Literal { value:Literal::Str(ref s),.. } if s==seat)
+                })).collect::<Vec<_>>();
+                if others.is_empty() { return; }
+                out.push(Diagnostic::new(site,"N_WRITE_DENIED",format!("{} admits only '{seat}', but {table}'s {effect} rule `{}` requires the written {} to be this caller; writing another principal's value refuses E_RULE",program.name,rule.quarry(),others.join(", ")),
+                    format!("if this trusted seat should write other principals' rows, amend `{effect} <- {}` to `{effect} <- ({}) or viewer = '{seat}'`; the mutation require alone grants no table authority (guide deploy, first admin)",rule.quarry(),rule.quarry())).guide("rules"));
+            });
+        }
+        match statement {
+            Stmt::If { condition,then_body,else_body,.. }=> { body(catalog,schema,program,then_body,seat(condition).map(String::from).or_else(||gate.clone()),out); body(catalog,schema,program,else_body,gate.clone(),out); }
+            Stmt::For { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,gate.clone(),out),
+            Stmt::As { body:inner,.. }=>body(catalog,schema,program,inner,None,out),
+            Stmt::Return { .. }|Stmt::Break { .. }|Stmt::Continue { .. }=>break,
+            _=>{}
+        }
+    }
+}

llp/3000.007.003-the-engine-writes-derived-data.rfc.md:8:**Revised:** 2026-09-23 (revision 8: lane INTEGRATION's counterexample. F1 refused a maintained column on any table a timeline maps, so the Clique shape, `likeCount` and its hour index on the posts that Following maps, could not compile. Only a timeline's owned tables are refused now; its mapped tables are ordinary parents and sources, shown by a differential test (§3.1, §12)); 2026-09-22 (revision 7: lane F3b's counterexample and lane F3a's review. The stream re-reads current row images into historical commits, so no write-time check can keep an old commit deliverable. A 146 KB, 1,024-row commit grew to 18.5 MB after 1,021 later one-row updates that each fit. Deliverability is restated: each form bounds its own derived rows, and a lagging client recovers through the existing snapshot restart at limit 1, tested end to end. Owned timeline rows take digest identities derived from their natural keys, never caller-supplied ids, which closes an existence probe on private inboxes; §3.3); 2026-09-22 (revision 6: lane F3a's second counterexample. Lazy author registration moves a write into the first post and breaks "never refuse what the recipe admits" at the write ceiling. Registration is now eager: on profile insert after activation, and once at activation for existing profiles, bounded by the author cap); 2026-09-22 (revision 5: lane F3a's counterexample. The recipe writes a duplicate follow edge, and F3 drops it, so the maximum-backfill promotion uses 1,023 writes against the recipe's 1,024. The recipe-trace oracle allows F3 to admit what the recipe refuses only for that duplicate write, never the reverse); 2026-09-22 (revision 4, after the focused F3 review, READY WITH CHANGES. Inputs are transaction-visible rows applied as actual transitions. F3 gains the removal flag's transition rules, correct attribution of fresh event identity (N1), lazy author registration, a trusted internal write path, the disclosure and device-execution rules, and restoration and import rules. The follow row is corrected to 1,000 source rows read, a 865-event window and the 159 allowance including the application's edge (N2). Deliverability covers composed commits, and F3's oracle is recipe-trace equivalence. Dispositions are in §12); 2026-09-22 (revision 3, after the Astra max delta review found revision 2 still NOT READY on D1 and D4. F2 gains a full contract: preserved recorded paths, activation-time capture, live resolution, field ownership including `Engine::load`, nullability, and event-free activation. F3 names its durable state, gains a declared removal signal and an empty-table first scope, and corrects its history window and bounds. §5's delivery cases are corrected. The §11 decisions were taken under the author's delegation as LLP 3000.007 rulings 45–51. llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2763:| `crates/snapback4-core` | the value model and IR (Snapback 2's serialized forms); the schema with `sync` and the leave policy; one order-preserving key encoding; the two-table SQLite store; rule evaluation with `decided_true`/uniform hoisting; the runtime meter with capped reads and aborted writes; the interpreter (points, scans with opaque cursors, merges, walks, writes with unique/cap/immutable/after-insert constraints, maintained aggregates); the partition log (audience inversion, joins expanded when streamed, scope tombstones, snapshot, merged stream); at-most-once mutations by client-minted id, replayed before the current generation judges a resend; generations on adopt; the day-two classifier; the plain-JSON wire forms; after the two blind reviews: tagged partitions, the audience bound to the read rule's probe index, whole commits per page, same-commit grants withdrawn, completeness as a class, meter marks; then the log keyed by group (1.15× the rows at scale). Corpus: 172 pass, 0 fail, 121 skipped by named reason | `b7449cdaac`, `1ff36fe04d`, the reviews landing |
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2765:| `loopbar/` | the loop-bar brief (reduced Clique M1–M3, four-part proof) and the serial runner with independent verification → `metrics/loopbar.jsonl`; **the bar met on 0.0.11** (3/3 green in 443–537 s, no attributed loop; record under `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-12-snapback4-loopbar/`) | `11c5202532`, the loop-bar landing |
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2782:| `benchmarks/runs/2026-09-13-snapback4-clique-0003/` | the Clique hub run on 0.1.0, the whole brief, nothing struck: every milestone green in 7,002 s (M8 at 6,926 s), graded 84/1/16 of 101 (O1 62, O2 48, O4 35, O5 band 30), M7 capability-failure on the app's pre-existing logout crash after delivering its polls and migration; eight fix-forward releases the same night (0.1.1–0.1.8: one thread per request, asset facts on the argument, `a if cond else b`, `sum(page, .x)`, a one-second clock, the paged snapshot with `N_NO_HORIZON`, the outbox in submission order, `N_WIDE_MERGE`); then what the run said to do next: the `feeds` card (fan-out on write, compiled and run in `tests/clique.rs`), `onSession`/`useSession` for the sign-out contract the app's logout crash exposed, and the simulator witness proving a cold start offline and writes composed offline surviving a restart on SQLite | the run's landings, 0.1.12 |
llp/3000-snapback4.explainer.md:2784:| `crates/snapback4-device` | one device over a `DeviceStore` (`store.rs`): `device.rs` applies the stream with the watermark, stages paged snapshots, keeps the outbox in submission order over confirmed images, answers queries and predictions through `engine::local`; `sqlite.rs` binds the core's store on a native host with the C ABI (`ffi.rs`, `include/snapback4.h`); `memory.rs` is the store for the web, transactions as an undo log, each commit's changes reported for the host to persist; `call.rs` is the one JSON dispatcher; `web.rs` the wasm surface (open with what the host kept, call, drain). `tests/device.rs` runs every scenario on both stores against a real engine; `tests/native.rs` what only SQLite has (two devices on one store, a failing store). Measured whole as wasm: 312,924 B gzip before the diet | the native landing; the one-implementation landing |

llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:2320:| A secondary hidden unique collision during an update is `NOT_FOUND` even when the target is readable; §8.7 (f) and S05 say `E_RULE` | GPT 5, Grok 1 | Accepted: `E_RULE` (generic, no index, no key) when the target is readable; `NOT_FOUND` when the target is unreadable, which is stricter than (f) and is now written into (f); the regression expects both |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:2321:| The absent-target upsert path is not one word with the hidden-refused path: a complete patch naming an unknown or foreign asset raises `E_ASSET_UNKNOWN` before insert authority when the row is absent, `NOT_FOUND` when it is hidden; the hidden path evaluates the read rule's probes and meters the merged image, the absent path neither | GPT 2 | Accepted: on the absent branch the insert rule is decided before any asset or constraint diagnostic, and a refusal is `NOT_FOUND`; the meter is marked before target resolution and restored on every `NOT_FOUND`, so hidden-refused and absent cost the same on every meter field; paired regression with the complete patch and a foreign asset, comparing meters |

llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:2342:| A uniformly decided page over a hidden prefix charges an examined row before its verdict, so a meter already at the ceiling aborts for one hidden row and commits for none; a uniform rule whose `exists` probe crosses an expiring table charges that table's expired siblings the same way | GPT 1 | Accepted: the verdict of a uniformly decided rule is established from the pinned prefix before any candidate is examined, and a false verdict returns the empty complete page at no charge; a rule probe costs one unit whatever expired siblings it passes, or such rules are excluded from "uniform" for mutation pages; the plain-prefix restriction of row 6 applies to every page a mutation reads, predicting or not, looped or bound (Grok's untested residual) |

llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:2598:**2026-09-17 — lane MQ implementation.** A binder-proven, cursor-free query that is not declared `held` and returns a record of independent fields, each holding at most one read, with no reading `require`, can compose retained and authoritative reads. A found point read of a delivered left row counts as retained. Composition is entered only when at least one field routes to the server and at least one is retained. Every field that would stay local as its own query comes from a second local observation, and only the server-routed fields come from the original query's one server reply. The result's `complete` is the conjunction of the server word and every locally kept field's word; it reports `fresh: false`, no `since`, and no continuation. Composition is never entered while a predictable write is unobserved; the whole local answer stands until it settles. Offline or on request failure, the whole local answer stands. The server and the wire are unchanged.

llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7713:| L14 | **The horizon repair precedes maintained columns**, and gate 5 of LLP 3000.007.001 becomes the build lane's milestone zero (§21.10). The defect is filed P1 and handed to the wave 13 lead, who owns read completeness and is already in `engine/read.rs`'s horizon check; this session briefs the repair only if that lead declines it. **Resolved the same day: the wave 13 lead took it as lane W13g** — a correctness landing under §20.22's invariant, launched after W13c leaves `read.rs`, reviewed blind by two families, shipping with 0.2.29; this session stays out of `read.rs` and `device.rs`. W13g then **proved the boundary cannot be inferred from row images** (the stream serves current images, so a mutable ordering key can move below the floor before a device sees it; two histories with different floors produce byte-identical payloads) and the wave 13 lead authorised an additive snapshot field carrying each completed horizoned group's floor (lane W13g-2, the field under the same two-family review). **Landed on main 2026-09-17 from the reviewed tip `e4f274b479` after five blind rounds (§20.22.7); the acquisition's selection time is put to the author there.** | LLP 3000.007.001 §3.6 stated its rule before the measurement: past 10%, a device-side repair is designed and reviewed first. Measured: 58.3%. The alternative — refusing the column on horizoned tables — would refuse the flagship case and send builders back to the hand-kept form, which has the same defect and `update <- allow` besides. Gate 5 measures activation, which does not exist until it is built; a prototype measured first, with a stop, is the only honest reading of "before a lane is briefed" |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7715:| L16 | W15a's brief is corrected, not the ruling: **a held poll under a proven absence guard charges the server exactly what a graced recipient charges**, field by field; absent, graced and every unchanged form charge what they did; device prediction is unchanged; and no server case may exceed what the prediction reserves (the lane stops if one does). The lane continues as W15a-2 | The first session used its stop clause on a real conflict: the brief asked for the emit to execute *and* for server meters identical to a base that skipped it (3 steps and 202 heap bytes for the minimal program). Its measurements price ruling 32 for the author: a 50-member send with all 49 recipients connected goes from 817 steps / 43,297 heap / 0 effect rows to the graced case's 1,356 / 69,400 / 49 effect rows + 49 dependency rows, every one satisfied with zero worker attempts when the clients re-poll. It also makes the server's charges independent of whether a recipient is held, graced or absent, which ruling 18 wanted and the base did not have (6 steps against 9) |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7716:| L17 | After W15a's first blind review (GPT NOT READY, Grok READY WITH CHANGES): **"held never charges more than graced" is withdrawn**. The contract is: prediction, absent, graced and unchanged forms charge exactly what they did; for the paved form — presence values feeding only proven absence guards — held equals graced field by field; and in *every* composition the held case charges at most what device prediction reserves (prediction evaluates the connected path and charges every safe untaken arm, which includes the projected absence arms), the ticketed six-byte divergence excepted. Hedging stays: with `if not online: emit A` and `if online: emit B`, an unconfirmed recipient gets A deferred (cancelled if they return) **and** B. A fix session applies this with the review's other findings — an exponential, unmetered setup walk on alias chains; the server-only projection compiled into the wasm device; "never dropped" restated as durable admission — and the fixed commit goes back to both families | GPT showed L16's closing claim — that the change makes the server's charges independent of the recipient's connection state — is true only of the paved form: a payload that keeps its ordinary value makes a held recipient run work a graced one skips. The alternative that would make it true everywhere is for the **server to charge every safe presence arm as prediction does**, so that presence never changes a meter on any host. That changes what absent and graced mutations are charged today, reaches every effects-only mutation and the cards' cost statements, and is therefore **put to the author rather than built here** |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7727:| L28 | **L26 amended: cardinality is provenance of its own.** W15b's fourth round — both families, independently — showed L26's clause "how many rows a loop sums" was too broad: `count(xs { … })` over an argument list stores `count(xs)`, and `for i in range(0, amount): delta = delta + 1` stores `amount`; in both the caller supplies the number, and can set 999 in one call. A collection carries a cardinality provenance beside its items' data: READ for a read page, ARGUMENT for an argument list or an argument-bounded range, AMBIENT for a literal; shaping, filtering, slicing and sorting preserve it; `count`/`len` return it as data; a loop accumulation over a domain takes the domain's. A finite choice among ambient constants stays selection. Under L27 the round-4 finding stops the landing; the fix is one more session and a fifth round, confirmatory | Two families reaching the same rule from two constructions is the strongest evidence a review loop produces; the rule is now stated in the terms the pass computes (data, control, cardinality), which is what makes the next round able to confirm rather than construct |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7728:| L29 | **Loops prefer silence.** W15b's fifth round (one family READY; the other one Medium: a conditional reset inside an argument-bounded loop let the join drop the domain's cardinality) is the fifth loop or join shape the pass had modelled optimistically. The loop rule is made shape-independent: every binding assigned anywhere inside a loop body takes the domain's cardinality into its data — ARGUMENT for an argument list or argument-bounded range, UNKNOWN for an unknown domain, READ for a read domain — regardless of resets, joins, breaks or position. The accepted cost: an unconditional reset inside an argument-bounded loop (which stores 1) is silent — a false negative on a program no builder writes. **The sixth round is the last**: a wording finding is applied and the lane lands; a behavioural finding is met by extending the same conservatism (the construct goes UNKNOWN) and the lane lands without a further round | A note's only unacceptable outcome is a note on a caller-supplied value; precision inside loops bought nothing but review rounds. The bound on rounds is stated before the round, as L22 and L27 were |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7742:| **High.** The argument fails for authorization established *inside* the mutation: a viewer who may write a hidden gate row enables it, binds the expiring page of that gate's room, disables it; abort-versus-commit then shows the unswept population of a prefix no query by that viewer could examine | GPT 1 | Accepted, conservatively: a new condition, *a stable verdict* — no write to a table the page's read rule depends on (each table its `exists` probes name, and a probed maintain's source) can execute before the page's scan: none earlier in statement order, none inside a loop that encloses the scan. The broader contract — transaction-local authorization as an accepted disclosure — is **not** proposed; it would be the author's ruling |
llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7760:| **High.** A stable verdict is not a stable examined population. With `read <- .owner = viewer`, a blind update the viewer's write authority admits can set a hidden row's `owner` to the viewer and its expiry to the past; a page over the viewer's own prefix `[viewer, 'A']` then examines that row or does not, and `E_BOUND` versus the program's own refusal reveals the hidden row's retained column. D(page) is empty — the rule has no probe — and a snapshot query of the prefix is empty in both worlds | GPT 1 | The page's *table*, and every maintain source that can change it, must also be unwritten before the scan |

 succeeded in 0ms:
   180	## 7. Wave 11 diagnostic precision (QDIAG)
   181	
   182	The nullable-reference lint counts a null value as needing no target lookup.
   183	At `if arg != null`, only the null edge carries that exemption; a lookup on
   184	the other edge must still dominate the write. Join by intersection and clear
   185	facts on rebinding. This changes advisory output only.
   186	
   187	Operation parameter defaults remain explicit: declare an optional parameter
   188	and bind `value = arg ?? literal`. Refuse `= literal` with that exact idiom.
   189	A multi-line `require` keeps `else CODE` on its closing line; the refusal names
   190	that line and preserves the author's code. `?` precedes `on delete keep`.
   191	
   192	Program absence checks on a row lookup (`t[key] = null`) use the unreadable
   193	probe note too. Raw table-rule probes, including delete rules, still observe
   194	all rows; the note's repair states that distinction. A later owner `require`
   195	after `! else NOT_FOUND` gets a refusal-order note only when every read-rule
   196	branch requires that same owner. A branch admitting staff or another party
   197	can legitimately reach the custom refusal and keeps the note quiet.
   198	
   199	A child-owner proof requires equality in every child insert branch, immutable
   200	(or unupdatable) child owner/reference and parent owner, no maintained or
   201	field-owned value, no opaque/job writer, no kept parent identity that ordinary deletion/expiry could recreate,
   202	and equal read rules after replacing
   203	only that owner. No other row fields may appear in either rule. A successful
   204	parent lookup (or a direct probe in its returned row projection) supplies the
   205	parent-readable fact. This is advisory analysis of ordinary admitted writes;
   206	trusted system seeds must preserve the declared application invariant. It never
   207	changes read authority, admission or completeness.
   208	
   209	## 8. Wave 11 contextual spellings and refusal codes (QDIAG amendment)
   210	
   211	A required same-table row in a reference-typed column, point key, or operation
   212	argument lowers to its `.id`. This includes an inserted snapshot and a looked-up
   213	snapshot; it evaluates its expression once in source order, introduces no
   214	lookup, and grants no existence or read authority. Shorthand fields get the same
   215	projection. Cross-table rows and optional rows remain refused; an optional
   216	upsert must be narrowed with `! else CODE` first. A generic `id` or `principal`
   217	position does not implicitly project a row. Helper parameters retain their
   218	existing call-site row specialization (LLP 3000.007 §5.2; the existing helper implementation specializes same-table rows at the call site): a helper called with
   219	a row keeps its row fields, and one called with a reference must look it up.
   220	Lists and objects receive no deep automatic projection. Explicit `.id` remains
   221	valid. Generated API and runtime IR remain those of the explicit spelling.
   222	
   223	Image and video bounds accept integer decimal byte units KB, MB and GB; 5MB
   224	means 5,000,000 bytes. Overflow above u32 refuses at parsing. This is a constant
   225	spelling, not a new value type, upload format, meter or protocol capability.
   226	
   227	Readable secondary unique collisions with no authored `else CODE` now return
   228	E_UNIQUE and name the declared key columns, never an internal planner index.
   229	Authored codes still win. The intrinsic id conflict remains E_CONSTRAINT.
   230	Hidden collisions retain E_RULE (and hidden write targets retain NOT_FOUND),
   231	including their deliberately ambiguous public message: a rule denial, unreadable
   232	key holder or physical authorization cut remain indistinguishable. The wording
   233	says that a row you cannot read holds this key as one possible cause. Only the
   234	developer-owned trace may identify the actual private clause. No new hidden
   235	existence bit is exposed; this preserves LLP 3000 §7 and this RFC §5.
   236	
   237	The engine/model/oracles preserve rollback, conflict order, null-key uniqueness,
   238	if-absent behavior, device prediction and unchanged named refusal precedence.
   239	Lean's visible secondary conflict code follows E_UNIQUE; explicit row projection
   240	has the existing field semantics. Generated equivalent implicit/explicit sources,
   241	Lean examples, simulator and both device stores exercise the projection and
   242	unique conflict. Native and Lean fixed-seed runs must add no failure signatures.
   243	
   244	A development-only check rejects an operation principal argument equal to a
   245	bare declared persona (alice), naming dev:alice. Static principal column literals
   246	get advisory N_PERSONA_PRINCIPAL. Production HTTP does not perform the dev argument check: arbitrary account
   247	principals remain valid and personas confer no authority. The static note is
   248	a compilation advisory whenever `use personas` declares the matching name,
   249	including a production schema that retains its development cast. Developer query/test
   250	output may count observed physical rows filtered by their read rules through
   251	an opt-in collector, outside the wire envelope and durable receipts. It never
   252	adds hidden counts to serve, browser responses, sync, the device or output data;
   253	counts describe encountered rows, not the whole unreadable population.
   254	
   255	Every service-seat E_RULE path, durable or ephemeral, for an ordinary table denial, hidden collision
   256	or authorization exhaustion appends the same static authored table rule clause.
   257	It never identifies which cause occurred, or copies row values. This fulfils
   258	the seat explanation without relaxing hidden-collision ambiguity.
   259	
   260	The independent Lean surface emitter projects known row-valued columns and
   261	keys from its own source types. Unresolved reference expression types and row
   262	helper specialization outside its fragment are named unsupported cases, never
   263	accepted oracle cases: reference-expression-type and row-helper-snapshot.
   264	The native/compiler and two-device witnesses still exercise row helper fields.
  1085	## 12. Wave 11 cap recipe (QDIAG, 2026-10-14)
  1086	
  1087	The resource law and runtime prices above are unchanged. `check` now computes
  1088	one recipe for the whole delete across its children, using the ordinary action
  1089	reservation plus the root write. Trial caps are evaluated together against all
  1090	seven ceilings, including rows and heap, rather than proposing 500 per child
  1091	and revealing the summed refusal later. The recipe reports rows/heap and names
  1092	each cap to apply. A maintained count source commonly reserves three writes
  1093	per child (the row and its old/new aggregate groups). Fixed transient and
  1094	retained-event heap matter as well. Existing smaller caps/unique bounds stay
  1095	smaller; a cap is always a product decision. Work earlier in the mutation uses
  1096	the same meter, so this is a reservation recipe, not a claim for arbitrary
  1097	preceding work. If no fixed fitting recipe exists, restrict and a job are the
  1098	repair. A maintained-sum source remains excluded from cascades; ordinary
  1099	system-job deletes unwind each contribution before the marked parent is deleted.

llp/3000.007.003-the-engine-writes-derived-data.rfc.md:8:**Revised:** 2026-09-23 (revision 8: lane INTEGRATION's counterexample. F1 refused a maintained column on any table a timeline maps, so the Clique shape, `likeCount` and its hour index on the posts that Following maps, could not compile. Only a timeline's owned tables are refused now; its mapped tables are ordinary parents and sources, shown by a differential test (§3.1, §12)); 2026-09-22 (revision 7: lane F3b's counterexample and lane F3a's review. The stream re-reads current row images into historical commits, so no write-time check can keep an old commit deliverable. A 146 KB, 1,024-row commit grew to 18.5 MB after 1,021 later one-row updates that each fit. Deliverability is restated: each form bounds its own derived rows, and a lagging client recovers through the existing snapshot restart at limit 1, tested end to end. Owned timeline rows take digest identities derived from their natural keys, never caller-supplied ids, which closes an existence probe on private inboxes; §3.3); 2026-09-22 (revision 6: lane F3a's second counterexample. Lazy author registration moves a write into the first post and breaks "never refuse what the recipe admits" at the write ceiling. Registration is now eager: on profile insert after activation, and once at activation for existing profiles, bounded by the author cap); 2026-09-22 (revision 5: lane F3a's counterexample. The recipe writes a duplicate follow edge, and F3 drops it, so the maximum-backfill promotion uses 1,023 writes against the recipe's 1,024. The recipe-trace oracle allows F3 to admit what the recipe refuses only for that duplicate write, never the reverse); 2026-09-22 (revision 4, after the focused F3 review, READY WITH CHANGES. Inputs are transaction-visible rows applied as actual transitions. F3 gains the removal flag's transition rules, correct attribution of fresh event identity (N1), lazy author registration, a trusted internal write path, the disclosure and device-execution rules, and restoration and import rules. The follow row is corrected to 1,000 source rows read, a 865-event window and the 159 allowance including the application's edge (N2). Deliverability covers composed commits, and F3's oracle is recipe-trace equivalence. Dispositions are in §12); 2026-09-22 (revision 3, after the Astra max delta review found revision 2 still NOT READY on D1 and D4. F2 gains a full contract: preserved recorded paths, activation-time capture, live resolution, field ownership including `Engine::load`, nullability, and event-free activation. F3 names its durable state, gains a declared removal signal and an empty-table first scope, and corrects its history window and bounds. §5's delivery cases are corrected. The §11 decisions were taken under the author's delegation as LLP 3000.007 rulings 45–51. Dispositions are in §12); 2026-09-22 (revision 2, after the [Astra max review](./reviews/3000.012-application-still-owns-derived-data.astra.md) found revision 1 NOT READY: F2 becomes engine-recorded attribution over public targets; F3 states the recipe's answer contract and operation matrix within its supported shape; the substrate test becomes shared interfaces with per-form lifecycle policies; MC1 is framed as a gate revision; the acceptance counters are corrected; dispositions in §12); 2026-09-22 (initial)

llp/3000.007-relaxed-quarry-and-explicit-residency.rfc.md:7713:| L14 | **The horizon repair precedes maintained columns**, and gate 5 of LLP 3000.007.001 becomes the build lane's milestone zero (§21.10). The defect is filed P1 and handed to the wave 13 lead, who owns read completeness and is already in `engine/read.rs`'s horizon check; this session briefs the repair only if that lead declines it. **Resolved the same day: the wave 13 lead took it as lane W13g** — a correctness landing under §20.22's invariant, launched after W13c leaves `read.rs`, reviewed blind by two families, shipping with 0.2.29; this session stays out of `read.rs` and `device.rs`. W13g then **proved the boundary cannot be inferred from row images** (the stream serves current images, so a mutable ordering key can move below the floor before a device sees it; two histories with different floors produce byte-identical payloads) and the wave 13 lead authorised an additive snapshot field carrying each completed horizoned group's floor (lane W13g-2, the field under the same two-family review). **Landed on main 2026-09-17 from the reviewed tip `e4f274b479` after five blind rounds (§20.22.7); the acquisition's selection time is put to the author there.** | LLP 3000.007.001 §3.6 stated its rule before the measurement: past 10%, a device-side repair is designed and reviewed first. Measured: 58.3%. The alternative — refusing the column on horizoned tables — would refuse the flagship case and send builders back to the hand-kept form, which has the same defect and `update <- allow` besides. Gate 5 measures activation, which does not exist until it is built; a prototype measured first, with a stop, is the only honest reading of "before a lane is briefed" |

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/cascade_repair.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/cascade_repair.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..de563fb355
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/cascade_repair.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,56 @@
+//! A fitting recipe for the whole delete, evaluated with the engine's prices.
+use snapback4_core::schema::{Schema, Constraint, references::{OnDelete, Price}};
+use std::collections::BTreeSet;
+
+pub(super) fn reaches(schema:&Schema,root:&str,target:&str)->bool {
+    let mut seen=BTreeSet::from([root.to_owned()]);let mut todo=vec![root.to_owned()];
+    while let Some(parent)=todo.pop() {
+        if parent==target {return true;}
+        for r in schema.referencing(&parent) {if r.action==OnDelete::Cascade && seen.insert(r.child.into()) {todo.push(r.child.into());}}
+    }
+    false
+}
+
+// @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — caps share one delete reservation, never a per-child allowance.
+pub(super) fn recipe(schema: &Schema, parent: &str, job: bool) -> String {
+    let mut targets = BTreeSet::new();
+    let mut reached = BTreeSet::from([parent.to_owned()]);
+    let mut todo = vec![parent.to_owned()];
+    while let Some(table) = todo.pop() {
+        for r in schema.referencing(&table) {
+            if r.action.writes() { targets.insert((r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned())); }
+            if r.action == OnDelete::Cascade && reached.insert(r.child.to_owned()) { todo.push(r.child.to_owned()); }
+        }
+    }
+    let candidate = |cap: u32| {
+        let mut trial = schema.clone();
+        for (child, column) in &targets {
+            // A unique reference already has the tightest possible bound.
+            if trial.reference_cap(child, column) == Some(1) { continue; }
+            let table = trial.tables.get_mut(child).unwrap();
+            let mut found = false;
+            for constraint in &mut table.constraints {
+                if let Constraint::Cap { by, max } = constraint && by.len() == 1 && &by[0] == column {
+                    *max = (*max).min(cap); found = true;
+                }
+            }
+            if !found { table.constraints.push(Constraint::Cap { by: vec![column.clone()], max: cap }); }
+        }
+        trial.prepare_write_reservations();
+        let price = trial.delete_total_reservation(parent)?;
+        Some((trial, price))
+    };
+    let fits = |price: &Price| super::reference_actions::over_ceiling(price, job).is_none();
+    let fallback = format!("keep `on delete restrict` and delete the child rows first in a job (`for row in <child>:`), then delete the {parent} row; choose caps only if they are product limits (guide writes)");
+    if !candidate(1).is_some_and(|(_, price)| fits(&price)) { return format!("even cap 1 cannot fit this delete's fixed heap/row reservation; {fallback}"); }
+    let (mut low, mut high) = (1, 500);
+    while low < high {
+        let mid = low + (high - low + 1) / 2;
+        if candidate(mid).is_some_and(|(_, price)| fits(&price)) { low = mid; } else { high = mid - 1; }
+    }
+    let (trial, price) = candidate(low).unwrap();
+    let unique=targets.iter().map(|(child,column)|format!("`unique {column}` on {child}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or ");
+    let caps = targets.iter().map(|(child, column)| format!("`cap {} by {column}` on {child}", trial.reference_cap(child, column).unwrap())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
+    let limits = if job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() };
+    format!("apply {caps} together: the whole delete (including the root) reserves {} / {} rows written and {} / {} heap bytes, with all indexes, derived writes and event images priced together. A count-maintained source commonly reserves 3 × its rows (the row plus its old and new aggregate groups), not one row per child; for a one-to-one relation use {unique} instead; {fallback}", price[3], limits.rows_written, price[4], limits.heap_bytes)
+}

@@ -624,6 +624,23 @@ impl Binder<'_> {
         self.expr(expr, out)
     }
 
+    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
+        let (value, ty) = self.expr_as_identity(expr, out, expected)?;
+        Ok(self.row_identity(value, ty, expected))
+    }
+
+    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — contextual projection is exactly `.id`,
+    // without a lookup; optional and cross-table rows still need an edit.
+    pub(super) fn row_identity(&self, value: Expr, ty: Ty, expected: &Ty) -> (Expr, Ty) {
+        if !ty.is_optional() && matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::WriteRow(_)) {
+            let table = match ty.required() { Ty::Row(t) | Ty::WriteRow(t) => t, _ => unreachable!() };
+            if expected.required() == Ty::Ref(table.clone()) {
+                return (ir::field(value, "id"), Ty::Ref(table));
+            }
+        }
+        (value, ty)
+    }
+
     pub(super) fn call(&mut self, name: &str, args: &[A], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
         if let Some(result) = self.job_query_call(name, args, site, out) { return result; }
         // Compiler-only references bypass the function catalog entirely.

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/types.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/types.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..31869c30ea
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/parser/types.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,202 @@
+//! Column and parameter types, including precise unsupported-default repairs.
+use super::Parser;
+use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Fallible, Site, syntax, syntax_with}, lexer::Kind, print};
+impl Parser {
+    pub(super) fn parse_args(&mut self) -> Fallible<Vec<ArgDecl>> {
+        self.take("(")?;
+        let mut args = Vec::new();
+        while !self.at(")") {
+            let name = self.peek().clone();
+            let binding = self.local_name()?;
+            self.take(":")?;
+            let type_site = self.peek().site.clone();
+            let type_ = self.parse_type(false).map_err(|mut d| {
+                if d.mechanical && d.message.starts_with("once is a column modifier") {
+                    // Nested element types need a whole-list rewrite, not an argument fragment.
+                    if d.site == type_site { d.site = name.site.clone(); d.rewrite = format!("{binding}: {}", d.rewrite); }
+                    else { d.mechanical = false; }
+                }
+                d
+            })?;
+            if self.at("=") || self.at("default") {
+                let at = self.peek().site.clone();
+                self.position += 1;
+                let fallback = self.expression(0, &[",", ")"])?;
+                let mut optional = type_.clone(); optional.optional = true;
+                return Err(syntax_with(&at, "operation parameters have no literal defaults; omission is null for an optional parameter",
+                    format!("declare `{binding}: {}` and bind `{binding}Value = {binding} ?? {}` at the start of the body", print::scalar_type(&optional), print::expression(&fallback))).guide("types"));
+            }
+            args.push(ArgDecl { name: binding, type_, site: name.site });
+            if !self.maybe(",") {
+                break;
+            }
+        }
+        self.take(")")?;
+        Ok(args)
+    }
+
+    pub(super) fn parse_type(&mut self, column: bool) -> Fallible<ScalarType> {
+        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
+        let mut kind;
+        if self.maybe("[") {
+            let item = self.parse_type(column)?;
+            self.take("]")?;
+            // @ref LLP 3000.007#5.4 — list ranges share text's inclusive spelling.
+            let bounds_error = |site: &Site| syntax_with(site, "list bounds require <=N or an inclusive a..b range", "write [T] <=N or [T] a..b, for example [int] 1..8").guide("types");
+            let number = |parser: &mut Self| -> Fallible<u32> {
+                if parser.at("-") {
+                    return Err(syntax(&parser.peek().site, "list bounds must be nonnegative integers"));
+                }
+                if !parser.at_kind(Kind::Number) { return Err(bounds_error(&parser.peek().site)); }
+                let token = parser.take_kind(Kind::Number)?;
+                if token.value.contains('.') {
+                    return Err(syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers; fractional bounds are not allowed"));
+                }
+                token.value.parse::<u32>().map_err(|_| syntax(&token.site, "list bounds must be integers from 0 through 4294967295"))
+            };
+            let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
+                (0, number(self)?)
+            } else {
+                let min = number(self)?;
+                if !self.maybe(".") || !self.maybe(".") { return Err(bounds_error(&self.peek().site)); }
+                (min, number(self)?)
+            };
+            if min > max {
+                return Err(syntax(&site, "list minimum must not exceed its maximum"));
+            }
+            if item.optional {
+                return Err(syntax(&site, "list element types cannot be optional"));
+            }
+            kind = TypeKind::List { item: Box::new(item), min, max };
+        } else {
+            let name = self.word()?;
+            kind = match name.value.as_str() {
+                "text" => {
+                    let (min, max) = if self.maybe("<=") {
+                        (None, self.number()?)
+                    } else if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) {
+                        let min = self.number()?;
+                        self.take(".")?;
+                        self.take(".")?;
+                        (Some(min), self.number()?)
+                    } else {
+                        // The default is normalized here; every downstream
+                        // validator and resource calculation sees the same bound.
+                        (None, 10_000)
+                    };
+                    let mut format = None;
+                    if self.maybe("format") {
+                        let value = self.word()?;
+                        if value.value != "handle" && value.value != "url" {
+                            let bound = match min {
+                                Some(min) => format!("{min}..{max}"),
+                                None => format!("<={max}"),
+                            };
+                            return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("unknown text format {}", value.value), format!("use text {bound} format handle or text {bound} format url")));
+                        }
+                        format = Some(value.value);
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::Text { min, max, format }
+                }
+                "decimal" => TypeKind::Decimal { scale: self.scale("decimal 12")? },
+                "money" => {
+                    let currency = self.word()?;
+                    if currency.value.len() != 3 || !currency.value.bytes().all(|b| b.is_ascii_uppercase()) {
+                        return Err(syntax_with(&currency.site, "money currency must be exactly three uppercase letters", "use money USD or money USD 2"));
+                    }
+                    let scale = if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) { self.scale(&format!("money {} 12", currency.value))? } else { 2 };
+                    TypeKind::Money { currency: currency.value, scale }
+                }
+                "json" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Json { max: self.number()? }
+                }
+                "bytes" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Bytes { max: self.number()? }
+                }
+                "image" => {
+                    if !self.maybe("<=") { return Err(syntax_with(&site, "image needs a maximum upload size in bytes", "write `image <=5MB` (5,000,000 bytes), optionally followed by once or ?").guide("types")); }
+                    TypeKind::Image { max: self.media_size()?, once: false }
+                }
+                "video" => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    TypeKind::Video { max: self.media_size()? }
+                }
+                // `file` is a type only before `<=`: a table named `file` stays a reference.
+                // @ref LLP 3000.038#1 — the allow-list names only types the server can verify against the bytes.
+                "file" if self.at("<=") => {
+                    self.take("<=")?;
+                    let max = self.number()?;
+                    let mut types = Vec::new();
+                    if self.maybe("(") {
+                        while !self.at(")") {
+                            let value = self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?;
+                            if let Some(why) = snapback4_core::assets::file_type_refusal(&value.value) {
+                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, why, format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf', 'text/plain')")).guide("assets"));
+                            }
+                            if types.contains(&value.value) {
+                                return Err(syntax_with(&value.site, format!("'{}' is listed twice", value.value), "list each media type once"));
+                            }
+                            types.push(value.value);
+                            if !self.maybe(",") { break; }
+                        }
+                        self.take(")")?;
+                        if types.is_empty() {
+                            return Err(syntax_with(&site, "a file allow-list needs at least one media type", format!("file <={max} ('application/pdf'), or file <={max} for every verifiable type")).guide("assets"));
+                        }
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::File { max, types }
+                }
+                "cursor" => TypeKind::Cursor,
+                "id" => TypeKind::Id,
+                "principal" => TypeKind::Principal,
+                "int" if self.at_kind(Kind::Number) || matches!(self.peek().value.as_str(), "<=" | ">=" | "<" | ">" | "-" | ".") => {
+                    let (min, max) = self.int_range(&name.site)?;
+                    TypeKind::IntRange { min, max }
+                }
+                "int" => TypeKind::Int,
+                "time" => TypeKind::Time,
+                "bool" => TypeKind::Bool,
+                "enum" => {
+                    self.take("(")?;
+                    let mut variants = Vec::new();
+                    while !self.at(")") {
+                        variants.push(self.take_kind(Kind::Str)?.value);
+                        if !self.maybe(",") {
+                            break;
+                        }
+                    }
+                    self.take(")")?;
+                    if variants.is_empty() {
+                        return Err(syntax_with(&site, "enum needs at least one variant", "enum('draft', 'published')"));
+                    }
+                    TypeKind::Enum { variants }
+                }
+                other => TypeKind::Ref { table: other.to_owned() },
+            };
+        }
+        if self.maybe("once") {
+            if !column {
+                let optional = self.maybe("?");
+                return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is a column modifier, not an argument constraint; declare it on the owning table column", print::scalar_type(&ScalarType { kind, optional })).mechanical());
+            }
+            match &mut kind {
+                TypeKind::Image { once, .. } => *once = true,
+                TypeKind::Video { .. } => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns; video ranges are unspecified", "use photo: image <=10000000 once, or remove once from the video column")),
+                TypeKind::File { .. } => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns; a downloaded file is saved by the browser, so one view cannot be kept", "remove once from the file column; use image <=10000000 once for one-view photos").guide("assets")),
+                _ => return Err(syntax_with(&site, "once is only specified for image columns", "remove once from this column; use image <=10000000 once for one-use images")),
+            }
+        }
+        let optional = self.maybe("?");
+        Ok(ScalarType { kind, optional })
+    }
+
+    pub(super) fn media_size(&mut self) -> Fallible<u32> {
+        let site = self.peek().site.clone();
+        let size = self.number()?;
+        let multiplier = match self.peek().value.as_str() { "KB" => 1_000, "MB" => 1_000_000, "GB" => 1_000_000_000, _ => return Ok(size) };
+        self.position += 1;
+        size.checked_mul(multiplier).ok_or_else(|| syntax_with(&site, "upload bound exceeds 4,294,967,295 bytes", "use image <=5MB or a byte count fitting u32").guide("types"))
+    }
+}

use snapback4_lang::compile;
fn checked(source: &str) -> snapback4_lang::Bound { compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]) }
const REFS: &str = "table parents:\n  read <- allow\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents ? on delete keep\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\n";
#[test]
fn optional_reference_checks_join_the_null_edge() {
    for condition in ["parent != null", "not (parent = null)", "null != parent"] {
        let b = checked(&format!("{REFS}mutation add(parent: parents ?):\n  if {condition}:\n    row = parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  insert kids {{ parent }}\n"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    }
    for body in ["  if parent != null:\n    unused = true\n", "  if parent != null and skip:\n    row = parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND\n"] {
        let b = checked(&format!("{REFS}mutation add(parent: parents ?, other: parents ?, skip: bool):\n{body}  insert kids {{ parent }}\n"));
        assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
        assert!(b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_REFERENCE_UNCHECKED"), "{:?}", b.notes);
    }
}
#[test]
fn syntax_repairs_name_the_actual_form() {
    let b = checked("mutation add(body: text <=20 = 'hi'):\n  return body\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite.contains("body ?? 'hi'") && d.rewrite.contains("text <=20 ?")), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let b = checked("mutation add(a: bool, b: bool):\n  require (a or\n    b)\n  else NOPE\n  return true\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite.contains("closing line") && d.rewrite.contains("else NOPE")), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
    let b = checked("table parents:\n  public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep ?\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.rewrite == "parent: parents ? on delete keep"), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
}

#[test]
fn whole_cascade_recipe_fits_once_across_children() {
    let source="table roots:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  delete <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable votes:\n  root: roots on delete cascade\n  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:\n  root: roots on delete cascade\n  owner: principal\n  body: text <=10000\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\nmutation remove(root: roots):\n  delete roots[root]\n";
    for source in [source.to_owned(),source.replace("  owner: principal\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:","  owner: principal\n  cap 500 by root\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  sync\ntable comments:").replace("  body: text <=10000\n","  body: text <=10000\n  cap 500 by root\n")] {
        let b=checked(&source);
        let d=b.diagnostics.iter().find(|d|d.code=="E_CASCADE_BOUND").unwrap_or_else(||panic!("{:?}",b.diagnostics));
        assert!(d.rewrite.contains("heap bytes") && d.rewrite.contains("3 ×"),"{}",d.rewrite);
        let caps: Vec<_>=d.rewrite.split('`').filter(|s|s.starts_with("cap ")).collect();
        assert_eq!(caps.len(),2,"{}",d.rewrite);
        let edited=source.lines().filter(|line|!line.trim().starts_with("cap 500")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("\n");
        let edited=edited.replace("  root: roots on delete cascade",&format!("  root: roots on delete cascade\n  {}",caps[0]));
        let fixed=checked(&(edited+"\n"));
        assert!(fixed.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{}\n{:?}",d.rewrite,fixed.diagnostics);
        let price=fixed.schema.delete_total_reservation("roots").unwrap();
        let ceiling=snapback4_core::Ceilings::default();
        assert!(price[3]<=ceiling.rows_written && price[4]<=ceiling.heap_bytes,"{price:?}");
    }
}
const COERCION: &str="table parents:\n  name: text <=20\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  read <- allow\n  insert <- allow\nfn label(p: parents): p.name\nquery parentName(p: parents):\n  return parents[p] { name }\nmutation add(id: id):\n  p = insert parents { id, name: 'new' }\n  insert kids { id: 'child', parent: p }\n  return { label: label(p), key: parents[p] }\n";
#[test]
fn contextual_row_ids_preserve_helper_snapshots_and_explicit_ir() {
    let implicit=checked(COERCION);
    assert!(implicit.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",implicit.diagnostics);
    let explicit=checked(&COERCION.replace("parent: p }","parent: p.id }").replace("key: parents[p]","key: parents[p.id]"));
    assert!(explicit.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",explicit.diagnostics);
    fn erase_sites(v:&mut serde_json::Value) { match v { serde_json::Value::Object(o)=> { o.remove("site"); for v in o.values_mut(){erase_sites(v);} }, serde_json::Value::Array(a)=>for v in a{erase_sites(v);},_=>{} } }
    let programs=|b:&snapback4_lang::Bound| {let mut v=serde_json::to_value(b.programs.iter().map(|p|&p.program).collect::<Vec<_>>()).unwrap();erase_sites(&mut v);v};
    assert_eq!(programs(&implicit),programs(&explicit));
    for source in [COERCION.replace("parent: p }","parent: (parents[id]) }"),COERCION.replace("id: 'child', parent: p","id: p, parent: p"),COERCION.replace("table kids:","table strangers:\n  public\ntable kids:").replace("parent: parents on delete keep","parent: strangers on delete keep")] {
        let refused=checked(&source); assert!(!refused.diagnostics.is_empty(),"unexpectedly accepted: {source}");
    }
}

fn media_units_and_precise_type_repairs() {
    let b=checked("table pictures:\n  photo: image <=5MB\n  video: video <=2GB ?\n  public\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    assert!(checked("table pictures:\n  photo: image <=5GB\n").diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.message.contains("4,294,967,295")));
    assert!(checked("table pictures:\n  photo: image\n").diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.rewrite.contains("image <=5MB")));
    let b=checked("table values:\n  owner: principal\n  n: int 1..100 ?\n  public\nview totals: sum values of .n by .owner\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.message.contains("optional") && d.message.contains("range is accepted")),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    let b=checked("table names:\n  name: text <=10\n  public\nmutation add(name: text <=10):\n  insert names { name: lower(name) }\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.rewrite.contains("Unicode") && d.rewrite.contains("3 ×")),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
    let b=checked("query q(text: text <=20, q: text <=10):\n  return text matching q first 5 by .id\n");
    assert!(b.diagnostics.iter().any(|d|d.rewrite.contains("tokens(text)")),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);
}

use snapback4_core::{Context, Engine, Value, WriteState, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::collections::BTreeMap;
fn id(s:&str)->Value{Value::Id(s.into())}
fn text(s:&str)->Value{Value::String(s.into())}
fn args(v:&[(&str,Value)])->BTreeMap<String,Value>{v.iter().map(|(k,v)|(k.to_string(),v.clone())).collect()}
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
fn why(e:&mut Engine,op:&str,who:&str,values:&[(&str,Value)])->snapback4_core::Refusal{match e.mutate(op,None,Context::new(who,args(values)).at(100)).unwrap().state{WriteState::Failed {why,..}=>why,s=>panic!("{s:?}")}}
const UNIQUE:&str="table rows:\n  owner: principal\n  writer: principal\n  handle: text <=20\n  other: text <=20 ?\n  unique handle\n  unique other else OTHER_TAKEN 'Other key is taken'\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .writer = viewer\n  update <- .writer = viewer\nmutation add(id: id, owner: principal, writer: principal, handle: text <=20, other: text <=20 ?):\n  return insert rows {id,owner,writer,handle,other}\nmutation absent(id: id, owner: principal, writer: principal, handle: text <=20, other: text <=20 ?):\n  return insert rows {id,owner,writer,handle,other} if absent\nquery page(c: cursor ?):\n  return rows first 10 by .id after c\n";
fn seed(e:&mut Engine,id_:&str,owner:&str,handle:&str,other:Option<&str>){e.seed(vec![("rows".into(),snapback4_core::value::object([("id",id(id_)),("owner",id(owner)),("writer",id(owner)),("handle",text(handle)),("other",other.map(text).unwrap_or(Value::Null))]))]).unwrap();}
fn input(id_:&str,owner:&str,handle:&str,other:Option<&str>)->Vec<(&'static str,Value)>{vec![("id",id(id_)),("owner",id(owner)),("writer",id(owner)),("handle",text(handle)),("other",other.map(text).unwrap_or(Value::Null))]}
#[test]
fn visible_unique_names_the_declaration_and_authored_code_wins(){
 let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"old","alice","key",Some("other"));
 let visible=why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("new","alice","key",None));
 assert_eq!(visible.code,"E_UNIQUE");assert!(visible.message.contains("unique handle") && !visible.message.contains("$intent"));
 let named=why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("new","alice","different",Some("other")));assert_eq!(named.code,"OTHER_TAKEN");
 assert_eq!(why(&mut e,"add","alice",&input("old","alice","different",None)).code,"E_CONSTRAINT");
 let absent=e.mutate("absent",None,Context::new("alice",args(&input("new","alice","key",None))).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(absent.state,WriteState::Sent {result:Value::Null,..}));
 // Null secondary keys do not conflict; the handle remains independently unique.
 for (i,handle) in [("two","two"),("three","three")] {assert!(matches!(e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",args(&input(i,"alice",handle,None))).at(100)).unwrap().state,WriteState::Sent {..}));}
}
#[test]
fn hidden_unique_is_the_same_public_refusal_as_rule_denial_including_seats(){
 for viewer in ["alice","service:ops"] {
  let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"old","bob","key",None);
  let collision=why(&mut e,"add",viewer,&input("new",viewer,"key",None));
  let mut denied=input("new",viewer,"different",None);denied[2].1=id("other");
  let denial=why(&mut e,"add",viewer,&denied);
  assert_eq!(collision,denial,"{viewer}");assert_eq!(collision.code,"E_RULE");assert!(collision.message.contains("a row you cannot read holds this key"));
  if viewer.starts_with("service:"){assert!(collision.message.contains("rows rule clause: insert <- .writer = viewer"));}
  assert_eq!(e.store().rows("rows").unwrap().len(),1);
 }
}
#[test]
fn hidden_counter_is_request_local_and_changes_no_query_envelope_or_meter(){
 let mut e=engine(UNIQUE);seed(&mut e,"a","alice","a",None);seed(&mut e,"b","bob","b",None);
 let plain=e.query("page",Context::new("alice",args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100)).unwrap();
 let trace=RefusalTrace::default();
 let traced=e.query("page",Context::new("alice",args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone())).unwrap();
 assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(plain).unwrap(),serde_json::to_value(traced).unwrap());
 assert_eq!(trace.hidden(),1);
 let next=RefusalTrace::default();e.query("page",Context::new(snapback4_core::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL,args(&[("c",Value::Null)])).at(100).with_refusal_trace(next.clone())).unwrap();assert_eq!(next.hidden(),0);
}
#[test]
fn ephemeral_seat_denials_have_the_same_static_clause(){
 let mut e=engine("table typing:\n  owner: principal\n  unique owner\n  cap 50\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  ephemeral 4s\nmutation add(owner: principal):\n  return insert typing {owner}\n");
 let denied=why(&mut e,"add","service:ops",&[("owner",id("alice"))]);assert_eq!(denied.code,"E_RULE");assert!(denied.message.ends_with("typing rule clause: insert <- .owner = viewer"),"{denied:?}");
}
#[test]
fn hidden_point_images_are_counted_outside_the_query_envelope(){
 let source=format!("{UNIQUE}query point(key: rows):\n  return rows[key]\n");let mut e=engine(&source);seed(&mut e,"hidden","bob","key",None);
 let trace=RefusalTrace::default();let values=args(&[("key",id("hidden"))]);
 let plain=e.query("point",Context::new("alice",values.clone()).at(100)).unwrap();
 let traced=e.query("point",Context::new("alice",values).at(100).with_refusal_trace(trace.clone())).unwrap();assert_eq!(serde_json::to_value(plain).unwrap(),serde_json::to_value(traced).unwrap());assert_eq!(trace.hidden(),1);
}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/persona_principals.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/persona_principals.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..d7f37c0772
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/persona_principals.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,33 @@
+//! Development argument checks shared by the host and fixture runner.
+use serde_json::Value as Json;
+#[cfg(test)] use serde_json::json;
+use snapback4_core::Refusal;
+use std::collections::BTreeMap;
+pub(crate) fn arguments(personas:&[String],kinds:&BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>,args:&Json)->Option<Refusal> {
+    fn bare(kind:&snapback4_core::ir::ArgType,value:&Json,personas:&[String])->Option<String> {
+        use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
+        match kind {
+            ArgType::Principal => value.as_str().filter(|v| personas.iter().any(|p| p==v)).map(String::from),
+            ArgType::Optional(inner)=>bare(inner,value,personas),
+            ArgType::List { of:Some(inner),.. }|ArgType::ListRange { of:Some(inner),.. }=>value.as_array()?.iter().find_map(|v|bare(inner,v,personas)),
+            _=>None
+        }
+    }
+    let fields=args.as_object()?;
+    kinds.iter().find_map(|(name,kind)|bare(kind,fields.get(name)?,personas).map(|p|Refusal::new("E_INPUT","input",format!("argument {name}: '{p}' is a persona name, not a principal; write 'dev:{p}' (or use the authenticated account principal in production)")).guide("personas")))
+}
+#[cfg(test)]
+mod qdiag_tests {
+ use super::*;
+ use snapback4_core::ir::ArgType;
+ #[test] fn declared_persona_principals_are_checked_without_rewriting_identity(){
+  let personas=vec!["alice".into()];let kinds=BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Principal)]);
+  let value=json!({"p":"alice"});
+  let why=arguments(&personas,&kinds,&value).unwrap();assert_eq!(why.code,"E_INPUT");assert!(why.message.contains("dev:alice"));assert_eq!(value,json!({"p":"alice"}));
+  for p in ["dev:alice","aliceOther","user:alice","service:alice"] {assert!(arguments(&personas,&kinds,&json!({"p":p})).is_none());}
+  assert!(arguments(&[],&kinds,&value).is_none());
+  assert!(arguments(&personas,&BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Id)]),&value).is_none());
+  let lists=BTreeMap::from([("p".into(),ArgType::Optional(Box::new(ArgType::List {of:Some(Box::new(ArgType::Principal)),max:5})))]);
+  assert!(arguments(&personas,&lists,&json!({"p":["dev:alice","alice"]})).is_some());
+ }
+}

             if let Json::Object(fields) = &args_json {
                 for (name, value) in fields {

 }
 
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — persona names are not their principals; dev only.
+pub(super) fn persona_argument(shared: &Shared, kinds: &BTreeMap<String,snapback4_core::ir::ArgType>, args: &Json) -> Option<Refusal> {
+    if !shared.personas { return None; }
+    let bound = lock(&shared.bound);
+    crate::persona_principals::arguments(&bound.personas,kinds,args)
+}

             let dependent = if dependents.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!("; {} depend on it", dependents.join(", ")) };
             notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&site(root), "N_NO_PRODUCTION_WRITER",
                 format!("{root}: no production writer can bootstrap this root{cycle}{dependent}; fixtures and /seed are dev-only"),

 }
+fn bootstrap_recipe(bound: &Bound, table: &str) -> String {
+    let writers: Vec<_> = bound.programs.iter().filter(|p| ir::write_tables(&p.program).iter().any(|(target,effect)| target==table && matches!(*effect,"insert"|"upsert"))).map(|p|p.program.name.clone()).collect();
+    let missing = if writers.is_empty() { format!("missing mutation path: no mutation inserts {table}") }
+        else { format!("missing service-seat bootstrap path through {} (their {table} insert rule depends on rows not yet provisioned)",writers.join(", ")) };
+    format!("{missing}; add a bootstrap{table} mutation that requires `viewer = 'service:ops'`, allow that branch in {table}'s insert rule, and make its required probes readable to the seat. Mint `snapback4 token --service ops --release ./deployment`, then call the mutation with the real account principal and its bearer token. For synced rosters use the independent seat-or-member branch; snapback4 guide deploy --section first admin")
+}

//! Shared type-directed positional point selection for programs and rules.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — identity wins; otherwise ordered compatible declared keys only.
use super::catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo};
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, diag::Diagnostic, types::Ty};

pub(super) fn candidates<'a>(info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, types: &[(Ty, bool)], synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>) -> Vec<&'a IndexDecl> {
    let identity = read.index.is_none() && types.len() == 1 &&
        (types[0].0.required() == Ty::Id || types[0].0.required() == Ty::Ref(read.table.clone())
            || (!types[0].0.is_optional() && matches!(types[0].0.required(),Ty::Row(t)|Ty::WriteRow(t) if t==read.table)));
    info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
        .filter(|index| match &read.index {
            Some(name) => &index.name == name,
            None if identity => index.name == "byId",
            None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == types.len(),
        })
        // Explicit indexes keep their existing mismatch diagnostics and range semantics.
        .filter(|index| read.index.is_some() || types.iter().zip(&index.columns).all(|((ty, literal), column)| {
            let expected = if index.computed.contains_key(column) { Ty::Int }
                else if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(read.table.clone()) }
                else { info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown) };
            ty.comparable(&expected) || (!ty.is_optional() && matches!((ty.required(),expected.required()),(Ty::Row(a)|Ty::WriteRow(a),Ty::Ref(b)) if a==b)) || (*literal && expected.is_identity())
        })).collect()
}

pub(super) fn refusal(info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, candidates: &[&IndexDecl], catalog: &Catalog) -> Diagnostic {
    let alternatives = if candidates.is_empty() && read.index.is_some() {
        info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| !catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()))).collect::<Vec<_>>()
    } else { candidates.to_vec() };
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#15.7 — `budgets['general']` (lane 020): a text value names the declared
    // text key far more often than a row id, so that named form leads (and is --fix's choice).
    let mut alternatives = alternatives;
    alternatives.sort_by_key(|index| index.columns.iter().any(|column| column == "id"));
    let mut changes = Vec::new();
    for index in &alternatives {
        // Same-column opposite-order indexes need the explicit name as well.
        let duplicate = info.indexes.iter().any(|other| other.name != index.name && other.unique
            && other.columns.len() == index.columns.len() && other.columns.iter().all(|c| index.columns.contains(c)));
        if read.index.is_none() { changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::NamedKey { columns: index.columns.clone(), index: duplicate.then(|| index.name.clone()) }); }
        // Unknown explicit indexes and computed keys retain verified legacy alternatives.
        changes.push(crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(index.name.clone()));
    }
    let message = if alternatives.is_empty() {
        format!("{} has no declared unique key accepting these positional argument types in order; name the key columns, or use where for a population", read.table)
    } else {
        format!("{} positional argument types leave {} candidate keys; name the intended key columns or choose a compatible unique index explicitly", read.table, candidates.len())
    };
    let diagnostic = Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", message, "").guide("reads").repair_read(&read.site, changes);
    // The user explicitly opts into one context-verified choice with --fix.
    if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 { diagnostic.mechanical() } else { diagnostic }
}
//! Bind key operands once, retaining authored sequencing and contextual literals.
use super::*;

impl<'a> Binder<'a> {
    pub(super) fn bind_key_parts(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, end: usize,
        keys: &mut Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        for (position, part) in read.keys.iter().enumerate().take(end).skip(keys.len()) {
            let mut setup = setup::Body::default();
            let expected = part.column.as_deref().or_else(|| read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)).and_then(|i| i.columns.get(position)).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column));
            let (e, t) = match expected {
                Some(expected) => self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup, &expected)?,
                None => self.expr(&part.value, &mut setup)?,
            };
            self.sequence_setup(keys.iter_mut().map(|(_, e, _)| e), setup, out);
            let e = if expr::allocates(&e) { self.snapshot(e, out) } else { e };
            keys.push((part.column.clone(), e, t));
        }
        if let Some(index) = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|i| &i.name == name)) {
            for (position,(column,value,ty)) in keys.iter_mut().enumerate() {
                if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().or_else(|| index.columns.get(position).map(String::as_str)).and_then(|c| key_identity(info,c)) {
                    (*value,*ty) = self.row_identity(value.clone(),ty.clone(),&expected);
                }
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    // Mixed-key normalization names an already-bound positional prefix. Only
    // a literal acquires new context; compound expressions keep their IR/setup.
    pub(super) fn name_bound_keys(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr,
        keys: &mut [(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)]) -> Fallible<()> {
        for ((column, expr, ty), part) in keys.iter_mut().zip(&read.keys) {
            *column = part.column.clone();
            if let Some(expected) = column.as_deref().and_then(|column| key_identity(info, column))
                && matches!(&part.value, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(_), .. }) {
                (*expr, *ty) = self.expr_as_reference(&part.value, &mut setup::Body::default(), &expected)?;
            }
        }
        Ok(())
    }
}

fn key_identity(info: &TableInfo, column: &str) -> Option<Ty> {
    let ty = if column == "id" { Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone()) }
        else { Ty::from_scalar(info.columns.get(column)?) };
    ty.is_identity().then_some(ty)
}
