    }

    // ----- reads -----

    fn read(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if read.matching.is_some() && self.binding(&read.table).is_some_and(|b| matches!(b.ty.required(), Ty::Text(_) | Ty::Enum(_))) {
            return Err(type_error(&read.site, format!("`{}` is text; `matching` searches a table's word index", read.table),
                format!("for text compare `{0} = q` or `{0} != q`; for whole-token membership use `q in tokens({0})`; for word-prefix row search write `<table> matching q first 50 by .id`", read.table)).guide("types"));
        }
        if let Some(timeline) = self.schema.timelines.iter().find(|timeline| timeline.name == read.table) {
            let expanded = if let Some(plan) = self.catalog.feed_views.get(&read.table) {
                super::feed_view::read(timeline, plan, read)?
            } else { super::timeline::read(timeline, read)? };
            // @ref LLP 3000.013#14.4 — the scale certificate trusts only the scan this expansion generates.
            let outer = self.expanding_feed.replace((timeline.table("Feed"), read.site.clone()));
            let bound = self.expr(&expanded, out);
            self.expanding_feed = outer;
            return bound;
        }
        if let Some(shaped) = self.page_shape(read) { return self.expr(&shaped, out); }
        // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — a bound row of this table addresses itself, as in `update t[row]`.
        if let Some(by_id) = self.row_as_key(read) { return self.read(&by_id, out); }
        if self.is_page(read, ReadMode::FilteredPoint)? {
            let (scan, take) = self.scan(read, out)?;
            return Ok((Expr::Scan(scan), Ty::Page(read.table.clone(), take)));
        }
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        if let Some(maintain) = &info.maintained {
            if read.keys.len() != maintain.by.len() || read.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.as_ref()).any(|column| !maintain.by.contains(column)) {
                return Err(super::maintains::refusal(&read.site, format!("{} scalar lookup needs the complete group key: {}", read.table, maintain.by.join(", ")), format!("supply every group key, or page the view: {} first 20 by .id after c", read.table)));
            }
        }
        let value = self.read_value(read, ReadMode::FilteredPoint, out)?;
        if let Some(maintain) = &info.maintained {
            // A maintained point read is the typed value: zero when absent.
            let row = self.temp("countRow");
            let tmp = self.temp("total");
            let zero = Expr::Literal(Value::Int(0));
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: row.clone(), value });
            out.push(Stmt::If {
                condition: Expr::Binary { op: BinaryOp::Eq, left: Box::new(ir::var(&row)), right: Box::new(Expr::Literal(Value::Null)) },
                then_body: vec![Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: zero }],
                else_body: vec![Stmt::Let { name: tmp.clone(), value: ir::field(ir::var(&row), "value") }],
            });
            let ty = info.columns.get("value").map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Int);
            let ty = self.maintained_result_type(maintain, read, ty);
            return Ok((ir::var(&tmp), ty));
        }
        Ok((value, Ty::Row(read.table.clone()).optional()))
    }

    fn scan(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Scan, u32)> {
        self.scan_at(read, out, prefix::Position::Page)
    }

    fn scan_at(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, out: &mut setup::Body, position: prefix::Position) -> Fallible<(Scan, u32)> {
        let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;

        if let Some(m) = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table).filter(|m| m.groups_by_parent(self.schema)) {
            let parent = self.schema.tables[&m.source].through.as_ref().unwrap();

For a scalar supply every group key: {}[{}]. Omit an explicit aggregate read rule to inherit parent authorization", parent.parent, read.table, parent.reference, read.table, parent.reference, read.table, m.by.join(", "))).guide("maintains"));
            }
        }

        if info.maintained.is_some() && let Some(position) = index.columns.iter().position(|field| field == "value") {

            }

            return Err(type_error(&range.site, "a page holds at least one row", print::read_expr(&ReadExpr { range: Some(ast::RangeSpec { take: 50, ..range.clone() }), ..read.clone() })));
        }
        let mut page_source = read.clone();
        page_source.computed.extend(index.computed.iter().map(|(key, value)| {
            let mut value = value.clone();
            if let A::Call { args, .. } = &mut value
                && let Some(A::Name { name, site }) = args.first().cloned() {
                args[0] = A::Member { base: Box::new(A::Focal { site: site.clone() }), name, site };
            }
            (key.clone(), value)
        }));

            key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone());
        }

        let (read, filter) = self.scan_filter(read, &index)?;

        // Key expressions precede the seek bounds and cursor, including their setup.
        let prefix: Vec<Expr> = prefix.into_iter().map(|value| self.snapshot(value, out)).collect();
        let seek = self.seek(info, read, &index, out)?;

        if let Some(cursor) = &range.cursor {
            let (e, t) = self.expr(cursor, out)?;
            if !matches!(t.required(), Ty::Cursor) {
                return Err(type_error(cursor.site(), format!("after expects a cursor, got {}", t.name()), "declare c: cursor ? and pass after c"));
            }
            if !info.total_cursor(&index) {
                let columns = index.columns.iter().map(|column| index.computed.get(column)
                    .map(print::expression).unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone())).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                if index.unique {
                    // Do not add id to the unique constraint: that would
                    // weaken it. Plan a separate pageable index from the read.

                        planned.index = None;
                        for (key, column) in planned.keys.iter_mut().zip(&index.columns) { key.column.get_or_insert_with(|| column.clone()); }
                        planned.order_keys = index.columns.iter().skip(prefix.len()).cloned().collect();
                        print::read_expr(&planned)
                    } else {
                        let mut name = format!("{}Page", index.name);
                        while info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.name == name) { name.push('_'); }
                        planned.index = Some(name.clone());
                };
                let ir_op = match op {
                    "+" => BinaryOp::Add,
                    "-" => BinaryOp::Sub,
                    _ => BinaryOp::Mul,
                };
                return Ok((Expr::Binary { op: ir_op, left: Box::new(l), right: Box::new(r) }, result));
            }
            other => return Err(unknown(site, format!("unknown operator {other}"), "use =, !=, <, <=, >, >=, +, -, *, and, or, ??")),
        };
        let mut present = Vec::new();
        if matches!(ir_op, BinaryOp::Lt | BinaryOp::Lte | BinaryOp::Gt | BinaryOp::Gte) {
            if nullable_left { present.push(l.clone()); }
            if nullable_right { present.push(r.clone()); }

    /// An expression in an identity position: a text literal becomes an id.
    pub(super) fn expr_as_identity(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if let A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(text), .. } = expr {
            if text.is_empty() {
                return Err(type_error(expr.site(), "an empty text literal cannot be an id", "use a non-empty id literal, or null"));
            }
            return Ok((Expr::Literal(Value::Id(text.clone())), expected.required()));
        }
        self.expr(expr, out)
    }

    pub(super) fn expr_as_reference(&mut self, expr: &A, out: &mut setup::Body, expected: &Ty) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        let (value, ty) = self.expr_as_identity(expr, out, expected)?;
        Ok(self.row_identity(value, ty, expected))
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — contextual projection is exactly `.id`,
    // without a lookup; optional and cross-table rows still need an edit.
    pub(super) fn row_identity(&self, value: Expr, ty: Ty, expected: &Ty) -> (Expr, Ty) {
        if !ty.is_optional() && matches!(ty.required(), Ty::Row(_) | Ty::WriteRow(_)) {
            let table = match ty.required() { Ty::Row(t) | Ty::WriteRow(t) => t, _ => unreachable!() };
            if expected.required() == Ty::Ref(table.clone()) {
                return (ir::field(value, "id"), Ty::Ref(table));
            }
        }
        (value, ty)
    }

    pub(super) fn call(&mut self, name: &str, args: &[A], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Expr, Ty)> {
        if let Some(result) = self.job_query_call(name, args, site, out) { return result; }
        // Compiler-only references bypass the function catalog entirely.
        let primitive = name.strip_prefix("$builtin:");
        if primitive.is_none() && let Some(function) = self.catalog.functions.get(name).cloned() {
            return self.fn_call(&function, args, site, out);
        }
//! Referential actions (LLP 3000.039): each reference between ordinary durable
//! tables says what deleting its target does — `on delete restrict` (the
//! default), `cascade`, `set null` or `keep`. This pass synthesizes the index
//! a reference is probed through, lowers the action into the schema, refuses
//! what a column cannot carry, checks that a delete's cascade fits its
//! program, and names every delete whose behavior the restrict default changed.
// @ref LLP 3000.039#2 — restrict is the default; cascade and set null need a cap on the reference.
use super::catalog::Catalog;
use super::intent_walk as walk;
use crate::{ast::*, diag::Diagnostic};
use snapback4_core::schema::{self as core, references::{self, OnDelete as Action}};
use std::collections::{BTreeMap, BTreeSet};

fn action(declared: OnDelete) -> Action {
    match declared { OnDelete::Restrict => Action::Restrict, OnDelete::Cascade => Action::Cascade, OnDelete::SetNull => Action::SetNull, OnDelete::Keep => Action::Keep }
}

/// Before the schema is bound: every reference whose action probes its
/// children gets an index leading with the reference (then the child's
/// `expire at` column), unless one already exists. A `cap N by <column>` is
/// served by the same index.
pub(super) fn indexes(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    let ordinary = |catalog: &Catalog, name: &str| catalog.tables.get(name).is_some_and(|info| info.maintained.is_none() && info.decl.ephemeral.is_none());
    let mut wanted = Vec::new();
    for (name, info) in &catalog.tables {
        if !ordinary(catalog, name) { continue; }
        for column in &info.decl.columns {
            let TypeKind::Ref { table: parent } = &column.type_.kind else { continue };
            if column.on_delete.as_ref().is_some_and(|(a, _)| *a == OnDelete::Keep) || column.written_by.is_some() || !ordinary(catalog, parent) { continue; }
            if info.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|through| through.reference == column.name) { continue; }
            // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.3 — the effective deadline: a read-through child's `$deadline`, else `expire at`.
            let mut leading = vec![column.name.clone()];
            if info.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.expiring) { leading.push(snapback4_core::schema::through::DEADLINE.to_owned()); }
            else if let Some((expire, _)) = &info.decl.expire { leading.push(expire.clone()); }
            if info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.is_plain() && index.columns.starts_with(&leading)) { continue; }
            leading.push("id".into());
            wanted.push((name.clone(), IndexDecl { name: references::reference_index_name(&column.name), columns: leading, unique: false, refusal: None, words: None, computed: BTreeMap::new(), site: column.site.clone() }));
        }
    }
    for (table, index) in wanted {
        catalog.synthesized.insert((table.clone(), index.name.clone()));
        catalog.tables.get_mut(&table).expect("catalog table").indexes.push(index);
    }
}

/// After receipts and timelines are bound (they decide which tables are
/// engine bookkeeping): lower each action, refusing what a column cannot carry.
pub(super) fn bind(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &mut core::Schema, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
    let mut lowered: Vec<(String, String, Action)> = Vec::new();
    let mut unbounded = BTreeMap::new();
    for (name, info) in &catalog.tables {
        if info.maintained.is_some() { continue; }
        for column in &info.decl.columns {
            let declared = column.on_delete.as_ref();
            let site = declared.map_or(&column.site, |(_, site)| site);
            let refuse = |diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>, message: String, rewrite: String| diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", message, rewrite).guide("writes"));
            let TypeKind::Ref { table: parent } = &column.type_.kind else {
                if declared.is_some() { refuse(diagnostics, format!("{name}.{} is not a reference; `on delete` belongs on a column whose type names a table", column.name), format!("{}: <table> on delete …, or remove `on delete`", column.name)); }
                continue;
            };
            if !schema.tables.contains_key(parent) || !schema.tables.contains_key(name) { continue; }
            // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.1 — a field-owned reference is private to its owner: probing it would disclose it.
            if column.written_by.is_some() {
                if declared.is_some() {
                    refuse(diagnostics, format!("{name}.{} is written by its field owner, so it carries no delete action: probing it would tell the parent's deleter about a private value", column.name), "remove `on delete`".into());
                }
                continue;
            }
            if !schema.actionable(name) || !schema.actionable(parent) {
                if declared.is_some() {
                    refuse(diagnostics, format!("{name}.{} -> {parent} carries no delete action: ephemeral tables, engine-maintained tables and receipt or timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes, so the row stays when {parent} is deleted", column.name), "remove `on delete`".into());
                }
                continue;
            }
            let chosen = declared.map_or(Action::Restrict, |(a, _)| action(*a));
            let table = &schema.tables[name];
            let through = table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column.name);
            if chosen.writes() && let Some(reason) = references::write_target_refusal(schema, name) {
                refuse(diagnostics, format!("{} on {name}.{}: {reason}; another row's delete may not delete or rewrite its rows", chosen.words(), column.name), if reason.contains("maintained sum") { format!("{}: {parent} on delete restrict   -- or keep. For a maintained sum source, unwind it with ordinary deletes in a system job: `job unwind():\n  for row in {name}:\n    delete {name}[row.id]`; each delete updates the sum. Mark the parent for cleanup, filter the job to those parents, and delete each parent only after its children are gone (guide writes)", column.name) } else { format!("{}: {parent} on delete restrict   -- or keep", column.name) });
                continue;
            }
            if chosen == Action::Keep && through {
                refuse(diagnostics, format!("on delete keep on read-through {name}.{}: a read-through child lives within its parent (LLP 3000.014 §3), so it cannot outlive it", column.name), format!("{}: {parent} on delete cascade, or leave it undeclared (restrict)", column.name));
                continue;
            }
            if chosen == Action::SetNull && let Some(reason) = references::set_null_refusal(schema, name, table, &column.name) {
                refuse(diagnostics, format!("on delete set null on {name}.{}: {reason}", column.name), format!("{}: {parent} on delete cascade, or {}: {parent} on delete restrict", column.name, column.name));
                continue;
            }
            if chosen == Action::Cascade && through && table.is_expiring() {
                refuse(diagnostics, format!("on delete cascade on read-through {name}.{} needs a child that never expires: an expired, unswept child still holds its parent (LLP 3000.014 §3.3)", column.name),
                    format!("keep `{}: {parent}` (restrict); delete live {name} rows, let the sweep take expired ones, then delete the {parent} row", column.name));
                continue;
            }
            if chosen.writes() && schema.reference_cap(name, &column.name).is_none() {
                unbounded.entry(parent.clone()).or_insert_with(|| site.clone());
            }
            // `keep` is the legacy meaning, so the schema spells it by absence: a
            // schema whose references all keep stays byte-identical and capability-free.
            if chosen != Action::Keep { lowered.push((name.clone(), column.name.clone(), chosen)); }
        }
    }
    for (name, column, chosen) in lowered {
        schema.tables.get_mut(&name).expect("bound table").on_delete.insert(column, chosen);
    }
    // Event prices depend on the actions just lowered and must precede bound checks.
    schema.prepare_write_reservations();
    // Diagnose an authored root's whole cascade once, including missing bounds
    // below an already-capped edge. Avoid contradictory parent/child recipes.
    let mut authored=BTreeSet::new();
    for doc in &catalog.documents {for program in &doc.programs {
        let mut body=program.prelude.clone();body.extend(program.body.clone());
        walk::statements(&mut body,&mut |expr| if let Expr::Write {op:WriteOp::Delete,table,..}=expr && schema.tables.contains_key(table) {authored.insert(table.clone());});
    }}
    let mut roots=BTreeMap::new();
    for (parent,site) in &unbounded {
        let found=authored.iter().filter(|root|super::cascade_repair::reaches(schema,root,parent)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
        if found.is_empty() {roots.insert(parent.clone(),site.clone());}
        else {for root in found {roots.entry(root.clone()).or_insert_with(||site.clone());}}
    }
    let names=roots.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
    roots.retain(|root,_|!names.iter().any(|other|other!=root && super::cascade_repair::reaches(schema,other,root) && !super::cascade_repair::reaches(schema,root,other)));
    for (parent,site) in roots {
        let missing=schema.tables.keys().filter(|table|super::cascade_repair::reaches(schema,&parent,table)).flat_map(|table|schema.referencing(table))
            .filter(|r|r.action.writes() && schema.reference_cap(r.child,r.column).is_none())
            .map(|r|format!("{}.{} needs `cap N by {}` or plain single-column `unique {}`",r.child,r.column,r.column,r.column)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join("; ");
        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site,"E_CASCADE_BOUND",format!("delete {parent}: {missing}; all children share one reservation"),super::cascade_repair::recipe(schema,&parent,false)).guide("writes"));
    }
    // A cascade must end: no table reaches itself through cascades.
    let mut reported = BTreeSet::new();
    for name in schema.tables.keys().cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>() {
        if !unbounded.is_empty() || schema.delete_reservation(&name).is_some() || !schema.is_referenced(&name) { continue; }
        let Some(r) = schema.referencing(&name).into_iter().find(|r| r.action == Action::Cascade) else { continue };
        if !reported.insert((r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned())) { continue; }
        let site = catalog.tables.get(r.child).and_then(|info| info.decl.columns.iter().find(|c| c.name == r.column)).map(|c| c.on_delete.as_ref().map_or(&c.site, |(_, s)| s).clone()).unwrap_or_default();
        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", format!("on delete cascade from {name} reaches {name} again: a cascade must end, so the cascading references cannot form a cycle"),
            format!("{}: {name} on delete restrict   -- or set null on an optional reference", r.column)).guide("writes"));
    }
    if let Some(table) = schema.deletion_cycle() && !diagnostics.iter().any(|d| d.code == "E_REFERENCE_ACTION") {
        let site = catalog.tables.get(&table).map(|info| info.decl.site.clone()).unwrap_or_default();
        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", format!("{table} reaches itself through `on delete cascade` and `read through`: a cascade deletes children before parents, and a read-through child must go before its parent, so no order can delete those rows"),
            "make one of the cascading references `on delete restrict`").guide("writes"));
    }
    for issue in references::validate(schema) {
        let site = catalog.site_for(&issue.path);
        if diagnostics.iter().any(|d| matches!(d.code.as_str(), "E_REFERENCE_ACTION" | "E_CASCADE_BOUND")) { break; }
        diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_REFERENCE_ACTION", issue.message, "declare the reference's action the line names (guide writes)").guide("writes"));
    }
}

/// Every delete site: a cascade whose reservation cannot fit its program is
/// `E_CASCADE_BOUND`; an undeclared reference that can now refuse the delete
/// is `N_DELETE_RESTRICTED` (it replaces the 0.4 orphans note).
// @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — the note fires exactly where the restrict default changed a delete's behavior.
pub(super) fn sites(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &core::Schema, diagnostics: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) -> Vec<Diagnostic> {
    // parent -> the undeclared references that now refuse its delete.
    let mut undeclared: BTreeMap<&str, Vec<(&str, &str)>> = BTreeMap::new();
    for (name, info) in &catalog.tables {
        for column in info.decl.columns.iter().filter(|c| c.on_delete.is_none()) {
            let TypeKind::Ref { table: parent } = &column.type_.kind else { continue };
            // A read-through child refused its parent's delete before (E_PARENT_HAS_CHILDREN).
            if info.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column.name) { continue; }
            if schema.tables.get(name).and_then(|t| t.on_delete.get(&column.name)) == Some(&Action::Restrict) {
                undeclared.entry(parent.as_str()).or_default().push((name.as_str(), column.name.as_str()));
            }
        }
    }
    // Guards that prove no child exists (an unfiltered count required zero,
    // a count-like sum, a status every child insert flips away from): the
    // default cannot change that delete.
    let guards = super::delete_guards::Guards::new(catalog);
    let mut notes = Vec::new();
    for doc in &catalog.documents {
        for program in doc.programs.iter().filter(|p| !matches!(p.kind, ProgramKind::Query) && !p.native) {
            let original = program;
            let program = program.clone();
            // Each delete with the (child, reference) pairs a dominating zero-count
            // guard proves childless, and every sum view the program reads.
            let (deletes, summed) = guarded_deletes(&program, &guards);
            let word = if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job { "job" } else { "mutation" };
            let mut noted: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
            let mut priced: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
            let mut read_noted: BTreeSet<String> = BTreeSet::new();
            for (parent, site, counted) in deletes {
                if noted.contains(&parent) { continue; }
                if priced.insert(parent.clone()) && let Some(price) = schema.delete_reservation(&parent) && let Some(over) = over_ceiling(&price, program.kind == ProgramKind::Job) {
                    let chain = schema.referencing(&parent).into_iter().filter(|r| r.action.writes())
                        .map(|r| format!("{}.{} ({}, cap {})", r.child, r.column, r.action.words(), schema.reference_cap(r.child, r.column).unwrap_or(0))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                    diagnostics.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "E_CASCADE_BOUND",
                        format!("{word} {}: `delete {parent}[…]` reserves {over} for what it deletes or clears through {chain}, whether or not those rows exist, so the {word} can never commit", program.name),
                        super::cascade_repair::recipe(schema, &parent, program.kind == ProgramKind::Job)).guide("writes"));
                }
                // The delete reaches every table its cascades reach; their undeclared
                // references refuse it too.
                let mut reached = vec![parent.clone()];
                let mut at = 0;
                while at < reached.len() {
                    let next: Vec<String> = schema.referencing(&reached[at]).into_iter().filter(|r| r.action == Action::Cascade).map(|r| r.child.to_owned()).collect();
                    for table in next { if !reached.contains(&table) { reached.push(table); } }
                    at += 1;
                }
                let kept: Vec<(&str, &str, &str)> = reached.iter().flat_map(|table| undeclared.get(table.as_str()).into_iter().flatten().map(move |(c, col)| (table.as_str(), *c, *col)))
                    .filter(|(target, c, col)| !(*target == parent.as_str() && (counted.contains(&((*c).to_owned(), (*col).to_owned())) || guards.status_guarded(original, &site, &parent, c, col)))).collect();
                // @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — the read prerequisite is the other behavior change: name it where a deleter may not be a reader.
                if program.kind != ProgramKind::Job && read_noted.insert(parent.clone()) && schema.is_referenced(&parent) && !deleters_read(schema, &parent) && !schema.children(&parent).any(|_| true) {
                    notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "N_DELETE_NEEDS_READ",
                        format!("mutation {}: `delete {parent}[…]` now also needs read authority on the row: other rows reference {parent} ({}), so a caller the delete rule admits but the read rule does not gets NOT_FOUND", program.name,
                            schema.referencing(&parent).into_iter().map(|r| format!("{}.{}", r.child, r.column)).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")),
                        format!("make every {parent} deleter a reader (`delete <- <the read rule> and …`), or mark the reference `on delete keep` if deleting {parent} should not consult those rows")).guide("writes"));
                }
                let Some(&(target, child, column)) = kept.first() else { continue };
                noted.insert(parent.clone());
                let named = kept.iter().map(|(_, child, column)| format!("{child}.{column}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                let through = if target == parent { String::new() } else { format!(" (through its cascade to {target})") };
                let sum = summed.iter().find(|(_, c, by, _)| target == parent && c == child && by == column).map(|(view, _, _, summed)| guards.sum_reason(view, child, column, summed)).unwrap_or_default();
                notes.push(Diagnostic::new(&site, "N_DELETE_RESTRICTED",
                    format!("{word} {}: `delete {parent}[…]`{through} now refuses E_REFERENCED while a live row references it ({named}): an undeclared reference is `on delete restrict`; before 0.4.24 the referencing rows stayed and named a deleted row{sum}", program.name),
                    format!("declare what deleting a {target} row does to each: `{column}: {target} on delete cascade` deletes the {child} rows (with `cap N by {column}`), `{column}: {target} ? on delete set null` clears an optional reference, `on delete keep` keeps them as before, and `on delete restrict` preserves intended audit history and keeps this refusal; any declaration silences this note. Anyone whose insert rule admits a {child} row naming a {target} can hold it under restrict")).guide("writes"));
            }
        }
    }
    notes
}

/// The meter a reservation exceeds for a mutation (or a job), if any.
pub(super) fn over_ceiling(price: &references::Price, job: bool) -> Option<String> {
    let c = if job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() };
    let limits = [(price[3], c.rows_written, "rows written"), (price[2], c.examined, "rows examined"), (price[1], c.index_entries, "index entries"), (price[0], c.steps, "steps"), (price[4], c.heap_bytes, "heap bytes"), (price[5], c.output_bytes, "output bytes"), (price[6], c.rule_probes, "rule probes")];
    limits.iter().find(|(used, limit, _)| used > limit).map(|(used, limit, kind)| format!("{used} {kind} (a {} may use {limit})", if job { "job chunk" } else { "mutation" }))
}

/// Whether every principal the delete rule admits can read the row, by a
/// structural sufficient implication proof. This is deliberately not a rule
/// satisfiability solver: an unproved application invariant keeps the note.
fn deleters_read(schema: &core::Schema, table: &str) -> bool {
    let Some(t) = schema.table(table) else { return true };
    fn required_bool_fact(table: &core::Table, rule: &core::RuleExpr) -> Option<(core::RuleTerm, bool)> {
        let comparison = |left: &core::RuleTerm, right: &core::RuleTerm, equal: bool| {
            let (term, core::RuleTerm::Literal(snapback4_core::Value::Bool(value))) = (match (left, right) {
                (term, literal @ core::RuleTerm::Literal(_)) | (literal @ core::RuleTerm::Literal(_), term) => (term, literal),
                _ => return None,
            }) else { return None };
            let core::RuleTerm::Field(field) = term else { return None };
            matches!(table.columns.get(field), Some(core::ColumnType::Bool)).then(|| (term.clone(), if equal { *value } else { !*value }))
        };
        match rule {
            core::RuleExpr::Eq(left, right) => comparison(left, right, true),
            core::RuleExpr::Ne(left, right) => comparison(left, right, false),
            core::RuleExpr::Not(inner) => required_bool_fact(table, inner).map(|(term, value)| (term, !value)),
            _ => None,
        }
    }
    fn implies(table: &core::Table, stronger: &core::RuleExpr, weaker: &core::RuleExpr) -> bool {
        if stronger == weaker || required_bool_fact(table, stronger).is_some_and(|fact| Some(fact) == required_bool_fact(table, weaker))
            || weaker.is_uniformly_readable() || matches!(stronger, core::RuleExpr::Deny) { return true; }
        match (stronger, weaker) {
            (_, core::RuleExpr::And(items)) => items.iter().all(|item| implies(table, stronger, item)),
            (core::RuleExpr::Or(items), _) => items.iter().all(|item| implies(table, item, weaker)),
            (core::RuleExpr::And(items), _) => items.iter().any(|item| implies(table, item, weaker)),
            (_, core::RuleExpr::Or(items)) => items.iter().any(|item| implies(table, stronger, item)),
            _ => false,
        }
    }
    implies(t, &t.rules.delete, &t.rules.read)
}

/// Every delete in a program, in order, each with the (child, reference)
/// pairs a guard proves have no row naming the deleted key: a top-level
/// `require <count>[<the deleted key>] = 0` earlier in the program, over an
/// unfiltered count (or count-like sum) of that child by that reference, with
/// no write of the child between it and the delete. A read of a count that
/// does not dominate the delete, names another key or is not required to be
/// zero proves nothing. Also every non-count-like sum view the program reads
/// (the note explains why it does not count).
// @ref LLP 3000.039#7 — only a dominating zero requirement on the deleted key proves the restrict cannot refuse.
fn guarded_deletes(program: &ProgramDecl, guards: &super::delete_guards::Guards) -> (Vec<(String, crate::diag::Site, BTreeSet<(String, String)>)>, Vec<(String, String, String, String)>) {
    let statements: Vec<Stmt> = program.prelude.iter().chain(&program.body).cloned().collect();
    let zero = |e: &Expr| matches!(e, Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Number(n), .. } if n.parse::<f64>().is_ok_and(|n| n == 0.0));
    let count_read = |e: &Expr| match e {
        Expr::Read(read) if read.keys.len() == 1 && read.filter.is_none() && read.range.is_none()
            && matches!(&read.keys[0].value, Expr::Name { name, .. } if name != "new") =>
            guards.counts.get(&read.table).map(|pair| (pair.clone(), crate::print::expression(&read.keys[0].value))),
        _ => None,
    };
    // (statement index, (child, reference), key text) for each zero requirement.
    let mut requires: Vec<(usize, (String, String), String)> = Vec::new();
    // Tables each statement writes other than by delete.
    let mut writes: Vec<BTreeSet<String>> = Vec::new();
    let mut uncertain = Vec::new();
    let mut deletes = Vec::new();
    let mut summed: Vec<(String, String, String, String)> = Vec::new();
    for (index, statement) in statements.iter().enumerate() {
        if let Stmt::Require { condition: Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. }, .. } = statement && (op == "=" || op == "==") {
            let found = if zero(right) { count_read(left) } else if zero(left) { count_read(right) } else { None };
            if let Some((pair, key)) = found { requires.push((index, pair, key)); }
        }
        let mut body = vec![statement.clone()];
        let mut written = BTreeSet::new();
        let mut opaque = matches!(statement, Stmt::Bind { .. } | Stmt::If { .. } | Stmt::For { .. } | Stmt::As { .. });
        let mut found: Vec<(String, crate::diag::Site, Option<String>)> = Vec::new();
        walk::statements(&mut body, &mut |value: &mut Expr| match value {
            Expr::Write { op: WriteOp::Delete, table, keys, site, .. } =>
                found.push((table.clone(), site.clone(), (keys.len() == 1).then(|| crate::print::expression(&keys[0].value)))),
            Expr::Write { table, .. } => { written.insert(table.clone()); }
            Expr::Call { .. } => { opaque = true; }
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } => {
                if let Some((child, by, column)) = guards.sums.get(&read.table) && !summed.iter().any(|(view, ..)| view == &read.table) {
                    summed.push((read.table.clone(), child.clone(), by.clone(), column.clone()));
                }
            }
            _ => {}
        });
        writes.push(written);
        uncertain.push(opaque);
        for (table, site, key) in found {
            let proven = requires.iter().filter(|(at, (child, _), required)| *at < index && key.as_deref() == Some(required.as_str())
                && !uncertain[at + 1..=index].iter().any(|opaque| *opaque)
                && !writes[at + 1..=index].iter().any(|w| w.contains(child)))
                .map(|(_, pair, _)| pair.clone()).collect();
            deletes.push((table, site, proven));
        }
    }
    (deletes, summed)
}

mod common;
use common::*;
use snapback4_core::{Context, WriteState};
use snapback4_device::{device::Device,store::DeviceStore};
use serde_json::json;
const SOURCE:&str="table parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  unique label\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  unique parent\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nfn label(p: parents): p.label\nmutation add(id: id, child: id, title: text <=20):\n  p = insert parents { id, label: title }\n  insert kids { id: child, parent: p }\n  return label(p)\nmutation clash(id: id, parent: parents):\n  insert parents { id: 'rolledback', label: 'temporary' }\n  insert kids { id, parent }\nquery parents(c: cursor ?):\n  return parents first 20 by .id after c\n";
fn run<H:Host>(host:&mut H){
 let (mut e,b)=compile(SOURCE);
 let mut d=host.open("qdiag",Some(b)).unwrap();
 for (i,page) in snapshot_pages(&mut e,"alice",10).iter().enumerate(){d.apply(page,i==0).unwrap();}
 let args=json!({"id":"p","child":"k","title":"label"});
 d.predict("add","alice",&args,100,vec![],1).unwrap();
 let actual=e.mutate("add",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("p")),("child",id("k")),("title",snapback4_core::Value::String("label".into()))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(actual.state,WriteState::Sent { result:snapback4_core::Value::String(ref s),.. } if s=="label"),"{actual:?}");
 let row=e.store().rows("kids").unwrap().pop().unwrap();
 assert_eq!(row.field("parent").unwrap(),&id("p"));
 let predicted=d.predict("clash","alice",&json!({"id":"other","parent":"p"}),100,vec![],2).unwrap_err();
 assert_eq!(predicted.code(),"E_UNIQUE","{predicted:?}");
 let conflict=e.mutate("clash",None,Context::new("alice",common::args(&[("id",id("other")),("parent",id("p"))])).at(100)).unwrap();
 assert!(matches!(conflict.state,WriteState::Failed { ref why,.. } if why.code=="E_UNIQUE" && why.message.contains("unique parent") && !why.message.contains("$intent")),"{conflict:?}");
 assert_eq!(e.store().rows("parents").unwrap().len(),1,"the earlier write rolled back");
 assert_eq!(query(&mut d),json!([{"id":"p","label":"label","pending":true}]),"device refusal rolled back");
 host.close("qdiag",d);
}
fn query<S:DeviceStore>(d:&mut Device<S>)->serde_json::Value{snapback4_device::call::call(d,json!({"op":"query","name":"parents","viewer":"alice","args":{"c":null},"now":100}))["ok"]["data"].clone()}

#[test]fn qdiag_sqlite(){run(&mut Sqlite::new("wave11-qdiag"));}
//! Compare implicit/explicit row-reference worlds through the simulator's
//! wire, receipts, stream, settlement and device-reopen observation harness.
#[path="common/write_observation.rs"] mod observation;
use snapback4_core::Engine;
use serde_json::json;
fn engine(source:&str)->Engine{let b=snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(),source.into())]);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);Engine::memory(b.schema,b.programs.into_iter().map(|p|p.program)).unwrap()}
#[test]
fn contextual_projection_preserves_every_published_observation(){
 let source="table parents:\n  label: text <=20\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\nmutation create(id: id, kid: id):\n  parent = insert parents { id, label:'parent' }\n  insert kids { id:kid, parent }\n  return parent.id\nquery list(c: cursor ?):\n  return kids first 10 by .id after c\n";
 let mut a=engine(source);let mut b=engine(&source.replace("id:kid, parent","id:kid, parent:parent.id"));
 let (_,a)=observation::observe(&mut a,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 let (_,b)=observation::observe(&mut b,"create","alice",json!({"id":"p","kid":"k"}),"qdiag-op",("list",json!({"c":null})));
 a.assert_same(&b);
}
use snapback4_difftest::{generate,execute,lean};
#[test]
fn generated_contextual_sources_match_native_and_lean(){
    for seed in 0..32 {
        let (case,implicit,explicit)=generate::qdiag(seed);
        let a=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&implicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
        let b=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&explicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
        assert_eq!(a,b,"seed {seed}");
        for source in [&implicit,&explicit] {
            let checked=lean::compare(&case,source).unwrap();
            assert!(matches!(checked.verdict,snapback4_difftest::oracle::Verdict::Pass),"seed {seed}: {:?}",checked.verdict);
        }
    }
}

             || case.program.tables.iter().any(|t| t.columns.iter().any(|c| matches!(c.ty, crate::ast::Type::IntRange(..))));

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/src/generate.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/src/generate.rs
index 8479350d96..1282870e9a 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/src/generate.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/src/generate.rs
@@ -346,3 +346,20 @@ pub(crate) fn page3(case: &mut Case) {
         for (name, row) in &mut case.rows { if name == "access" { row["role"] = json!(if rng.pick(2) == 0 { "admin" } else { "staff" }); } }

+
+/// Wave-11 contextual reference family. Keep the sources independent of IR;
+/// the explicit spelling is a metamorphic oracle for the contextual spelling.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — inserted/lookup snapshots project only in reference contexts.
+pub fn qdiag(seed:u64) -> (Case,String,String) {
+    let mut case=base(seed);
+    case.program.body=Body::Block(Block { stmts:vec![],ret:Expr::Bool(true) });
+    case.program.prelude.clear(); case.steps.clear(); case.rows.clear(); case.budget=Ceilings::default();
+    case.args=json!({"key":"new-parent","k2":"new-child","bucket":0,"c":null,"n":null,"a":0,"b":1});
+    let mut rng=Rng::new(seed);
+    let (owner,visible)=(if rng.pick(2)==0 {"alice"} else {"bob"},rng.pick(2)==0);
+    if rng.pick(3)==0 { case.rows.push(("kids".into(),json!({"id":"old-child","item":"new-parent","owner":owner}))); }
+    let read=if visible {"allow"} else {".owner = viewer"};
+    let implicit=format!("table items:\n  owner: principal\n  label: text <=64\n  public\n  insert <- allow\n  sync public\ntable kids:\n  item: items on delete keep\n  owner: principal\n  unique item\n  read <- {read}\n  insert <- allow\n  online only\nmutation run(key: id, k2: id, bucket: int, c: cursor ?, n: int ?, a: int, b: int):\n  item = insert items {{ id:key, owner:viewer, label:'parent' }}\n  insert kids {{ id:k2, item, owner:viewer }}\n  return item.id\nquery observe(key: id, k2: id):\n  return {{ item:items[key], child:kids[k2] }}\n");
+    let explicit=implicit.replace("id:k2, item, owner","id:k2, item:item.id, owner");
+    (case,implicit,explicit)
+}

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/wave11_qdiag.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/wave11_qdiag.rs
new file mode 100644
index 0000000000..7be3caf742
--- /dev/null
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-difftest/tests/wave11_qdiag.rs
@@ -0,0 +1,14 @@
+use snapback4_difftest::{generate,execute,lean};
+#[test]
+fn generated_contextual_sources_match_native_and_lean(){
+    for seed in 0..32 {
+        let (case,implicit,explicit)=generate::qdiag(seed);
+        let a=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&implicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
+        let b=execute::execute(&mut execute::engine(&case,&explicit).unwrap(),&case,"run",&case.args);
+        assert_eq!(a,b,"seed {seed}");
+        for source in [&implicit,&explicit] {
+            let checked=lean::compare(&case,source).unwrap();
+            assert!(matches!(checked.verdict,snapback4_difftest::oracle::Verdict::Pass),"seed {seed}: {:?}",checked.verdict);
+        }
+    }
+}

+#[test]
+fn contextual_reference_projection_is_independent_and_unresolved_helpers_are_named(){
+ let source="table parents:\n  name: text <=20\n  public\n  insert <- allow\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  public\n  insert <- allow\nmutation add(id: id):\n  parent = insert parents {id,name:'parent'}\n  insert kids {id:'child',parent}\n  return parent.id\n";
+ let implicit=body(&module(source),"add");
+ let explicit=body(&module(&source.replace("id:'child',parent}","id:'child',parent:parent.id}")),"add");
+ assert_eq!(implicit,explicit);
+ let helper=source.replace("mutation add(id: id):","fn same(p: parents): p\nmutation add(id: id):").replace("id:'child',parent}","id:'child',parent:same(parent)}");
+ assert_eq!(reason(&module(&helper),"add"),"reference-expression-type");
+}

     1	//! Referential actions: what deleting a referenced row does to the rows that
     2	//! name it (`on delete restrict | cascade | set null`).
     3	// @ref LLP 3000.039#2 — every reference between ordinary durable tables carries one action; restrict is the default.
     4	use super::*;
     5	pub use reservations::Price;
     6	
     7	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash, Serialize, Deserialize)]
     8	#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]
     9	pub enum OnDelete {
    10	    Restrict,
    11	    Cascade,
    12	    SetNull,
    13	    /// The referencing row stays and names a deleted row (the pre-0.5 meaning),
    14	    /// declared on purpose: history, audit trails, contributions that outlive
    15	    /// their target.
    16	    Keep,
    17	}
    18	
    19	impl OnDelete {
    20	    pub fn words(self) -> &'static str {
    21	        match self { Self::Restrict => "on delete restrict", Self::Cascade => "on delete cascade", Self::SetNull => "on delete set null", Self::Keep => "on delete keep" }
    22	    }
    23	    /// The actions that write the children, bounded by a cap on the reference.
    24	    pub fn writes(self) -> bool { matches!(self, Self::Cascade | Self::SetNull) }
    25	    /// The actions a delete must consult (every one but `keep`).
    26	    pub fn acts(self) -> bool { self != Self::Keep }
    27	}
    28	
    29	/// One action-carrying reference `child.column -> parent`.
    30	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug)]
    31	pub struct Referencing<'a> {
    32	    pub child: &'a str,
    33	    pub table: &'a Table,
    34	    pub column: &'a str,
    35	    pub action: OnDelete,
    36	}
    37	
    38	/// The prefix of the reference index a live child of `parent` is found under.
    39	pub const REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX: &str = "$reference:";
    40	pub fn reference_index_name(column: &str) -> String { format!("{REFERENCE_INDEX_PREFIX}{column}") }
    41	
    42	/// Heap a closure keeps per reached row: its table and id (an id is at most
    43	/// 144 canonical bytes) and the set and queue entries holding them.
    44	pub const CLOSURE_IDENTITY_BYTES: u64 = 384;
    45	/// Child rows a cascade or set-null walk reads per seek (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
    46	pub const REFERENCE_PAGE: usize = 4;
    47	
    48	impl Schema {
    49	    /// Whether rows of `name` take part in referential actions, as child or
    50	    /// parent: an ordinary durable application table. Ephemeral rows, engine
    51	    /// derivations and receipt/timeline bookkeeping keep their own lifetimes.
    52	    pub fn actionable(&self, name: &str) -> bool {
    53	        let Some(table) = self.table(name) else { return false };
    54	        table.ephemeral.is_none() && self.maintain_for(name).is_none() && self.private_owner(name).is_none()
    55	            && !self.is_recount_work(name) && !self.timelines.iter().any(|t| t.owns(name))
    56	            && !self.receipts.iter().any(|f| [&f.scope, &f.members, &f.runs].iter().any(|t| t.as_str() == name))
    57	    }
    58	
    59	    /// Every reference into `parent` a delete consults (all but `keep`), in
    60	    /// (child, column) order.
    61	    pub fn referencing<'a>(&'a self, parent: &str) -> Vec<Referencing<'a>> {
    62	        // A plain loop into a vector: iterator adapters monomorphize per call site on the web device.
    63	        let mut out = Vec::new();
    64	        for (child, table) in &self.tables {
    65	            for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
    66	                if action.acts() && matches!(table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: target }) if target == parent) {
    67	                    out.push(Referencing { child, table, column, action: *action });
    68	                }
    69	            }
    70	        }
    71	        out
    72	    }
    73	
    74	    /// A delete of a `parent` row consults its referencing rows, so it needs
    75	    /// read authority on that row (LLP 3000.039 §3 step 1).
    76	    pub fn is_referenced(&self, parent: &str) -> bool { !self.referencing(parent).is_empty() }
    77	
    78	    /// The index a reference is probed through: its column first, then the
    79	    /// child's deadline column when the child can expire, so one seek skips an
    80	    /// expired backlog. A `read through` reference uses its child index.
    81	    pub fn reference_index(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<&str> {
    82	        let table = self.table(child)?;
    83	        if table.through.as_ref().is_some_and(|t| t.reference == column) {
    84	            return table.indexes.contains_key(through::CHILDREN).then_some(through::CHILDREN);
    85	        }
    86	        // The effective deadline: a read-through child's `$deadline`, else `expire at`.
    87	        let deadline = table.expiry_column();
    88	        table.indexes.iter().find(|(_, index)| {
    89	            let columns = index.columns();
    90	            index.is_plain() && columns.first().map(String::as_str) == Some(column)
    91	                && deadline.is_none_or(|deadline| columns.get(1).map(String::as_str) == Some(deadline))
    92	        }).map(|(name, _)| name.as_str())
    93	    }
    94	
    95	    /// The cap bounding a reference's children: `cap N by <column>` exactly,
    96	    /// or one when the reference itself is a plain unique key. A nullable
    97	    /// reference may have many nulls, but a parent delete probes one non-null
    98	    /// parent id, whose unique group still has at most one row.
    99	    pub fn reference_cap(&self, child: &str, column: &str) -> Option<u32> {

    /// Whether the viewer may `effect` the row. The system principal is the
    /// store's owner and passes every rule; validation and constraints
    /// still run for it.
    pub(crate) fn check_rule(&mut self, table_name: &str, effect: Effect, old: Option<&Value>, next: &Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> {
        if effect != Effect::Read && self.timeline_may_write(table_name, effect) { return Ok(true); }
        if self.context.is_system() {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        if effect == Effect::Read && self.is_provisional(table_name, next)? { return self.provisional_readable(table_name, next); }
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        // A device holds a synced table's rows because the server delivered
        // each under the read rule and withdraws what it revokes; what
        // `retain delivered-history` kept stays readable here (LLP 3000 §7).
        if effect == Effect::Read && self.trust_synced_read(table) {
            return Ok(true);
        }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#8 — item 7(a): an unnamed update rule judges old and next.
        let mut rule = table.rules.for_effect(effect).clone();
        if effect == Effect::Update && old.is_some() && table.judges_old_image() { rule = rule.old_and_next(); }
        if effect == Effect::Update && let Some(old) = old {
            for (field, authority) in &table.written_by {
                if !authority.audience && old.field(&authority.owner).ok() != Some(&Value::Id(self.context.viewer.clone())) {
                    rule.fold_untouched_field(field, &table.columns[field]);
                }
            }
        }
        if effect == Effect::Read { self.rule_dependencies(&rule); }
        let bytes = self.rule_scratch(&rule);
        let result = self.authorized_scratch(bytes, &[(MeterKind::RuleProbes, rules::rule_probes(&rule))], |runner, allowance| {
            let mut next = next.clone();
            materialize_optional_fields(&mut next, table);
            let old = old.map(|old| {
                let mut old = old.clone();
                materialize_optional_fields(&mut old, table);
                old
            });
            runner.evaluate_sequence_rule(allowance, table_name, &rule, effect, old.as_ref(), &next)
        }).map_err(|error| error.authorization_context(table_name, effect));
        self.trace_rule_result(&result, table_name, effect);
        result
    }

    /// A row by id, materialized, without a rule check.
    pub(crate) fn fetch(&mut self, _allowance: &Authorization, table_name: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self.schema.table(table_name).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        let found = if crate::rows::disclosed::actual_size(table) {
            self.live_lookup(_allowance, table_name, "byId", &table.indexes["byId"], &[Value::Id(id.into())], #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")] None)?
        } else { self.authorized_scratch(crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table), &[], |runner, authority| {
            runner.tx.get(authority, table_name, id).map(|found| {
                        found.map(|mut row| {
                materialize_optional_fields(&mut row, table);
                row
            })
                    })
        })? };
        if self.device && (table.sync.is_none() || (found.is_none() && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|s| s.horizon.is_some()))) { self.unknown_fact(table_name, "byId", &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())], "write target"); }
        match found {
            Some(row) if !self.inactive(table_name, &row)? => Ok(Some(row)),
            _ => Ok(None),
        }
    }

    /// A server write may address an unopened row without making that image
    /// readable. Fixed decode admission prevents its payload size from
    /// selecting a public meter result.
    pub(crate) fn fetch_write_target(
        &mut self,
        allowance: &Authorization,
        table_name: &str,
        id: &str,
    ) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        if self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .is_none_or(|table| table.not_before.is_none())
        {
            return self.fetch(allowance, table_name, id);
        }
        self.schema.refuse_private(table_name)?;
        let table = self
            .schema
            .table(table_name)
            .ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("missing table {table_name}")))?;
        if self.device {
            self.unknown_fact(
                table_name,
                "byId",
                &[Value::Id(id.to_owned())],
                "scheduled write target",
            );
            return Ok(None);
//! The page candidate contract: a provisional deadline travels with its image
//! and is emitted only by the page. The scan loop inlines this under the
//! sealed projection boundary (`AuthorizedRow`, LLP 3000.015 §1); the
//! read_verdict tests exercise the contract itself.
use super::{expiry::Pending, runner::Runner};
use crate::{error::RuntimeError, schema::Effect, value::Value};

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn read_candidate(
        &mut self,
        table_name: &str,
        row: Value,
        uniform_verdict: Option<bool>,
        prefix_expiry: Pending,
    ) -> Result<Option<(Value, Pending)>, RuntimeError> {

        self.next_expiry.inherit(prefix_expiry);
        let allowed = match uniform_verdict {
            Some(verdict) => verdict && self.provisional_readable(table_name, &row)?,
            None => self.check_rule(table_name, Effect::Read, None, &row)?,
        };
        if self.read_capped() || !allowed {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        let table = &self.schema.tables[table_name];
        self.note_expiry(table, &row);
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.21 — an unfinished live total is unavailable.
        let row = self.live_aggregate(table_name, row)?;
        if self.read_capped() {
            return Ok(None);
        }
        // Authorization is not emission. Filters, lookahead and retention still
        // have to finish before the caller can commit this candidate's deadline.
        row.map(|mut row| {
            self.stamp_source(table_name, &mut row)?;
            Ok((super::recorded::public_image(table, row), expiry.defer()))
        }).transpose()
    }
}

        // decodes a full transient image; the streaming consumer retains only
        // its ordering key. Selection remains useful in the private decoder.
        if (self.context.is_system() || trusted && !table.read_lifetime_sensitive()) && table.recorded.is_empty() && table.through.is_none() && table.written_by.values().all(|a| a.audience) {
            if metered { self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'_>>,Vec<u8>,
                )>(limit))?; }
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            let live = table.read_lifetime_sensitive();
            // A web device never reads as the system, the only reader of an expiring table here.
            #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
            let live = false;
            let rows = if live { self.live_store_scan(name, index, prefix, bounds, dir, limit, metered)? }
            else if metered {
                self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan_metered(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit,&mut runner.meter)
                })?
            } else { self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                    runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,bounds,dir,limit)
                })? };

            // own prediction rows still meet a non-uniform rule, as prepaid internal work outside the caller's
            // heap; after an earlier cut such a row stays hidden (the read is incomplete either way).
            let check = self.device && !self.context.is_system() && !table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable();
            let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(rows.len());
