                    self.prepaid_scan(&[], false, |runner, scope| {
                        runner.candidate_verdict(scope, name, index, prefix, row, true, rule_prepaid)
                    })?
                } else { Some(AuthorizedRow { projection:self.disclosed_row(table,row),authority:RowAuthorization(()),expiry:Default::default() }) };
                out.push((row, key));
            }
            return Ok(out);
        }
        let bytes = crate::rows::disclosed::decode_reservation(table).saturating_add(self.rule_scratch(&table.rules.read));
        if metered { self.meter.heap(crate::rows::word_heap::slots::<(Option<AuthorizedRow<'_>>, Vec<u8>,
            )>(limit))?; }
        let mut cursor = bounds.clone();
        let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(limit);
        for _ in 0..limit {
            let candidate = self.reserved_scratch(bytes, &[], !trusted, |runner, scope| {
                loop {
                #[cfg(not(feature = "server-search"))]
                let selected = runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,&cursor,dir,1)?;
                #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
                let selected = if let Some(fields) = fields { runner.tx.scan_selected(scope,name,index,prefix,&cursor,dir,fields)? } else { runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,&cursor,dir,1)? };
                let Some((row,key)) = selected.into_iter().next() else { return Ok(None); };
                // Advancing over a rejected candidate is internal decoder work.
                cursor.after = crate::key::decode(&key).map(|v|v.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect());
                // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.6 — an expired, unswept candidate is never returned, counted or charged.
                if runner.step_inactive(name, &row, &key)? { continue; }
                return Ok(Some((runner.candidate_verdict(scope, name, index, prefix, row, trusted, rule_prepaid)?,key,
                    )));
                }
            })?;
            let Some((row,key)) = candidate else { break; };
            // Only this sealed surviving projection can be priced on the caller ledger.
            if metered && let Some(row) = &row {
                self.meter.heap(row.heap_bytes() + row.projection_heap() + row.materialization_heap()
                    + crate::rows::word_heap::key_decode(table.index(index).map_or(1,|i|i.components.len()+1),key.len()))?;
            }
            out.push((row,key));
        }
        Ok(out)
    }

    /// A system read of an expiring table takes the store path, so the store
    /// would price every fetched entry. Open the authored range once, step
    /// over expired entries internally, and price live entries through the
    /// same canonical-image codec used by every store.
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.24.6 — the public meter charges only rows live at the read's clock.
    #[cfg_attr(not(feature = "sqlite"), allow(dead_code))]
    fn live_store_scan(&mut self, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &crate::rows::Bounds, dir: crate::rows::Dir, limit: usize, metered: bool) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let mut cursor = bounds.clone();
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        // Price the authored range once. Internal cursors over expired keys
        // cannot select another public scan-opening charge or wider bounds.
        let mut guard = super::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut self.meter, |meter, scope| {
            if metered { crate::rows::word_heap::scan_open(meter, name, index, prefix, bounds, scope)?; }
            Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(crate::rows::word_heap::StoreGuard::open(meter, super::read::EXPIRED_STEP_GUARD))
        })?;
        while out.len() < limit {
            let window = self.metered_scan(|runner, scope| {
                runner.tx.scan(scope,name,index,prefix,&cursor,dir,limit - out.len())
            })?;
            if window.is_empty() { break; }
            for (row, key) in window {
                cursor.after = crate::key::decode(&key).map(|v|v.into_iter().skip(prefix.len()).collect());
                if self.step_inactive(name, &row, &key)? { continue; }
                if metered {
                    let additional = super::authorized_scan::InternalLedger::with_meter(&mut self.meter, |meter, _| {
                        crate::rows::word_heap::scan_value_entry(meter, &mut guard, out.len(), out.capacity(), &row, &key)
                    })?;
                    if additional != 0 { out.reserve_exact(additional); }
                }
                out.push((row, key));
            }
        }
        Ok(out)
    }

    /// One fetched candidate's expiry, rule and field verdict, inside an
    /// internal scope. Unadmitted rule work is an unknown fact on a device.
    fn candidate_verdict(&mut self, scope: &super::authorized_scan::Authorization, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], mut row: Value, trusted: bool, rule_prepaid: bool) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);
//! Console aggregation stays on the evidence writer, outside request paths.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — one counted line per cause in a fixed one-second window.
use serde_json::Value;
use std::{collections::VecDeque, io::Write, time::{Duration, Instant}};

const WINDOW: Duration = Duration::from_secs(1);
const LIMIT: usize = 32;
struct Entry { record: Value, count: u64, until: Instant }
#[derive(Default)]
pub(super) struct Console { entries: VecDeque<Entry>, detail: bool }

fn same_cause(a: &Value, b: &Value) -> bool {
    ["operation", "code", "table", "clause", "phase", "site", "executionSite", "generation", "hiddenBy", "preflight"]
        .iter().all(|key| a[key] == b[key])
}
impl Console {
    /// `dev --verbose` keeps the full evidence line; the default is one short line per cause.
    pub fn with_detail(detail: bool) -> Self { Self { entries: VecDeque::new(), detail } }
    pub fn wait(&self, now: Instant) -> Duration {
        self.entries.front().map_or(WINDOW, |entry| entry.until.saturating_duration_since(now))
    }
    pub fn push(&mut self, record: Value, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        self.push_at(record, Instant::now(), identity, out);
    }
    pub(super) fn push_at(&mut self, record: Value, now: Instant, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        self.flush_due(now, identity, out);
        if let Some(entry) = self.entries.iter_mut().find(|entry| same_cause(&entry.record, &record)) {
            entry.count = entry.count.saturating_add(1);
            return;
        }
        // Bounded even under distinct operations/causes: end the oldest window
        // early rather than losing its count or growing memory without bound.
        if self.entries.len() == LIMIT { self.emit_first(identity, out); }
        self.entries.push_back(Entry { record, count: 1, until: now + WINDOW });
    }
    pub fn flush_due(&mut self, now: Instant, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        while self.entries.front().is_some_and(|entry| entry.until <= now) { self.emit_first(identity, out); }
    }
    pub fn flush_all(&mut self, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        while !self.entries.is_empty() { self.emit_first(identity, out); }
    }
    fn emit_first(&mut self, identity: &Value, out: &mut impl Write) {
        let entry = self.entries.pop_front().unwrap();
        let r = entry.record;
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — a counted refusal remains one line beside lifecycle diagnostics.
        // Raw signal/thread-exit writes do not share Rust's stderr lock. Format
        // the complete line before touching the descriptor, so they cannot
        // splice themselves between this message's formatting fragments.
        let table = r["table"].as_str().filter(|t| !t.is_empty()).map(|t| format!(" {t}")).unwrap_or_default();
        // A lookup refusal whose row the read rule hid: name that layer, since the author's later `require` never ran.
        let hidden = r["hiddenBy"]["table"].as_str().map(|t| format!("; the lookup's {t} row is hidden by its read rule at {} (a hidden row reads as absent at a lookup, before any later require)", r["hiddenBy"]["readRuleSite"].as_str().unwrap_or("<generated>"))).unwrap_or_default();
        let kind = if r["preflight"].as_bool() == Some(true) { "auth preflight probe" } else { "refusal" };
        let line = if self.detail { format!("dev {kind}: {}:{table} — {} at {}{hidden}; execution site {}; phase {}; generation {}; store {}; instance {}; code {}; count {}; window 1s; first request {}; first job {}; first time {}; first response {}; `snapback4 why recent --instance {}`\n",
            r["operation"].as_str().unwrap_or(""), r["clause"].as_str().unwrap_or(""), r["site"].as_str().unwrap_or("<generated>"), r["executionSite"], r["phase"], r["generation"], identity["storeId"], identity["instance"], r["code"], entry.count, r["requestId"], r["jobId"], r["at"], r["responseSequence"], identity["instance"].as_str().unwrap_or("")) }
        else {
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#16.5 — wave 6 #19: the human signal first, one short line; the record stays in `why recent`.
            let on = r["table"].as_str().filter(|t| !t.is_empty()).map(|t| format!(" on {t}")).unwrap_or_default();
            let hidden = r["hiddenBy"]["table"].as_str().map(|t| format!("; the {t} row is hidden by its read rule at {} (hidden reads as absent)", r["hiddenBy"]["readRuleSite"].as_str().unwrap_or("<generated>"))).unwrap_or_default();
            let times = if entry.count > 1 { format!(" ×{}", entry.count) } else { String::new() };
            format!("dev {kind}: {} {} at {} ({}{on}){hidden}{times} — snapback4 why recent\n", r["operation"].as_str().unwrap_or(""), r["code"].as_str().unwrap_or(""), r["site"].as_str().unwrap_or("<generated>"), r["clause"].as_str().unwrap_or(""))
        };
        let _ = out.write_all(line.as_bytes());
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use serde_json::json;
    #[test]
    fn shutdown_diagnostic_cannot_split_a_counted_refusal_line() {
        // Lifecycle diagnostics bypass Rust's stderr mutex. Simulate one
        // arriving after the writer's first fragment, as observed at shutdown.
        struct Interrupted { bytes: Vec<u8>, inserted: bool }
        impl std::io::Write for Interrupted {
            fn write(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> std::io::Result<usize> {
                self.bytes.extend_from_slice(bytes);
                if !self.inserted {
                    self.inserted = true;
                    self.bytes.extend_from_slice(b"SNAPBACK4_THREAD_EXIT verb=dev generation=1 thread=\"worker\" reason=\"shutdown\"\n");
                }
                Ok(bytes.len())
            }
            fn flush(&mut self) -> std::io::Result<()> { Ok(()) }
        }
        let mut out = Interrupted { bytes: Vec::new(), inserted: false };
        let mut console = Console::with_detail(true);
        let now = Instant::now();
        let identity = json!({"storeId":"store", "instance":"instance"});
        let record = json!({"operation":"add", "code":"E_RULE", "table":"rows",
            "clause":"insert rule", "phase":"insert rule", "site":"schema.q:5:3",
            "executionSite":"add:3", "generation":1, "requestId":2});
        for _ in 0..8 { console.push_at(record.clone(), now, &identity, &mut out); }
        assert!(out.bytes.is_empty(), "the aggregation window is still open");
        console.flush_all(&identity, &mut out);
        let text = String::from_utf8(out.bytes).unwrap();
        let lines: Vec<_> = text.lines().collect();
        assert_eq!(lines.len(), 2, "{text}");
        assert!(lines[0].starts_with("dev refusal: add: rows — insert rule at schema.q:5:3;"), "{text}");
        assert!(lines[0].contains("code \"E_RULE\"; count 8; window 1s; first request 2;"), "{text}");
        assert!(lines[0].ends_with("`snapback4 why recent --instance instance`"), "{text}");
        assert_eq!(lines[1], "SNAPBACK4_THREAD_EXIT verb=dev generation=1 thread=\"worker\" reason=\"shutdown\"", "{text}");
    }
    #[test]
    fn counted_line_stays_intact_between_uncoordinated_writes() {
        // Lifecycle diagnostics bypass Rust's stderr lock. Model another
        // complete diagnostic arriving after every underlying writer call.
        struct Interleaved(Vec<u8>);
        impl Write for Interleaved {
            fn write(&mut self, bytes: &[u8]) -> std::io::Result<usize> {
                self.0.extend_from_slice(bytes);
                self.0.extend_from_slice(b"SNAPBACK4_THREAD_EXIT reason=shutdown\n");
                Ok(bytes.len())
            }
            fn flush(&mut self) -> std::io::Result<()> { Ok(()) }
        }
        let mut console = Console::with_detail(true);
        let mut out = Interleaved(Vec::new());
        let now = Instant::now();
        let identity = json!({"instance":"test"});
        let record = json!({"operation":"add","code":"E_RULE","clause":"insert rule"});
        for _ in 0..8 { console.push_at(record.clone(), now, &identity, &mut out); }
        console.flush_all(&identity, &mut out);
        let text = String::from_utf8(out.0).unwrap();
        let lines: Vec<_> = text.lines().collect();
        assert_eq!(lines.len(), 2, "{text}");
        assert!(lines[0].starts_with("dev refusal: add:"), "{text}");
        assert!(lines[0].contains("code \"E_RULE\"; count 8; window 1s;"), "{text}");
        assert_eq!(lines[1], "SNAPBACK4_THREAD_EXIT reason=shutdown");
    }
    #[test]
    fn counts_repeats_without_sliding_and_separates_causes() {
        let mut console = Console::with_detail(true);
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let now = Instant::now();
        let identity = json!({"instance":"test"});
        let record = json!({"operation":"add","code":"E_RULE","clause":"insert rule"});
        for n in 0..1000 {
            let mut r = record.clone(); r["requestId"] = json!(n);
            console.push_at(r, now + Duration::from_micros(n), &identity, &mut out);
        }
        assert!(out.is_empty());
        console.flush_due(now + WINDOW, &identity, &mut out);
        let text = String::from_utf8(out.clone()).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(text.lines().count(), 1);
        assert!(text.contains("count 1000; window 1s; first request 0"));
        console.push_at(record, now + WINDOW, &identity, &mut out);
        console.flush_due(now + 2 * WINDOW, &identity, &mut out);
        assert_eq!(String::from_utf8(out).unwrap().lines().count(), 2);
    }
    #[test]
    fn every_cause_field_separates_identified_counts() {
        let identity = json!({"storeId":"store", "instance":"instance"});
        let record = json!({"operation":"add", "code":"E_RULE", "table":"rows",
            "clause":"insert rule", "phase":"insert", "site":"schema.q:5:3",
            "executionSite":"add:1", "generation":1, "requestId":11});
        for (key, different) in [
            ("operation", json!("update")), ("code", json!("E_FIELD_AUTHORITY")),
            ("table", json!("otherRows")), ("clause", json!("update rule")),
            ("phase", json!("read through capture")), ("site", json!("schema.q:9:3")),
            ("executionSite", json!("add:2")), ("generation", json!(2)),
        ] {
            let mut console = Console::with_detail(true);
            let mut out = Vec::new();
            let now = Instant::now();
            let mut other = record.clone();
            other[key] = different;
            other["requestId"] = json!(22);
            for _ in 0..2 { console.push_at(record.clone(), now, &identity, &mut out); }
            for _ in 0..3 { console.push_at(other.clone(), now, &identity, &mut out); }
            console.flush_all(&identity, &mut out);
            let text = String::from_utf8(out).unwrap();
            let lines: Vec<_> = text.lines().collect();
            assert_eq!(lines.len(), 2, "grouping field {key}: {text}");
            for (line, expected, count, request) in [(lines[0], &record, 2, 11), (lines[1], &other, 3, 22)] {
                assert!(line.starts_with(&format!("dev refusal: {}: {} — {} at {};", expected["operation"].as_str().unwrap(), expected["table"].as_str().unwrap(), expected["clause"].as_str().unwrap(), expected["site"].as_str().unwrap())), "{key}: {line}");
                for field in ["code", "phase", "generation"] {
                    assert!(line.contains(&format!("{field} {};", expected[field])), "{key}: {line}");
                }
                assert!(line.contains(&format!("execution site {};", expected["executionSite"])), "{key}: {line}");
                assert!(line.contains(&format!("count {count}; window 1s; first request {request};")), "{key}: {line}");
                assert!(line.contains("store \"store\"; instance \"instance\";"), "{key}: {line}");
            }
        }
    }
    #[test]
    fn the_default_line_is_short_and_leads_with_the_operation_code_and_site() {
        let mut console = Console::default();
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let now = Instant::now();
        let identity = json!({"storeId":"store", "instance":"instance"});
        let record = json!({"operation":"acceptRide", "code":"NOT_FOUND", "table":"rides", "clause":"lookup", "phase":"lookup", "site":"schema.q:31:3",
            "executionSite":"acceptRide:1", "generation":1, "requestId":2, "hiddenBy":{"table":"rides","readRuleSite":"schema.q:12:3"}});
        for _ in 0..3 { console.push_at(record.clone(), now, &identity, &mut out); }
        console.flush_all(&identity, &mut out);
        let text = String::from_utf8(out).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(text, "dev refusal: acceptRide NOT_FOUND at schema.q:31:3 (lookup on rides); the rides row is hidden by its read rule at schema.q:12:3 (hidden reads as absent) ×3 — snapback4 why recent\n");
        assert!(text.len() < 200, "{text}");
    }
    #[test]
    fn auth_preflight_probes_are_labelled_and_never_grouped_with_operation_failures() {
        for detail in [false,true] {
            let mut console = Console::with_detail(detail);
            let mut out=Vec::new();let now=Instant::now();
            let ordinary=json!({"operation":"page","code":"E_AUTH","clause":"authentication","requestId":1});
            let mut preflight=ordinary.clone();preflight["preflight"]=json!(true);
            console.push_at(ordinary,now,&Value::Null,&mut out);
            console.push_at(preflight,now,&Value::Null,&mut out);
            console.flush_all(&Value::Null,&mut out);
            let text=String::from_utf8(out).unwrap();let lines=text.lines().collect::<Vec<_>>();
            assert_eq!(lines.len(),2,"{text}");assert!(lines[0].starts_with("dev refusal:"),"{text}");assert!(lines[1].starts_with("dev auth preflight probe:"),"{text}");
        }
    }
    #[test]
    fn distinct_causes_are_bounded_and_counts_flush_on_disconnect() {
        let mut console = Console::with_detail(true);
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        let now = Instant::now();
        for n in 0..100 {
            console.push_at(json!({"operation":n}), now, &Value::Null, &mut out);
            assert!(console.entries.len() <= LIMIT);
        }
        console.flush_all(&Value::Null, &mut out);
        assert_eq!(String::from_utf8(out).unwrap().lines().count(), 100);
    }
}
//! A request scope plus a response-layer backstop, never a caller channel.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — missing instrumentation must supersede older evidence.
use super::{Recent, Sites};
use serde_json::{Value, json};
use snapback4_core::{Context, engine::refusal_trace::RefusalTrace};
use std::{cell::RefCell, sync::{Arc, atomic::Ordering}};

thread_local! { static CURRENT: RefCell<Option<Evidence>> = const { RefCell::new(None) }; }
struct Evidence {
    recent: Recent,
    operation: String,
    request: u64,
    query: bool,
    trace: RefusalTrace,
    sites: Arc<Sites>,
    generation: Option<u64>,
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct RequestScope;
impl Drop for RequestScope { fn drop(&mut self) { CURRENT.with(|c| c.borrow_mut().take()); } }
pub(in crate::serve) fn begin(shared: &crate::serve::Shared, path: &str) -> Option<RequestScope> {
    if !shared.personas { return None; }
    let recent = shared.refusals.clone();
    let request = recent.requests.fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed) + 1;
    CURRENT.with(|c| *c.borrow_mut() = Some(Evidence { recent,
        operation: path.strip_prefix("/q/").or_else(|| path.strip_prefix("/m/")).unwrap_or(path).into(),
        request, query: path.starts_with("/q/"), trace: Default::default(), sites: Default::default(), generation: None }));
    Some(RequestScope)
}
pub(in crate::serve) fn context(context: Context, sites: Arc<Sites>, generation: u64) -> Context {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let Some(evidence) = c.as_mut() else { return context };
        evidence.sites = sites;
        evidence.generation = Some(generation);
        context.with_refusal_trace(evidence.trace.clone())
    })
}
pub(in crate::serve) struct Pending { recent: Recent, record: Value }
impl Drop for Pending {
    fn drop(&mut self) { self.recent.submit(std::mem::take(&mut self.record)); }
}
// Inspect the actual encoded envelope, after overflow substitution. This covers
// every responder using the shared JSON response layer, including receipt replay,
// static admission and future paths with no attached collector. No user data is
// copied into the record. Unknown empty answers enter only the opt-in local
// trace; successful visibility filtering is never a refusal.
pub(in crate::serve) fn response(bytes: &[u8]) -> Option<Pending> {
    CURRENT.with(|c| {
        let mut c = c.borrow_mut();
        let evidence = c.as_mut()?;
        let body: Value = serde_json::from_slice(bytes).ok()?;
        if evidence.query && evidence.trace.hidden() > 0 {
            eprintln!("dev query: {} hidden: {} (encountered row images filtered by read rules)", evidence.operation, evidence.trace.hidden());
        }
        let code = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why")).and_then(|v| v.get("code")).and_then(Value::as_str);
        let traced = evidence.trace.take();
        let why = body.get("denied").or_else(|| body.get("why"));
        // The one filtered read that is evidence about a program refusal: the
        // refusing `t[x] ! else CODE` lookup itself, whose read rule hid the row.
        let hidden = traced.as_ref().filter(|cause| !cause.terminal && cause.phase == "read rule").and_then(|cause| {
            let site = why?.get("site")?.as_str()?;
            evidence.sites.contains_key(&(cause.table.clone(), format!("lookup {site}"))).then(|| (cause.table.clone(), evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "read rule".into())).cloned()))
        });
        let cause = traced.filter(|cause| {
            // A filtered read earlier in a program is not evidence about a
            // later uninstrumented terminal refusal in that same request.
            code.is_none() || cause.terminal
        });
        let empty_answer = code.is_none();
        let code = match code {
            Some(code) => code,
            None if evidence.query && body.get("data").is_some() && body.get("loading") != Some(&Value::Bool(true)) => {
                // Visibility filtering is a successful read, not a refusal.
                if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| !cause.terminal && !cause.no_candidate) { return None; }
                if evidence.recent.trace_empty && empty(&body["data"]) { "EMPTY_READ" }
                else { return None; }
            }
            None => return None,
        };
        let preflight = code == "E_AUTH" && evidence.generation.is_none() && why.and_then(|v| v.get("site")).is_none();
        let mut record = json!({"preflight":preflight,"operation":evidence.operation, "code":code, "requestId":evidence.request,
            "generation":evidence.generation, "clause":"cause not instrumented", "at":snapback4_core::now_ms()});
        // A program's own `require … else CODE` (or `! else CODE`) carries
        // its source site in the refusal itself: the response's own
        // evidence, not an older trace, so the log can name the line.
        if cause.is_none() && let Some(why) = why
            && why.get("rule").and_then(Value::as_str) == Some(code) && let Some(site) = why.get("site").and_then(Value::as_str) {
            record["clause"] = json!("program refusal");
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["siteKind"] = json!("program");
            if let Some((table, rule)) = &hidden {
                record["hiddenBy"] = json!({"table": table, "readRuleSite": rule});
            }
        }
        if empty_answer {
            record["emptyRead"] = json!(true);
            record["cause"] = json!(if cause.as_ref().is_some_and(|cause| cause.no_candidate) { "no-candidate" } else { "unrecorded" });
            record["clause"] = json!("no probe miss and no rule cause were recorded");
        }
        if let Some(cause) = cause {
            let clause = evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.phase.clone()))
                .or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), cause.clause.clone())));
            let site = clause.or_else(|| evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table.clone(), "table".into())));
            // Whether the site is the clause's own line or the table's (an omitted rule).
            record["siteKind"] = json!(if clause.is_some() { "clause" } else if site.is_some() { "table" } else { "" });
            record["table"] = json!(cause.table);
            record["clause"] = json!(cause.clause);
            record["phase"] = json!(cause.phase);
            record["site"] = json!(site);
            record["executionSite"] = json!(cause.site);
            record["generation"] = json!(cause.generation);
            record["readRuleSite"] = json!(evidence.sites.get(&(cause.table, "read rule".into())));
        }
        // Published before sending: why refuses an old disk snapshot even when
        // the writer is blocked, this handoff drops, or responses finish out of order.
        Some(Pending { recent: evidence.recent.clone(), record: evidence.recent.sequence(record) })
    })
}
fn empty(value: &Value) -> bool {
    match value {
        Value::Null => true,
        Value::Array(values) => values.is_empty(),
        _ => false,
    }
}

//! One logical allowance for work whose shape can depend on unreadable rows.
use super::runner::Runner;
use std::cell::{Cell, RefCell};

// @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — one physical allowance per request, nested work included.
pub(super) const AUTHORIZATION_WALK_LIMIT: usize = 8192;
use crate::{
    assets::Asset,
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError}, meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter}, rows::{AcquiredBoundary, Bounds, Dir, PrefetchRequest, Rows}, schema::{Index, Table}, value::Value,
};

// Only this module mints the capability; it cannot be constructed or cloned
// by callers, and the borrowed token cannot escape the guarded closure.
pub struct Authorization { probes: Cell<u64>, remaining_probes: u64 }

impl Authorization {
    fn internal() -> Self { Self { probes: std::cell::Cell::new(0), remaining_probes: u64::MAX } }
    // The candidate's declared probe allowance is checked before authorization
    // executes. The enclosing scope commits this logical count on every exit.
    pub(super) fn admit_probes(&self, probes: u64) -> bool {
        let total = self.probes.get().saturating_add(probes);
        self.probes.set(total);
        total <= self.remaining_probes
    }

}


// No Rows implementation, Deref or raw accessor: row reads require the
// unforgeable token even when a caller has access to Runner's store facade.
pub(crate) struct GuardedRows<'a> { raw: &'a dyn Rows, remaining: Cell<usize>, authorizing: Cell<bool>, reference_prepaid: Cell<bool>, revision: Cell<u64>, lookup_cache: RefCell<Option<(String, String, Vec<Value>, Option<Value>)>> }
impl<'a> GuardedRows<'a> {
    pub(crate) fn new(raw: &'a dyn Rows) -> Self { Self { raw, remaining: Cell::new(AUTHORIZATION_WALK_LIMIT), authorizing: Cell::new(false), reference_prepaid: Cell::new(false), revision: Cell::new(0), lookup_cache: RefCell::new(None) } }
    pub(super) fn revision(&self) -> u64 { self.revision.get() }
    pub(super) fn remaining(&self) -> usize { self.remaining.get() }
    fn limit(&self, table: &str, requested: usize) -> Result<usize, RuntimeError> {
        if !self.authorizing.get() || self.reference_prepaid.get() { return Ok(requested); }
        if self.remaining.get() == 0 { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(requested.min(self.remaining.get()))
    }
    fn charge(&self, fetched: usize) {
        if self.authorizing.get() && !self.reference_prepaid.get() { self.remaining.set(self.remaining.get().saturating_sub(fetched)); }
    }
    /// Return the walk allowance of a fetched row the store stepped over as
    /// expired: such rows are absent, so they cannot exhaust a read's walk.
    pub(super) fn refund(&self, fetched: usize) {
        if self.authorizing.get() { self.remaining.set(self.remaining.get() + fetched); }
    }
    pub(crate) fn get(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, id: &str) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let row = self.raw.get(table, id)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(row.is_some()));
        Ok(row)
    }
    /// A key-only witness still consumes the shared physical authorization allowance.
    pub(crate) fn index_head(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir) -> Result<Option<Vec<Value>>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let key = self.raw.index_head(table, index, prefix, bounds, dir)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(key.is_some()));
        Ok(key)
    }
    pub(crate) fn lookup(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, index: &Index, key: &[Value]) -> Result<Option<Value>, RuntimeError> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — repeated unchanged points share decoded storage,
        // while every logical read still prepays its complete rule.
        if let Some((cached_table, cached_index, cached_key, row)) = &*self.lookup_cache.borrow()
            && cached_table == table && cached_index == name && cached_key == key { return Ok(row.clone()); }
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let row = self.raw.lookup(table, name, index, key)?;
        self.charge(usize::from(row.is_some()));
        *self.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some((table.into(), name.into(), key.to_vec(), row.clone()));
        Ok(row)
    }
    pub(crate) fn scan(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, limit: usize) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let permitted = self.limit(table, limit)?;
        let rows = self.raw.scan(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, permitted)?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        if permitted < limit && rows.len() == permitted { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(rows)
    }
    #[cfg(feature = "server-search")]
    pub(crate) fn scan_selected(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, fields: &[String]) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        self.limit(table, 1)?;
        let rows = self.raw.scan_selected(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, fields)?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        Ok(rows)
    }
    pub(crate) fn scan_metered(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], bounds: &Bounds, dir: Dir, limit: usize, meter: &mut Meter) -> Result<Vec<(Value, Vec<u8>)>, RuntimeError> {
        let permitted = self.limit(table, limit)?;
        let rows = InternalLedger::with_meter(meter, |meter, _| {
            self.raw.scan_metered(table, name, prefix, bounds, dir, permitted, _authorization, meter)
        })?;
        self.charge(rows.len());
        if permitted < limit && rows.len() == permitted { return Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { table: table.into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Read }); }
        Ok(rows)
    }
    pub(crate) fn count_prefix(&self, _authorization: &Authorization, table: &str, name: &str, prefix: &[Value], limit: u64) -> Result<u64, RuntimeError> {
        // This scalar adapter is used only by non-expiring, non-private
        // caps. It fetches no candidate rows and keeps its declared cap bound.
        self.raw.count_prefix(table, name, prefix, limit)
    }

    fn point_event_held(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value], row: &Value) -> bool {
        if self.device && !crate::schema::timeline::holds_event(self.schema, &read.table, row) {
            self.event_read_unknown(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
            return false;

    /// A key-authorized synthetic aggregate image (its fields are the caller's
    /// key, the group id derived from it and the disclosed total) is sealed
    /// without a second row lookup: nothing in it came from hidden storage.
    pub(super) fn authorized_synthetic(&self, table: &'r crate::schema::Table, row: Value) -> AuthorizedRow<'r> {
        AuthorizedRow { projection: self.disclosed_row(table, row).for_query(), authority: RowAuthorization(()), expiry: Default::default() }
    }

    // read that cannot examine a partially held card is unknown, never a hole.
    // Owned events have no horizon themselves; classify their source kinds
    // from schema/key metadata even when admission prevented the lookup.
    fn point_cut(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value]) {
        if self.device {
            self.point_miss(read, key);
        }
    }

    fn point_miss(&mut self, read: &Read, key: &[Value]) {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&read.table];
        self.event_read_unknown(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
        if self.device && table.sync.as_ref().is_some_and(|s| s.horizon.is_some()) {
            self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
            self.components.note(|frame| frame.speculative = true);

                }) {
            self.unknown_fact(&read.table, &read.index, key, &read.site);
        }
    }

#[cfg(all(test, feature = "sqlite", feature = "server-search"))]
mod tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Context, Schema, rows::{MemoryRows, Rows}, schema::{ColumnDefault, ColumnType, Index}, value::object};

    #[test]
    fn trusted_search_member_prepays_missing_defaults() {
        let rows = MemoryRows::new();
        let mut table = crate::schema::public_table([
            ("id".into(),ColumnType::Id), ("payload".into(),ColumnType::Text {max:1_000_000}),
        ].into(),[("byId".into(),Index::of(&["id"],true))].into());
        table.defaults.insert("payload".into(),ColumnDefault::Literal(Value::String("x".repeat(1_000_000))));
        // Existing storage can omit a subsequently adopted default. Authority
        // is public here, so this exercises only its materialization admission.
        rows.put("records",&table,&object([("id",Value::Id("hit".into()))]),None).unwrap();
        let schema = Schema { tables:[("records".into(),table)].into(),..Default::default() };
        for heap in [64 << 10,16 << 20] {
            let mut runner = Runner::new(&rows,&schema,Context::new("alice",Default::default()),
        for hidden in [0,1000000] {
            for refuses in [false,true] {
                let mut runner=Runner::new(&rows,&schema,Context::new("alice",Default::default()),Meter::new(Ceilings::default(),Cut::Abort),0);
                let result=runner.authorized_scratch(4096,&[(MeterKind::RuleProbes,2)],|runner,_| {
                    runner.meter.heap(hidden)?;
                    if refuses { Err(RuntimeError::Input("internal test error".into())) } else { Ok(()) }
                });
                assert_eq!(result.is_err(),refuses);
                assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.heap_bytes,0);
                assert_eq!(runner.meter.heap_peak,4096);
                assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.rule_probes,2);
            }
        }
    }

    #[test]
    fn allowance_alone_decides_admission_and_every_exit_keeps_it() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let schema = Schema::default();
        for cut in [Cut::Cap, Cut::Abort] {
            for hidden_work in [0, 1, 8193] {
                for refuses in [false, true] {
                    let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &schema, Context::new("alice", Default::default()), Meter::new(Ceilings { examined: 2, rule_probes: 2, heap_bytes: 8, ..Default::default() }, cut), 0);
                    let result = runner.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, 2), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, 2)], |runner, _| {
                        runner.meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined, hidden_work)?;
                        runner.meter.charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, hidden_work)?;
                        runner.meter.heap(hidden_work * 1024)?;
                        if refuses { Err(RuntimeError::Rule { table: "private".into(), effect: crate::schema::Effect::Insert }) }
                        else { Ok(()) }
                    });
                    assert_eq!(result.is_err(), refuses);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.examined, 2);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.rule_probes, 2);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.used.heap_bytes, 0);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.heap_peak, 0);
                    assert!(runner.meter.capped_at.is_none());
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.ceilings.examined, 2);
                    assert_eq!(runner.meter.cut, cut);
                    let result = runner.authorized_scan(&[(MeterKind::Examined, 1)], |_, _| -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { panic!("admission must precede all occupant access") });
                    if cut == Cut::Abort { assert!(matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: MeterKind::Examined, actual: 3, ceiling: 2, .. }))); }
                    else { assert!(result.is_ok()); assert!(runner.meter.capped_at.is_some()); }
                }
            }
        }
    }
}

/// Standalone physical accounting has its own meter. Its token is borrowed
/// only for `run`, never constructible on a caller ledger or returned by it.
pub struct InternalLedger(crate::meter::Meter);
pub struct InternalMeter<'a>(&'a mut Meter);
impl InternalMeter<'_> {
    pub fn heap(&mut self, bytes: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { self.0.heap(bytes) }
    pub fn charge(&mut self, kind: MeterKind, amount: u64) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError> { self.0.charge(kind, amount) }
    pub fn at(&mut self, site: &str) { self.0.at(site); }
    pub fn step(&mut self, steps: u64) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> { self.0.step(steps) }
    /// Release temporary allocations without exposing the saved heap counter.
    pub fn scratch<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self) -> T) -> T {
        let mark = self.0.mark();
        let result = work(self);
        self.0.release_heap(mark);
        result
    }
    pub fn examined_room(&self, rows: usize) -> bool { self.0.room(MeterKind::Examined, rows as u64) }
    pub(crate) fn room(&self, kind: MeterKind, amount: u64) -> bool { self.0.room(kind, amount) }
    pub(crate) fn guarded(&self) -> u64 { self.0.scratch }
    pub(crate) fn add_scratch(&mut self, bytes: u64) -> u64 { self.0.scratch = self.0.scratch.saturating_add(bytes); self.0.scratch }
}
impl InternalLedger {
    pub(crate) fn with_meter<T>(meter: &mut crate::meter::Meter, work: impl FnOnce(&mut InternalMeter<'_>, &Authorization) -> T,
    ) -> T {
        work(&mut InternalMeter(meter), &Authorization::internal())
    }
    pub fn new(meter: crate::meter::Meter) -> Self { Self(meter) }
    pub fn run<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut InternalMeter<'_>, &Authorization) -> T) -> T {
        work(&mut InternalMeter(&mut self.0), &Authorization::internal())
    }
}

#[cfg(all(test,feature="sqlite"))]
mod row_view_tests {
    use super::*;
    use crate::{Context, Schema, meter::Meter, rows::MemoryRows};
    #[test]
    fn internal_work_cannot_change_reserved_receipt_or_cut() {
        let rows=MemoryRows::new(); let schema=Schema::default();
        for hidden in [0,1,17<<20] {
            for error in [false,true] {
                let mut r=Runner::new(&rows,&schema,Context::new("alice",Default::default()),Meter::new(Ceilings::default(),Cut::Cap),0);
                let out=r.authorized_scratch(8192,&[(MeterKind::Examined,1),(MeterKind::Steps,1)],|r,_| {
                    r.meter.heap(hidden)?; r.meter.charge(MeterKind::Examined,hidden)?;
                    if error {Err(RuntimeError::Input("internal test error".into()))} else {Ok(())}
                });
                assert_eq!(out.is_err(),error);
                assert_eq!(r.meter.used.examined,1); assert_eq!(r.meter.used.steps,1);
                assert_eq!(r.meter.used.heap_bytes,0); assert_eq!(r.meter.heap_peak,8192);
                assert!(r.meter.capped_at.is_none()); assert_eq!(r.meter.ceilings,Ceilings::default());
            }
        }
    }
}

    /// One fetched candidate's expiry, rule and field verdict, inside an
    /// internal scope. Unadmitted rule work is an unknown fact on a device.
    fn candidate_verdict(&mut self, scope: &super::authorized_scan::Authorization, name: &str, index: &str, prefix: &[Value], mut row: Value, trusted: bool, rule_prepaid: bool) -> Result<Option<AuthorizedRow<'r>>, RuntimeError> {
        let table = &self.schema.tables[name];
        crate::schema::materialize_optional_fields(&mut row,table);
        let provisional = self.is_provisional(name,&row)?;
        let needs_rule = !self.context.is_system() && (!trusted || provisional);
        if needs_rule && !rule_prepaid && !self.device_work_active && !scope.admit_probes(super::rules::rule_probes(&table.rules.read)) {
            // @ref LLP 3000.020#3.2 — unresolved authority is unknown, not an absent contribution.
            if self.device { self.unknown_fact(name, index, prefix, "source rule admission"); }
            return Ok(None);
        }
        if trusted && !provisional && self.schema.maintain_for(name).is_some_and(|m| m.parent_key.is_some()) {
            return Ok(Some(self.authorized_synthetic(table, row)));
        }
        self.authorize_image(name,row)
    }

    /// Cold certification has already admitted the member and entire source
    /// group, including a decoder page reserved before entering prepaid work. Every row
    /// still receives the ordinary expiry, rule and field verdict below.
    // @ref LLP 3000.015#3 — live count certification shares its transaction and authority.
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.reserved_scratch(bytes, allowance, false, work)
    }

    pub(super) fn reserved_scratch<T: Default>(&mut self, bytes: u64, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        if self.device_work_active || self.context.is_system() { return self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work); }
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(bytes)?;
        let result = self.prepaid_scan(allowance, physical, work);
        self.meter.release_row_heap(mark, bytes);
        result
    }

    pub(super) fn rule_scratch(&self, rule: &crate::schema::RuleExpr) -> u64 {
        let mut sites = Vec::new();
        super::rules::probe_sites(rule, &mut sites);
        sites.iter().map(|(table, _)| self.witness_scratch(table)).max().unwrap_or(0)
    }

    pub(super) fn witness_scratch(&self, table: &str) -> u64 {
        let definition = &self.schema.tables[table];
        let own = crate::rows::disclosed::admission(definition);
        let source = self.schema.maintain_for(table)
            .and_then(|m| self.schema.table(&m.source)).filter(|source| source.is_expiring());
        own.saturating_add(source.map_or(0, crate::rows::disclosed::admission))
    }


    /// Ordinary data reads keep their existing meter and cut semantics. This
    /// scope grants storage access without disabling or restoring any charge.
    /// Hidden authorization must use authorized_scan's prepaid reservation.
    pub(crate) fn metered_scan<T>(&mut self, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        scan(self, &Authorization::internal())
    }

    /// A rule whose logical allowance the caller has already charged, possibly
    /// to a cut, still evaluates its witnesses under the physical allowance;
    /// the caller decides what its cut means for the answer (LLP 3000.014 §5).
    pub(super) fn prepaid_witnesses<T>(&mut self, work: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || !self.context.is_system());
        let result = work(self, &Authorization::internal());
        self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
        result
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.015#2 — admission depends on declared bounds, never on
    // occupants. Private scratch cannot cut the caller's prepaid scan.
    pub(crate) fn authorized_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        self.prepaid_scan(allowance, true, scan)
    }

    /// Public or already-authorized data reads still reserve their logical
    /// meter; they do no authorization traversal. A nested scope cannot opt
    /// out of its enclosing authorization's physical allowance.
    pub(super) fn prepaid_scan<T: Default>(&mut self, allowance: &[(MeterKind, u64)], physical: bool, scan: impl FnOnce(&mut Self, &Authorization) -> Result<T, RuntimeError>,
    ) -> Result<T, RuntimeError> {
        // This meter is the device's separate physical-work budget. Its
        // caller maps exhaustion to uncertainty, never a logical E_BOUND.
        if self.device_work_active {
            let active = self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system()));
            let result = scan(self, &Authorization::internal());
            self.tx.authorizing.set(active);
            return result;
        }
        for &(kind, amount) in allowance {
            if !self.read_charge(kind, amount)? { return Ok(T::default()); }
        }
        if self.read_capped() { return Ok(T::default()); }
        let scope = Authorization { probes: Cell::new(0), remaining_probes: self.meter.ceilings.rule_probes.saturating_sub(self.meter.used.rule_probes) };
        let state = self.enter_prepaid(physical);
        let result = scan(self, &scope);
        self.leave_prepaid(state, result.as_ref().err());
        if scope.probes.get() > 0 { self.read_charge(MeterKind::RuleProbes, scope.probes.get())?; }
        result
    }

    // Kept out of the generic closure wrapper: every protected access shares
    // the same scope transition instead of instantiating its restoration code.
    #[inline(never)]
    pub(super) fn enter_prepaid(&mut self, physical: bool) -> PrepaidState {
        let nested = self.caller_heap_ceiling.is_some();
        self.caller_heap_ceiling.get_or_insert((self.meter.ceilings.heap_bytes, self.meter.used.heap_bytes));
        PrepaidState {
            mark: self.meter.mark(),
            ceilings: std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter.ceilings, Ceilings::unbounded()),
            cut: std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter.cut, Cut::Abort),
            authorizing: self.tx.authorizing.replace(self.tx.authorizing.get() || (physical && !self.context.is_system())),
            nested,
        }
    }

    #[inline(never)]
    pub(super) fn leave_prepaid(&mut self, state: PrepaidState, error: Option<&RuntimeError>) {
        self.tx.authorizing.set(state.authorizing);
        self.meter.restore(state.mark);
        self.meter.ceilings = state.ceilings;
        self.meter.cut = state.cut;
        if !state.nested { self.caller_heap_ceiling = None; }
        if let Some(RuntimeError::Bound { kind: MeterKind::HeapBytes, site, .. }) = error {
            self.meter.capped_at.get_or_insert((MeterKind::HeapBytes, site.clone()));
        }
    }

}

#[cfg(all(test, feature = "sqlite"))]

    #[test]
    fn job_authority_edges_clear_raw_cache_but_keep_the_allowance() {
        let mut store = Store::memory().unwrap();
        let tx = store.transaction().unwrap();
        let schema = Schema::default();
        let mut runner = Runner::new(&tx, &schema, Context::system(), Meter::new(Ceilings::job(), Cut::Abort), 0);
        runner.tx.remaining.set(17);
        *runner.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some(("t".into(), "byId".into(), vec![], None));
        let child = runner.job_read_child(Context::new("user:alice", Default::default()));
        assert!(child.tx.lookup_cache.borrow().is_none());
        assert_eq!(child.tx.remaining(), 17);
        child.tx.remaining.set(13);
        *child.tx.lookup_cache.borrow_mut() = Some(("t".into(), "byId".into(), vec![], None));
        runner.finish_job_read_child(child);
        assert!(runner.tx.lookup_cache.borrow().is_none());
        assert_eq!(runner.tx.remaining(), 13);
    }

    fn absence(condition: &Expr, inverted: bool) -> Vec<ReadExpr> {
        match condition {
            Expr::Exists { read, .. } => {
                let mut out = if inverted { vec![read.clone()] } else { vec![] };
                out.extend(absence(&Expr::Read(read.clone()), false)); out
            },
            Expr::Unary { op, value, .. } if op == "not" => absence(value, !inverted),
            Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } if op == "=" || op == "!=" => {
                let pair = match (left.as_ref(), right.as_ref()) {
                    (value, Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(b), .. }) | (Expr::Literal { value: Literal::Bool(b), .. }, value) => Some((value, *b)),
                    _ => None,
                };
                pair.map(|(value, b)| absence(value, inverted ^ (op == "=" && !b || op == "!=" && b))).unwrap_or_else(|| absence(left, inverted).into_iter().chain(absence(right, inverted)).collect())
            }
            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => absence(left, inverted).into_iter().chain(absence(right, inverted)).collect(),
            Expr::If { then, condition, otherwise, .. } => {
                let mut all = Vec::new(); let mut copy = (**condition).clone();
                super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if let Expr::Exists { read, .. } = expr { all.push(read.clone()); } });
                all.extend(absence(then, inverted)); all.extend(absence(otherwise, inverted)); all
            }
            Expr::Unary { value, .. } | Expr::Member { base: value, .. } | Expr::Assert { value, .. }
                | Expr::ShapeUse { base: value, .. } | Expr::Walk { seed: value, .. } => absence(value, inverted),
            Expr::Array { items, .. } | Expr::Call { args: items, .. } => items.iter().flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect(),
            Expr::Object { fields, .. } => fields.iter().filter_map(|field| field.value.as_ref()).flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect(),
            Expr::Shape { base, fields, .. } => absence(base, inverted).into_iter().chain(fields.iter().filter_map(|field| match field {
                ShapeField::Field { value, .. } => Some(value), _ => None,
            }).flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted))).collect(),
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Created { read, .. } => {
                let mut values: Vec<_> = read.keys.iter().map(|key| &key.value).chain(read.seeks.iter().map(|key| &key.value)).collect();
                values.extend(read.filter.as_deref()); values.extend(read.matching.as_deref());
                values.extend(read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_deref()));
                values.into_iter().flat_map(|value| absence(value, false)).collect()
            },
            Expr::Write { keys, fields, condition, .. } => keys.iter().map(|key| &key.value)
                .chain(fields.iter().filter_map(|field| field.value.as_ref())).chain(condition.as_deref())
                .flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect(),
            Expr::Merge(merge) => {
                let mut values: Vec<Expr> = [&merge.matching, &merge.filter, &merge.cursor].into_iter().filter_map(|value| value.as_deref().cloned()).collect();
                for lane in &merge.lanes {
                    values.extend(lane.admission.as_deref().cloned()); values.extend(lane.distinct.clone());
                    values.extend(lane.prefix.iter().cloned()); values.extend(lane.loop_.as_ref().map(|(_, value)| value.clone()));
                    for read in lane.planned.iter().chain(lane.nulls.as_ref().map(|(_, read)| read)) { values.push(Expr::Read(*read.clone())); }
                    if let Some(fields) = &lane.project { values.extend(fields.iter().filter_map(|field| if let ShapeField::Field { value, .. } = field { Some(value.clone()) } else { None })); }
                }
                values.iter().flat_map(|value| absence(value, inverted)).collect()
            },
            Expr::Name { .. } | Expr::Focal { .. } | Expr::Literal { .. } => vec![],
        }
    }
    // Reverse dependency closure identifies query values consumed by guards,
    // across aliases/objects and all possible rebinding paths. The observation
    // is checked at its original statement, with that statement's authority.
    fn has_guard(catalog: &Catalog, statements: &[Stmt], visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
        if statements.iter().any(|stmt| matches!(stmt, Stmt::Require { .. } | Stmt::If { .. })) { return true; }
        let mut copy = analysis_body(statements); let mut found = false;
        super::intent_walk::statements(&mut copy, &mut |expr| {
            if matches!(expr, Expr::If { .. }) { found = true; }
            for (name, shape) in definitions(catalog, expr) {
                let key = format!("{shape}:{name}");
                if !visiting.insert(key.clone()) { found = true; continue; }
                found |= helper_body(catalog, &name, shape).is_some_and(|body| has_guard(catalog, &body, visiting));
                visiting.remove(&key);
            }
        });
        // Explicit guards can also be nested in loops/viewer scopes.
        found || statements.iter().any(|stmt| match stmt { Stmt::For { body, .. } | Stmt::As { body, .. } => has_guard(catalog, body, visiting), _ => false })
    }
    fn guarded_inputs(catalog: &Catalog, expr: &Expr) -> Vec<Expr> {
//! Developer-owned, in-memory refusal context. Never part of Refusal or receipts.
// @ref LLP 3000.015#2.1 — only a host opt-in can collect the private refusing clause.
use super::runner::Runner;
use crate::{RuntimeError, schema::Effect};

#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
#[derive(Clone, Debug, serde::Serialize)]
pub struct RefusingClause {
    pub table: String,
    pub viewer: String,
    pub clause: String,
    pub phase: String,
    pub terminal: bool,
    pub no_candidate: bool,
    pub site: String,
    pub generation: u64,
}
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default)]
pub struct RefusalTrace(std::sync::Arc<std::sync::Mutex<Option<RefusingClause>>>, std::sync::Arc<std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64>);
#[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
impl RefusalTrace {
    /// Physical row images encountered and filtered by read authority in this request.
    pub fn hidden(&self) -> u64 { self.1.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed) }
    pub fn take(&self) -> Option<RefusingClause> { self.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).take() }
}
impl Runner<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.031#1.2 — only a real, uncapped storage miss; never replace a clause.
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    pub(super) fn no_candidate(&self, table: &str, site: &str) {
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace {
            trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()).get_or_insert_with(|| RefusingClause {
                table: table.into(), viewer: self.context.viewer.clone(), clause: "no-candidate".into(), phase: "storage probe".into(),
                terminal: false, no_candidate: true, site: site.into(), generation: self.context.generation,
            });
        }
    }
    pub(super) fn refusing_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str) {
        self.refusing_phase(table, clause, clause);
    }
    pub(super) fn filtered_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str) {
        self.filtered_phase(table, clause, clause);
    }
    pub(super) fn filtered_phase(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str) {
        self.record_clause(table, clause, phase, false);
    }
    pub(super) fn refusing_phase(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str) {
        self.record_clause(table, clause, phase, true);
    }
    fn record_clause(&self, table: &str, clause: &str, phase: &str, terminal: bool) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace {
            *trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()) = Some(RefusingClause {
                table: table.into(), viewer: self.context.viewer.clone(), clause: clause.into(), phase: phase.into(), terminal, no_candidate: false, site: self.statement_site.clone(), generation: self.context.generation,
            });
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (table, clause, phase, terminal);
    }
    pub(super) fn trace_rule_result(&self, result: &Result<bool, RuntimeError>, table: &str, effect: Effect) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.is_some() {
            let phase = format!("{effect} rule");
            if matches!(result, Ok(false)) { self.record_clause(table, &phase, &phase, effect != Effect::Read); }
            self.trace_authorization(result, table, effect, &phase);
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (result, table, effect);
    }
    /// Preserve the phase before enclosing writes remap the public error effect.
    pub(super) fn trace_authorization<T>(&self, result: &Result<T, RuntimeError>, table: &str, effect: Effect, phase: &str) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.is_some() && matches!(result, Err(RuntimeError::AuthorizationBudget { .. })) {
            self.refusing_phase(table, &format!("authorization allowance ({effect})"), phase);
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (result, table, effect, phase);
    }
    pub(super) fn trace_unlocated_budget(&self, table: &str, effect: Effect) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if self.context.refusal_trace.as_ref().is_some_and(|trace| trace.0.lock().unwrap_or_else(|e|e.into_inner()).as_ref().is_none_or(|cause| !cause.clause.starts_with("authorization allowance"))) {
            self.refusing_phase(table, &format!("authorization allowance ({effect})"), "authorization access");
        }
        #[cfg(not(feature = "sqlite"))]
        let _ = (table, effect);
    }
    pub(super) fn hidden_row(&self) {
        #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
        if let Some(trace) = &self.context.refusal_trace { trace.1.fetch_add(1, std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Relaxed); }
    }
    pub(super) fn rule_refusal(&self, table: &str, effect: Effect, clause: &str) -> RuntimeError {
        self.refusing_clause(table, clause);
        RuntimeError::Rule { table: table.into(), effect }
    }

    78	    pub(super) fn seek(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<Option<ir::Range>> {
    79	        if read.seeks.is_empty() { return Ok(None); }
    80	        let mut range = ir::Range { gt: None, gte: None, lt: None, lte: None };
    81	        for bound in &read.seeks {
    82	            if index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) != Some(&bound.column) || read.matching.is_some() {

    84	            }
    85	            let expected = self.key_ty(info, index, &bound.column);
    86	            let (value, ty) = self.field_value(&ObjectField { spread: false, name: bound.column.clone(), value: Some(bound.value.clone()), site: bound.site.clone() }, &expected, out)?;
    87	            if !ty.comparable(&expected) || ty.is_optional() {
    88	                let endpoint = print::expression(&bound.value);
    89	                let rewrite = if ty.is_optional() {
    90	                    format!("put the read inside `if {endpoint} != null:`, then `{} where .{} {} {endpoint}`", read.table, bound.column, bound.op)
    91	                } else {
    92	                    format!("use a present {} endpoint in `{} where .{} {} {endpoint}`", expected.required().name(), read.table, bound.column, bound.op)
    93	                };
    94	                return Err(type_error(&bound.site, format!("seek on {}.{} expects present {}, got {}", read.table, bound.column, expected.required().name(), ty.name()), rewrite));
    95	            }
    96	            let slot = match bound.op.as_str() { ">" => &mut range.gt, ">=" => &mut range.gte, "<" => &mut range.lt, "<=" => &mut range.lte, _ => unreachable!("parser seek comparison") };
    97	            if slot.is_some() { return Err(type_error(&bound.site, "a seek bound is repeated", "write one lower and one upper bound")); }
    98	            *slot = Some(Box::new(self.snapshot(value, out)));
    99	        }
   100	        if (range.gt.is_some() && range.gte.is_some()) || (range.lt.is_some() && range.lte.is_some()) {
   101	            return Err(type_error(&read.site, "a seek has at most one lower and one upper bound", "t[prefix, col >= start, col < end] first 50 by index"));
   102	        }

   122	    pub(super) fn page_shape(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<A> {

   124	        let (base, name, site) = trailing_shape(predicate)?;

   139	    pub(super) fn scan_filter(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl) -> Fallible<(ReadExpr, Option<Box<Expr>>)> {
   140	        let mut read = read.clone();
   141	        let Some(mut predicate) = read.filter.take() else { return Ok((read, None)); };
   142	        // Planning knows parameter declarations; binding also knows helper,
   143	        // local and lane selectors. Only present, infallible endpoints may
   144	        // leave the short-circuit predicate in either pass.
   145	        let required = self.scopes.iter().flat_map(|scope| scope.keys())
   146	            .filter(|name| self.binding(name).is_some_and(|binding| !binding.ty.is_optional()))
   147	            .cloned().collect();
   148	        if !read.order_keys.is_empty() && read.matching.is_none() {
   149	            let info = self.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
   150	            let mut parts = Vec::new(); super::super::intent::split(*predicate, &mut parts);
   151	            let mut residual = Vec::new();
   152	            for part in parts {
   153	                let candidate = super::super::intent::comparison(&part).map(|(column, op, value)| (column, vec![op], value))
   154	                    .or_else(|| super::super::intent::equality(&part, info)
   155	                        .filter(|(column, _)| !self.key_ty(info, index, column).is_optional())
   156	                        .map(|(column, value)| (column, vec![">=".into(), "<=".into()], value)));

   158	                    && index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) == Some(&column)
   159	                    && super::super::intent_aggregates::safe_endpoint(&value, &required)

   161	                    for op in ops { read.seeks.push(ast::SeekBound { column: column.clone(), op, value: value.clone(), site: part.site().clone() }); }

   163	            }

   165	            predicate = remaining;
   166	        }
   167	        // A single next-column comparison is the same seek as its key spelling.
   168	        if read.seeks.is_empty() && read.matching.is_none()
   169	            && let A::Binary { op, left, right, site } = predicate.as_ref()
   170	            && matches!(op.as_str(), ">" | ">=" | "<" | "<=")

//! Job viewer scopes and calls to the same declared queries used by clients.
use super::*;

pub(super) fn diagnostic(site: &Site, code: &str, message: &str, rewrite: &str) -> Diagnostic {
    Diagnostic::new(site, code, message, rewrite).guide("jobs")
}
pub(super) fn read_only(site: &Site) -> Diagnostic {
    diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_READ_ONLY", "as is read-only: writes, emits, new ids and once asset consumption have side effects", "compute inside as; act outside the block")
}

impl Binder<'_> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#21.12 — viewer scopes are read-only, under the job's transaction and meter.
    pub(super) fn as_viewer(&mut self, principal: &A, body: &[S], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<()> {
        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Job || self.in_prelude || self.loops + self.caller_loops == 0 || self.viewer_depth.is_some() {
            return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_CONTEXT", "as belongs inside a job sweep and cannot nest", "put as recipient.principal: inside for recipient in recipients:"));
        }
        let (value, ty) = self.expr(principal, out)?;
        if !matches!(ty, Ty::Principal) && !matches!(principal, A::Literal { value: Literal::Str(s), .. } if snapback4_core::value::identity_fits(s) && s != snapback4_core::SYSTEM_PRINCIPAL) {
            return Err(diagnostic(principal.site(), "E_JOB_VIEWER_PRINCIPAL", "as requires a present non-system principal", "use a principal column or argument; keep intentional system reads outside as"));
        }
        let loops = self.loops;
        self.loops = 0;
        self.caller_loops += loops;
        self.clear_authority_refinements();
        self.viewer_depth = Some(self.depth);
        let mut code = setup::Body::default();
        let result = self.block(body, &mut code, false);
        self.clear_authority_refinements();
        self.viewer_depth = None;
        self.loops = loops;
        self.caller_loops -= loops;
        result?;
        out.push(Stmt::Let { name: self.temp("as"), value: Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::JobAs, args: vec![value, Expr::Block { body: code.code, value: Box::new(ir::lit(Value::Null)) }] } });
        Ok(())
    }

    // @ref LLP 3000.007#21.12 — calls are reevaluated under the new viewer,
    // including implicit/transitive helper dependencies. Snapshot fields stay
    // valid. Conservatively discard call, point and maintained-authorization
    // facts at both authority edges.
    fn clear_authority_refinements(&mut self) {
        self.call_types.clear();
        for scope in &mut self.scopes {
            for binding in scope.values_mut() {
                binding.fields.retain(|path, _| path.first().is_none_or(|part| {
                    part != "$pureCall"
                        && part != "$point"
                        && part != maintain_types::AUTHORIZATION
                }));
            }
        }
    }

    pub(super) fn job_query_call(&mut self, name: &str, args: &[A], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Option<Fallible<(Expr, Ty)>> {
        if self.viewer_depth.is_none() || self.catalog.functions.contains_key(name) { return None; }
        let all = name == "$jobAll" && self.viewer_depth.is_some();
        let (name, args) = if all {
            match args { [A::Call { name, args, .. }] => (name.as_str(), args.as_slice()), _ => return Some(Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "all takes a declared paged query call", "for row in all(unreadPreview(periodStart, periodEnd, null)):"))) }
        } else { (name, args) };
        if name == "all" && self.viewer_depth.is_some() { return Some(Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "all is a streaming for domain", "for row in all(queryName(..., null)):"))); }
        if self.viewer_depth.is_some() && matches!(name, "consume" | "consumeOnce") { return Some(Err(read_only(site))); }
        let caller_module = std::path::Path::new(&site.file).file_stem().and_then(|s| s.to_str()).unwrap_or("schema");
        let (module, decl) = self.catalog.documents.iter().find_map(|doc| {
            let module = std::path::Path::new(&doc.file).file_stem()?.to_str()?;
            doc.programs.iter().find(|decl| super::super::operation_name(module, &decl.name) == name || module == caller_module && decl.name == name).map(|decl| (module.to_owned(), decl.clone()))
        })?;
        Some((|| {
            if self.viewer_depth.is_none() || decl.kind != ProgramKind::Query || decl.native || decl.held {
                return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "only declared Quarry queries may be called inside a job as block", "compute with a query inside as; call mutations, actions and native operations outside it"));
            }
            let mut diagnostics = Vec::new();
            let query = Binder::bind(self.catalog, self.schema, &module, &decl, &mut diagnostics)
                .ok_or_else(|| diagnostics.into_iter().next().unwrap_or_else(|| diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "query could not be bound", "fix the declared query first")))?;
            if snapback4_core::ir::read_sites(&query.program).iter().any(|read| self.schema.table(&read.table).is_some_and(|table| table.ephemeral.is_some())) {
                return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "job viewer queries read durable data", "keep ephemeral reads in a client query; persist the state the job needs"));
            }
            if args.len() != query.args.len() { return Err(type_error(site, format!("{name} takes {} arguments", query.args.len()), "pass the same positional arguments as the declared query, including its cursor")); }
            let mut fields = BTreeMap::new();
            for ((name, expected), given) in query.args.iter().zip(args) {
                let (value, ty) = if expected.required().is_identity() { self.expr_as_reference(given, out, expected)? } else { self.expr(given, out)? };
                if !ty.assignable_to(expected) { return Err(type_error(given.site(), format!("{name} requires {}, got {}", expected.name(), ty.name()), "pass the query's declared argument type")); }
                fields.insert(name.clone(), value);
            }
            let cursors: Vec<_> = query.args.iter().filter(|(_, ty)| ty.required() == Ty::Cursor).map(|(name, _)| name.clone()).collect();
            let cursor = (cursors.len() == 1).then(|| cursors[0].clone());
            let ty = if all {
                let Some((item, _)) = query.result.sequence() else { return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "all needs a query returning a row list", "return the paged list from the query")); };
                if cursor.is_none() || snapback4_core::engine::drain_cursor(&query.program) != cursor { return Err(diagnostic(site, "E_JOB_VIEWER_QUERY", "all needs one cursor argument that owns the returned page", "declare c: cursor ? and return a page after c; start with null")); }
                Ty::List(Box::new(item), u32::MAX)
            } else { Ty::Object(BTreeMap::from([("data".into(), query.result), ("next".into(), Ty::Cursor.optional())])) };
            Ok((Expr::Builtin { builtin: Builtin::JobQuery { program: Box::new(query.program), cursor, all }, args: vec![Expr::Object(fields)] }, ty))
        })())
    }
}
| Snapback 4 0.2.6, 2026-09-13 (first green) | 472 | 652 | 854 | 338 | 588 | 783 | 804 |

Read as shape: where the declarative model fits the milestone — accounts,
posts, media, ephemeral content, offline writes — Snapback 4 is already
at or under Convex. Where it lost, the causes are on record:

- **M8 on 0.1.0 (2,945 s):** `sum(page, .x)` refused as documented, a bare
  `return` refused, `/` found only through its diagnostic, and a digest
  written as a job over a `digestWork` table because a loop could not
  carry a total ("more ceremony than writing the equivalent arithmetic
  in a familiar language or expressing it as SQL" — the builder's
  essay). On 0.2.6, with the guide compiled and the `feeds`/`jobs` cards,
  the same builder's M8 first green was 804 s: the discovery tax was
  paid by documentation, the ceremony remained.
- **M3 on both runs (716, 854 s against 304):** four parallel logins
  against the bounded Argon2 admission, a navigation race, a wasm URL
  not durably available during an offline reload, module operation
  naming (`api.chat.send`), an upsert that replaced an avatar. Client
  lifecycle and harness, not Quarry.
- **M4 on 0.2.6:** the documented `{ id }` video argument fails local
  prediction with `E_FIELD` while the same wire mutation succeeds
  (`issues/20260913-snapback4-video-id-only-prediction-refuses.md`).

### 2.2 Correction loops, by cause

The 0.2.6 Astra lane recorded 25 loops: 2 Snapback defects, 23 agent.
Grok recorded a capability failure at M3 and Muse was at M1 when this
revision was written. Reading every lane's loops by cause rather than by
the lane's honest self-blame:

| Cause | Astra 0.2.6 | Grok 0.2.6 | Muse 0.2.6 (M1, in flight) | Also |
  seven days. Refresh replaces the presented session, logout-all revokes every
  session for its principal, and expired sessions are pruned. Change polls and
  effect claims reauthenticate after waiting before observing or claiming new
  work. The web client
  keeps credentials in memory by default, exposes localStorage only as an
  explicit compatibility store, and refreshes a near-expiry session before it
  becomes unusable.

- **2026-09-13 — hidden rows stay hidden at write and replay boundaries
  (security review).** A unique conflict with a row the caller cannot read is
  the generic rule refusal. Audience insertion is admitted only when the
  caller already has the matching grant; audience keys cannot be changed to
  acquire one. The private mutation log may retain old operator data, but sync
  catch-up re-evaluates the current row and emits only its current readable
  image, or a deletion for a row that disappeared or expired. Previously
  delivered device data remains a property of that device.

- **2026-10-12 — a third asset kind, `file`, under the same contract
  (author, approving LLP 3000.038).** A document column (`file <=N`, with an
  optional allow-list of media types Snapback can verify) keeps one home,
  serving by the home row's read rule and the release/sweep lifetime above.
  The upload declares its type and filename (`?as=file&name=`); the server
  checks the declaration against the bytes and never stores a script-bearing
  type. A file is only ever served as an attachment (`nosniff`, a
  sandboxing CSP); `once` does not apply; nothing is transcoded.

- **2026-09-13 — assets are staged, bounded and viewer-private (security
  review).** Server IDs use operating-system or Web Crypto entropy. Upload
  bytes and asset deletion run outside the engine mutex, with bounded slots
