    fn fact_text(value: &Expr) -> String {
        let mut value = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut value, &mut |expr| {
            if is_viewer(expr) { *expr = Expr::Name { name: "viewer".into(), site: expr.site().clone() }; }
        });
        crate::print::expression(&value)
    }
    fn facts(condition: &Expr, out: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = condition && op == "and" { facts(left, out); facts(right, out); }
        else { out.insert(fact_text(condition)); }
    }
    fn readable(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, read: &ReadExpr, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
        let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return true };
        if super::owner_equality::readable(catalog, schema, read, known) { return true; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return table.rules.read.is_uniformly_readable(); };
        let mut pinned = BTreeMap::new();
        // Unlike older advisory lints, proof cannot guess a non-id key: typed ids
        // may select byId even beside one same-width principal/composite key.
        let selected = read.index.as_ref().and_then(|name| info.indexes.iter().find(|index| &index.name == name));
        let inferred: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()).collect();
        let positional_index = if read.index.is_some() { selected.filter(|index| index.is_plain()) }
            else { match inferred.as_slice() { [index] => Some(*index), _ => None } };
        if let Some(index) = positional_index {
            for (column, key) in index.columns.iter().zip(&read.keys) {
                pinned.insert(key.column.clone().unwrap_or_else(|| column.clone()), key.value.clone());
            }
        }
        // Extra named tests pin fields even when the positional prefix is unresolved.
        if selected.is_none_or(|index| index.computed.is_empty()) {
            for part in &read.keys { if let Some(column) = &part.column { pinned.insert(column.clone(), part.value.clone()); } }
        }
        if read.index.is_none() && read.keys.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).count() == 1 && read.keys.first().is_some_and(|part| part.column.is_none() && is_viewer(&part.value)) {
            let principal_keys: Vec<_> = table.indexes.values().filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns().len() == 1
                && table.columns.get(&index.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).collect();
            if let [index] = principal_keys.as_slice() { pinned.insert(index.columns()[0].clone(), read.keys[0].value.clone()); }
        }
        let mut filters = Vec::new();
        if let Some(filter) = &read.filter { super::intent::split((**filter).clone(), &mut filters); }
        for filter in filters {
            if let Expr::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = &filter && op == "="
                && let Some(column) = super::intent::column(left) { pinned.entry(column.to_owned()).or_insert_with(|| (**right).clone()); }
        }
        // A total self-membership key is witnessed by the probed row itself.
        if table.rules.read.disjuncts().iter().any(|rule| {
            let RuleExpr::Exists { table: target, index, key, equalities, .. } = rule else { return false };
            target == &read.table && equalities.is_empty() && table.index(index).is_some_and(|index|
                index.unique && index.columns().len() == key.len() && index.columns().iter().zip(key).all(|(column, value)| {
                    let required = column == "id" || table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|kind| !matches!(kind, snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Optional(_)));
                    required && (matches!(value, RuleTerm::Field(field) if field == column)
                        || matches!(value, RuleTerm::Viewer) && pinned.get(column).is_some_and(is_viewer))
                }))
        }) { return true; }
        let term = |term: &RuleTerm| -> Option<String> {
            match term {
                RuleTerm::Viewer => Some("viewer".into()),
                RuleTerm::Literal(value) => Some(RuleExpr::Eq(RuleTerm::Viewer, RuleTerm::Literal(value.clone())).quarry().trim_start_matches("viewer = ").to_owned()),
                RuleTerm::Field(column) => pinned.get(column).map(fact_text),
                _ => None,
            }
        };
        fn holds(rule: &RuleExpr, term: &impl Fn(&RuleTerm) -> Option<String>, schema: &Schema, known: &BTreeSet<String>) -> bool {
            match rule {
                RuleExpr::Allow | RuleExpr::Public { .. } => true,
                RuleExpr::Eq(a, b) => match (term(a), term(b)) {
                    (Some(a), Some(b)) => a == b || known.contains(&format!("({a} = {b})")) || known.contains(&format!("({b} = {a})")),
                    _ => false,
                },
                RuleExpr::And(items) => items.iter().all(|rule| holds(rule, term, schema, known)),
                RuleExpr::Or(items) => items.iter().any(|rule| holds(rule, term, schema, known)),
                RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. } => {
                    let Some(target) = schema.table(table).and_then(|t| t.index(index)) else { return false };
                    let Some(key) = key.iter().map(term).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { return false };
                    let plain = format!("exists {table}[{}]", key.join(", "));
                    let Some(extra) = equalities.iter().map(|(c, t)| term(t).map(|v| format!("{c} = {v}"))).collect::<Option<Vec<_>>>() else { return false };
                    let named = target.columns().iter().zip(&key).map(|(c, v)| format!("{c} = {v}"))
                        .chain(extra).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
                    // Positional spellings with multiple compatible index widths
                    // are not evidence for this particular raw-rule key.
                    let source = schema.table(table).unwrap();
                    let unambiguous = source.indexes.values().filter(|candidate| candidate.unique && candidate.columns().len() == key.len()).count() == 1
                        || key.len() == 1 && key[0] == "viewer" && target.columns().len() == 1
                            && source.columns.get(&target.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)
                            && source.indexes.values().filter(|candidate| candidate.unique && candidate.columns().len() == 1
                                && source.columns.get(&candidate.columns()[0]) == Some(&snapback4_core::schema::ColumnType::Principal)).count() == 1;
                    (equalities.is_empty() && unambiguous && known.contains(&plain)) || known.contains(&format!("exists {table}[{named}]"))
                },
                _ => false,
            }
        }
        holds(&table.rules.read, &term, schema, known)
    }
    fn guard(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, condition: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        if system { return; }
    }
    fn lookup_fact(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, names: &[String], value: &Expr, known: &mut BTreeSet<String>) {
        let mut shadowed = false;
        let mut copy = analysis_expr(value);
        super::intent_walk::expression(&mut copy, &mut |expr| { if matches!(expr, Expr::Name { name, .. } if names.contains(name)) { shadowed = true; } });
        if shadowed { return; }
        let Expr::Assert { value, .. } = value else { return };
        let Expr::Read(read) = value.as_ref() else { return };
        if schema.maintain_for(&read.table).is_some() || !point_probe(catalog, read) { return; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        let mut indexes: Vec<_> = info.indexes.iter().filter(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) } else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() }).collect();
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && read.keys.len() == 1 && is_viewer(&read.keys[0].value) {
            indexes.retain(|index| info.columns.get(&index.columns[0]).is_some_and(|kind| kind.kind == TypeKind::Principal));
        }
        if !named && read.index.is_none() && super::owner_equality::typed_id(catalog, read) { indexes.retain(|index| index.name == "byId"); }
        let [index] = indexes.as_slice() else { return };
        let keys = index.columns.iter().enumerate().filter_map(|(at, column)| {
            let key = if named { read.keys.iter().find(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column)) } else { read.keys.get(at) }?;
            Some(format!("{column} = {}", fact_text(&key.value)))
        }).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        // A successful ruled row lookup proves the indexed row exists. Extra
        // projected-field tests are not raw rule evidence and are deliberately omitted.
        known.insert(format!("exists {}[{keys}]", read.table));
    }
    fn point_probe(catalog: &Catalog, read: &ReadExpr) -> bool {
        if read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() { return false; }
        let Some(info) = catalog.tables.get(&read.table) else { return false };
        let named = read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_some());
        info.indexes.iter().any(|index| index.unique && index.is_plain() && read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|name| name == &index.name)
            && if named { index.columns.iter().all(|column| read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.as_ref() == Some(column))) }
                else { index.columns.len() == read.keys.len() })
    }
    fn expression_guard_node(catalog: &Catalog, schema: &Schema, program: &str, expr: &Expr, known: &BTreeSet<String>, global: &BTreeSet<String>, system: bool, observe_prefix: bool, visiting: &mut BTreeSet<String>, notes: &mut Vec<Diagnostic>) {
        if !observe_prefix {
            for input in guarded_inputs(catalog, expr) {
                input_guards(catalog, schema, program, &input, known, global, system, visiting, notes);
            }
        }
        if let Expr::Exists { read, site } = expr && (observe_prefix || point_probe(catalog, read)) {
            guard(catalog, schema, program, &Expr::Exists { read: read.clone(), site: site.clone() }, known, system, notes);
        }
        if let Expr::If { condition, .. } = expr {
10:        if !read.order_keys.is_empty() || read.range.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.keys.is_empty() { return Ok(true); }
17:        match probe.select_index(info, &lookup, point, &mut setup::Body::default()) {
25:                match probe.select_index(info, read, ReadMode::Range, &mut setup::Body::default()) {
93:        if !read.seeks.is_empty() && read.order_keys.is_empty() && !matches!(position, Position::Aggregate | Position::Exists) && !(position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job) {
//! One recognition of point keys versus prefix populations, shared by reads,
//! loop domains, merge discovery and program existence tests.
use super::*;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub(super) enum Position { Page, Domain, Aggregate, Exists }

impl Binder<'_> {
    pub(super) fn is_page(&self, read: &ReadExpr, point: ReadMode) -> Fallible<bool> {
        if !read.order_keys.is_empty() || read.range.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.keys.is_empty() { return Ok(true); }
        // Speculation cannot emit setup or consume compiler names. The selected
        // form binds its key expressions exactly once in the caller's body.
        let mut probe = self.clone();
        let info = probe.table(&read.table, &read.site)?;
        let mut lookup = read.clone();
        if point == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.maintained.is_some() && lookup.index.is_none() { lookup.index = Some("byGroup".into()); }
        match probe.select_index(info, &lookup, point, &mut setup::Body::default()) {
            Ok(_) => Ok(false),
            Err(error) if error.code == "E_QUARRY_AMBIGUOUS" => Err(error),
            Err(point_error) => {
                // Only an actual index prefix changes the form. A mistyped
                // full key or an uncovered named equality keeps its point
                // diagnostic, including the established repair.
                let mut probe = self.clone();
                match probe.select_index(info, read, ReadMode::Range, &mut setup::Body::default()) {
                    Ok(_) => Ok(true),
                    Err(error) if error.code == "E_QUARRY_AMBIGUOUS" => Err(error),
                    Err(error) if error.code == "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN" => Err(error),
                    Err(_) => Err(point_error),
                }
            },
        }
    }

    pub(super) fn prefix_bound(&self, read: &ReadExpr, index: &ast::IndexDecl, position: Position) -> Fallible<(ast::RangeSpec, Bound)> {
        if let Some(range) = &read.range { return Ok((range.clone(), Bound::Literal(range.take))); }
        let ceiling = if self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job { snapback4_core::Ceilings::job() } else { snapback4_core::Ceilings::default() }.examined;
        let pinned = &index.columns[..read.keys.len()];
        // One group per distinct source key: a parent-scoped source cap bounds
        // its aggregate rows too, without copying a constraint onto the view.
        // A pinned unique key bounds the group as a cap does (wave 3 #7); `.id = x`
        // plans as the closed seek `.id >= x and .id <= x` on the next column.
        let mut equal = pinned.to_vec();
        if let Some(next) = index.columns.get(read.keys.len()) {
            let printed = |op: &str| read.seeks.iter().filter(|seek| &seek.column == next && seek.op == op).map(|seek| crate::print::expression(&seek.value)).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            let (low, high) = (printed(">="), printed("<="));
            if read.seeks.len() == 2 && low.len() == 1 && low == high { equal.push(next.clone()); }
        }
        let cap = self.schema.group_bound(&read.table, pinned).into_iter().chain(crate::bind::unique_bound::unique_bound(self.schema, &read.table, &equal)).min().or_else(|| {
            let m = self.schema.maintain_for(&read.table)?;
            let parent = m.parent_key.as_ref()?;
            pinned.contains(&parent.reference).then(|| self.schema.group_bound(&m.source, &[parent.reference.clone()])).flatten()
        });
        let bound = if position == Position::Exists { Bound::Literal(1) }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.22 — ruling 24: the schema cap supplies the whole group's ordinary literal scan bound.
        // Queries with a non-uniform rule keep this existing capped path;
        // scan_at upgrades uniformly decided aggregates to streaming below.
        else if position == Position::Aggregate && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Query && let Some(cap) = cap.filter(|cap| u64::from(*cap) <= ceiling) { Bound::Literal(cap) }
        else if position == Position::Aggregate || (position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job) { Bound::Meter }
        else if let Some(cap) = cap.filter(|cap| u64::from(*cap) <= ceiling) { Bound::Literal(cap) }
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.22 — ruling 23: only query loop and lane domains may consume a prefix under the examined meter.
        else if position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Query { Bound::Meter }
        else {
            let columns = if pinned.is_empty() { String::new() } else { format!(" by {}", pinned.join(", ")) };
            let mut page = read.clone();
            page.index = Some(index.name.clone());
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.3 — a synced table's horizon keeps the newest rows, so its page reads newest first:
            // the page rewrite and N_NO_HORIZON's window then agree, and applying both draws no N_SYNC_ORDER.
            let window = self.schema.table(&read.table).and_then(|table| table.sync.as_ref()).map(|sync| sync.horizon.as_ref().map(|window| window.by.clone()));
            if let Some(Some(by)) = &window
                && let Some(horizon) = self.catalog.tables.get(&read.table).and_then(|info| info.indexes.iter().find(|candidate| &candidate.name == by))
                && horizon.words.is_none() && horizon.columns.len() > pinned.len() && horizon.columns[..pinned.len()] == *pinned {
                page.index = Some(horizon.name.clone());
            }
            let order = if window.is_some() { ast::Order::Desc } else { ast::Order::Asc };
            page.range = Some(ast::RangeSpec { order, take: 50, cursor: Some(Box::new(A::Name { name: "c".into(), site: read.site.clone() })), site: read.site.clone() });
            let suggestion = format!("page with `{} N` and a cursor: `{}` (declare `c: cursor ?`)", if window.is_some() { "last" } else { "first" }, self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(page.clone())));
            // A filter pinning a whole unique key names at most one row: offer the one-row read first.
            let one = (index.unique && index.words.is_none() && !pinned.is_empty() && pinned.len() == index.columns.len()).then(|| {
                let mut one = page.clone();
                one.range = Some(ast::RangeSpec { order: ast::Order::Asc, take: 1, cursor: None, site: read.site.clone() });
                format!("`unique {}` makes this at most one row: `{}`; or ", pinned.join(", "), self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(one)))
            }).unwrap_or_default();
            let rewrite = if cap.is_none() { format!("{one}declare `cap N{columns}` on {}, or {suggestion}", read.table) } else { suggestion };
            let message = match cap {
                Some(cap) => format!("{}'s whole group has cap {cap}, above the {ceiling} examined ceiling; one read cannot examine the whole group", read.table),
                None => format!("{} has no cap over this prefix; this position needs a bounded page", read.table),
            };
            return Err(if position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Query {
                placement(&read.site, format!("{message}; meter-bounded prefix loops and lane sources belong in queries"), rewrite).guide("bounds")
            } else { Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_BOUND", message, rewrite).guide("bounds") });
        };
        if !read.seeks.is_empty() && read.order_keys.is_empty() && !matches!(position, Position::Aggregate | Position::Exists) && !(position == Position::Domain && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Job) {
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_BOUND", "a seek needs an explicit page bound", format!("{} first 50 by {}", self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(read.clone())), index.name)).guide("bounds"));
        }
        Ok((ast::RangeSpec { order: ast::Order::Asc, take: bound.maximum() as u32, cursor: None, site: read.site.clone() }, bound))
    }

    pub(super) fn check_meter_prefix(&mut self, read: &ReadExpr, scan: &Scan, aggregate: bool) -> Fallible<()> {
        if scan.take != Bound::Meter || snapback4_core::engine::rules::scan_visibility(self.schema, scan).uniform { return Ok(()); }
        let table = &self.schema.tables[&scan.table];
        let index = &table.indexes[&scan.index];
        let pinned = index.columns().into_iter().take(scan.prefix.len()).collect::<std::collections::BTreeSet<_>>();
        let used = snapback4_core::engine::rules::deciding_fields(&scan.table, table, &table.rules.read);
        let missing = used.difference(&pinned).cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>();
        let mut leading = missing.clone();
        for column in index.columns().into_iter().skip(scan.prefix.len()) { if !leading.contains(&column) { leading.push(column); } }
        let mut page = read.clone();
        page.range = Some(ast::RangeSpec { order: ast::Order::Asc, take: 50, cursor: Some(Box::new(A::Name { name: "c".into(), site: read.site.clone() })), site: read.site.clone() });
        // @ref LLP 3000.028#4-put-the-rules-where-the-builder-meets-them — preserve the loop, callers and truncation cost in the reason.
        let jobs = self.call_chain.last().filter(|_| self.call_chain.len() > 1)
            .map(|(helper, _)| self.helper_job_callers(helper)).unwrap_or_default();
        let query_site = &self.call_chain[0].1;
        let declaration = self.catalog.source.declarations.get(&query_site.to_string());
        if aggregate && (self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Query || jobs.is_empty() || declaration.is_none()) {
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#11 — a total has no first-N repair (app-farm lane 087).
            let deciding = if missing.is_empty() { leading.join(", ") } else { missing.iter().map(|c| format!(".{c}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ") };
            let grant = snapback4_core::engine::rules::grant_decision(self.schema, scan);
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#17.8 — a readable grant the program requires before the total (`require exists staff[viewer]`)
            // makes every row readable for every caller who reaches it, which the server decides once (read.rs `granted`).
            let guard = grant.as_ref().map_or_else(String::new, |grant| format!("; or guard it with the grant, so every caller who reaches it reads every row: `require {} else …` before it (or `if {}:` around it)", grant.quarry(), grant.quarry()));
            let diagnostic = Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_BOUND", format!("{} by {}: a total over rows the viewer may only partly read is not decided by its pinned columns, so no page bound makes it exact", scan.table, scan.index),
                format!("pin {deciding} with where equalities so one verdict covers every row (the total then covers only that scope){guard}; {}. If the maintained count keeps the source's private read rule, its group keys must carry every field that rule reads (including {deciding}); a public total instead deliberately discloses the count across hidden rows; or return a `first N` page with a cursor and fold it in the client", self.total_view_recipe(read, scan))).guide("bounds");
            if grant.is_some() && self.caller_kind == ProgramKind::Query && self.call_chain.len() == 1 {
                self.pending_totals.push((scan.site.clone(), diagnostic));
                return Ok(());
            }
            return Err(diagnostic);
        }
        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Query || jobs.is_empty() || declaration.is_none() {
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_BOUND", format!("{} by {}: a meter-bounded prefix needs a read rule uniformly decided by its pinned columns", scan.table, scan.index),
                format!("use `first N`: `{}` (declare `c: cursor ?` and follow its cursor); for a complete read, scan by an index leading with {} and pin the deciding columns (N_NEVER_COMPLETE)", self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(page)), leading.join(", "))).guide("bounds"));
        }
        let chain = self.call_chain.iter().map(|(name, site)| format!("{name} (at {site})")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" -> ");
        let job = format!("the system job caller(s) {}", jobs.join(", "));
        let authored = self.catalog.read_origins.get(&read.site.to_string()).cloned().unwrap_or_else(|| self.catalog.source.expression(&A::Read(read.clone())));
        let rule = if missing.is_empty() { "read rule".into() } else { format!("{}-based read rule", missing.join(", ")) };
        // @ref LLP 3000.007#23 — a whole-query replacement points to that declaration, never its shared helper.
        let end = query_site.line + declaration.unwrap().lines().count() as u32 - 1;
        let context = format!("; viewer query caller chain: {chain}. `{authored}` (at {}) does not decide its {rule}; a helper grants no system authority. The replacement is for the entire unused viewer query `{}`: replace lines {}-{end} in {} with the printed comment. Keep the complete scheduled job. A separately named paged preview needs its own cursor; `first 50` is a partial preview, not an equivalent complete total. For a complete per-group read, pin {}. Inner recipient work must fit one chunk even when the outer sweep adapts its chunk size; an oversized recipient parks with E_BOUND rather than committing a partial total", read.site, self.call_chain[0].0, query_site.line, query_site.file, leading.join(", "));
        Err(Diagnostic::new(query_site, "E_QUARRY_BOUND", format!("{} by {}: a meter-bounded prefix needs a read rule uniformly decided by its pinned columns{context}", scan.table, scan.index),
            format!("-- {} removed: keep the complete helper under {job}'s chunk meter.", self.call_chain[0].0)).guide("bounds"))
    }

    /// A maintained view that keeps this total, rendered from the author's own
    /// table, column and group (lanes 007 062 067: "hard to map to this schema").
    // @ref LLP 3000.036#13.10 — a total over partly readable rows: the view discloses the total, never the rows.
    fn total_view_recipe(&self, read: &ReadExpr, scan: &Scan) -> String {
        use crate::bind::intent::{column, split};
        let table = &scan.table;
        let (kind, of) = self.total.clone().unwrap_or(("count", None));
        // The group: the read's equalities, in its index order.
        let mut groups: Vec<(String, String)> = read.keys.iter().filter_map(|key| key.column.clone().map(|column| (column, print::expression(&key.value)))).collect();
        let mut filters = Vec::new();
        if let Some(filter) = read.filter.clone() { split(*filter, &mut filters); }
        for filter in &filters {
            if let A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = filter && op == "=" && let Some(col) = column(left)
                && super::super::intent::independent(right) && !groups.iter().any(|(c, _)| c == col) {
                groups.push((col.to_owned(), print::expression(right)));
            }
        }
        let suffix = if kind == "count" { "Count" } else { "Total" };
        let name = format!("{table}{suffix}");
        let aggregate = match &of { Some(column) => format!("sum {table} of {column}"), None => format!("count {table}") };
        let (readers, who) = match snapback4_core::engine::rules::grant_decision(self.schema, scan) {
            Some(grant) => (grant.quarry(), "the callers that grant admits"),
            None => ("allow".to_owned(), "everyone; narrow it to the readers the total is for"),
        };
        if groups.is_empty() {
            // @ref LLP 3000.036#17.2 — a view needs a group key (the core refuses an empty `by`): a whole-table
            // total groups by a column every row holds the same value of (lane 020 pasted `count t` and got E_QUARRY_SYNTAX).
            let key = (1..).map(|n| if n == 1 { "totalKey".to_owned() } else { format!("totalKey{n}") })
                .find(|key| !table_columns(self.schema, table).contains(key)).unwrap();
            return format!("or keep the total in a view, which discloses the total, never the rows; a view groups by a key, so give every {table} row the same one: add `{key}: enum('all') default 'all'` and `immutable {key}` to {table}, declare `view {name}: {aggregate} by .{key}` with `read <- {readers}` ({who}), then read `{name}['all']`");
        }
        let by = format!(" by {}", groups.iter().map(|(c, _)| format!(".{c}")).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "));
        let lookup = format!("{name}[{}]", groups.iter().map(|(_, v)| v.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "));
        format!("or keep the total in a view, which discloses the total, never the rows: `view {name}: {aggregate}{by}` with `read <- {readers}` ({who}), then read `{lookup}`")
    }

    /// Arguments belong to the caller: bind and snapshot each one before
    /// introducing any parameter. `new` and reads are evaluated once, in
    /// source order, even when the helper uses the parameter repeatedly.
    fn helper_arguments(&mut self, name: &str, declared: &[ast::ArgDecl], args: &[A], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<BTreeMap<String, Binding>> {
        if self.depth >= MAX_EXPANSION_DEPTH {
            return Err(unsupported(site, "helpers nested deeper than eight levels", "flatten the helper"));
        }
        if args.len() != declared.len() {
            return Err(type_error(site, format!("{name} takes {} arguments; received {}", declared.len(), args.len()), format!("{name}({})", declared.iter().map(|a| a.name.clone()).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))));
        }
        let mut bindings = BTreeMap::new();
        for (declared, given) in declared.iter().zip(args) {
            if bindings.contains_key(&declared.name) {
                return Err(unknown(&declared.site, format!("duplicate argument {}", declared.name), "rename one helper parameter"));
            }
            let expected = Ty::from_scalar(&declared.type_);
            // Helpers are polymorphic over same-table row snapshots; retain their
            // existing row parameter semantics. Only a literal needs id conversion.
            let (expr, ty) = if expected.required().is_identity() && matches!(given, A::Literal { .. }) { self.expr_as_identity(given, out, &expected)? } else { self.expr(given, out)? };
            // @ref LLP 3000.036.002#8 — call-site expansion preserves a same-table row; references keep their existing meaning.
            let expected = match (expected.required(), ty.required()) {
                (Ty::Ref(table), Ty::Row(row)) if table == row && (!ty.is_optional() || expected.is_optional()) => {
                    if expected.is_optional() { Ty::Row(row).optional() } else { Ty::Row(row) }
                }
                (Ty::Ref(table), Ty::WriteRow(row)) if table == row && (!ty.is_optional() || expected.is_optional()) => {
                    if expected.is_optional() { Ty::WriteRow(row).optional() } else { Ty::WriteRow(row) }
                }
                _ => expected,
            };
            if !ty.assignable_to(&expected) {
                return Err(type_error(given.site(), format!("{name}'s {} is {}; got {}", declared.name, expected.name(), ty.name()), match (ty.required(), expected.required()) {
                    (Ty::Enum(variants), Ty::Text(max)) if variants.iter().any(|variant| variant.chars().count() > max as usize) => format!("change {name}'s parameter to `{}: {}`; not every variant fits text <={max}", declared.name, ty.name()),
                    _ if ty.is_optional() && !expected.is_optional() => self.parent_rewrite(given).unwrap_or_else(|| format!("{} — require this argument once before calling {name}; absent or hidden rows refuse NOT_FOUND", print::expression(&A::Assert { value: Box::new(given.clone()), code: "NOT_FOUND".into(), message: None, site: given.site().clone() }))),
                    _ => format!("pass {} for {}; a table parameter receives an id when called with a reference, or the same-table row snapshot when called with a row. To read fields from an id, bind `{table}[{}] ! else NOT_FOUND` in the helper; multiline fn bodies need `return value`", expected.name(), declared.name, declared.name, table = match expected.required() { Ty::Ref(t) => t, _ => "<table>".into() }),
                }));
            }
            let runtime = self.temp(&declared.name);
            self.maintenance_scopes.insert(runtime.clone(), format!("helper/{name}/parameter/{}", declared.name));
            out.push(Stmt::Let { name: runtime.clone(), value: expr });
            bindings.insert(declared.name.clone(), Binding { runtime, ty: expected.clone(), declared: expected, parameter: true, revision: 0, fields: BTreeMap::new(), error: None, origins: Vec::new() });
        }
        Ok(bindings)

    fn select_index(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, read: &ReadExpr, mode: ReadMode, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>, ast::IndexDecl, String)> {
        // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — retain bound keys for type selection and final lowering.
        // No cloned expression pass: nested lookups must not multiply binder work.
        let mut keys = Vec::<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>::new();
        let mixed = read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_some()) && read.keys.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none());
        let types = if mixed {
            let prefix = read.keys.iter().take_while(|key| key.column.is_none()).count();
            self.bind_key_parts(info, read, prefix, &mut keys, out)?;
            Some(keys.iter().map(|(_, expr, ty)| (ty.clone(), matches!(expr, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_))))).collect::<Vec<_>>())
        } else { None };
        let mut normalized = super::mixed_keys::read_typed(info, read, self.catalog, types.as_deref())?;
        self.name_bound_keys(info, &normalized, &mut keys)?;
        let read = normalized.as_ref();
        super::validate_key_names(read)?;
        let mut positional_reason = None;
        if !read.keys.is_empty() && read.keys.iter().all(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
            let declared = |index: &&ast::IndexDecl| !self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone()));
            let mut candidates = info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).filter(|index| match &read.index {
                Some(name) => &index.name == name,
                None => index.unique && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len(),
            }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
            if read.index.is_none() && candidates.len() > 1 {
                self.bind_key_parts(info, read, read.keys.len(), &mut keys, out)?;
                let types = keys.iter().map(|(_, expr, ty)| (ty.clone(), matches!(expr, Expr::Literal(Value::String(_))))).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                candidates = super::positional_keys::candidates(info, read, &types, &self.catalog.synthesized);
            }
            if read.index.is_none() && mode == ReadMode::Range && read.order_keys.is_empty() && candidates.len() == 1 {
                let scan = info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).find(|index| !index.unique && index.is_plain() && index.columns.len() > read.keys.len());
                let columns = scan.map(|index| index.columns.as_slice()).unwrap_or(&candidates[0].columns);
                let predicate = read.keys.iter().zip(columns).map(|(key, column)| format!(".{column} = {}", self.catalog.source.operand(&key.value))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" and ");
                return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN", "a positional key selects one declared unique row, not a scan prefix",
                    format!("{} where {predicate} first 20 by .id", read.table)).guide("reads"));
            }
            if candidates.len() != 1 {
                return Err(super::positional_keys::refusal(info, read, &candidates, self.catalog));
            }
            if read.index.is_none() {
                positional_reason = Some(if info.indexes.iter().filter(declared).filter(|index| index.unique && index.columns.len() == read.keys.len()).count() == 1 {
                    "only declared unique key of this arity"
                } else { "unique positional key selected by argument types" });
                normalized.to_mut().index = Some(candidates[0].name.clone());
            }
        }
        let read = normalized.as_ref();
        let point = mode != ReadMode::Range;
        self.bind_key_parts(info, read, read.keys.len(), &mut keys, out)?;
        let named = keys.iter().all(|(c, _, _)| c.is_some()) && !keys.is_empty();
        let mut selection = if read.index.is_some() { "explicit by" } else if named { "named equality columns" } else { "compatible positional key types" };
        // A `words(..)` index serves one read: `matching` with exactly its
        // leading columns as keys. Every other read sees plain indexes only.

            .indexes
            .iter()
            .filter(|index| read.index.as_ref().is_none_or(|explicit| explicit == &index.name))
            .filter(|index| read.seeks.iter().all(|b| index.columns.get(keys.len()) == Some(&b.column)))
            .filter(|index| if searching { index.searches_after(keys.len()) } else { index.words.is_none() })

                ReadMode::Point | ReadMode::FilteredPoint => index.unique && (index.columns.len() == keys.len() || (named && mode == ReadMode::FilteredPoint && info.total_unique(index) && index.columns.len() < keys.len())),

                }
            }
            _ => {
                let names: Vec<&str> = candidates.iter().map(|i| i.name.as_str()).collect();
                let changes = candidates.iter().filter(|index| !self.catalog.synthesized.contains(&(read.table.clone(), index.name.clone())))
                    .map(|index| crate::repair::ReadChange::Index(index.name.clone())).collect();
                Err(ambiguous(&read.site, format!("{} has {} indexes matching this key: {}; choose an index valid for this read and its arguments", read.table, names.len(), names.join(", ")), "")
                    .repair_read(&read.site, changes))
            }
        }

    fn key_ty(&self, info: &TableInfo, index: &ast::IndexDecl, column: &str) -> Ty {
        if index.computed.contains_key(column) { return Ty::Int; }
        if column == "id" {
            return Ty::Ref(info.decl.name.clone());
        }
        info.columns.get(column).map(Ty::from_scalar).unwrap_or(Ty::Unknown)
    }

    fn order_keys(&mut self, info: &TableInfo, index: &ast::IndexDecl, keys: Vec<(Option<String>, Expr, Ty)>) -> Fallible<Vec<Expr>> {
        let named = keys.iter().all(|(c, _, _)| c.is_some());
        if !named {
            for (position, (_, _, t)) in keys.iter().enumerate() {
                let expected = self.key_ty(info, index, &index.columns[position]);
                if !t.comparable(&expected) {
                    return Err(type_error(&index.site, format!("key {} is {}; {}.{}.{} is {}", position + 1, t.name(), info.decl.name, index.name, index.columns[position], expected.name()), format!("use {} for key {}", expected.name(), position + 1)));
                }
            }
            return Ok(keys.into_iter().map(|(_, e, _)| e).collect());
        }
        let mut ordered = Vec::new();
        for column in index.columns.iter().take(keys.len()) {
            let Some(position) = keys.iter().position(|(c, _, _)| c.as_deref() == Some(column)) else {
                return Err(unknown(&index.site, format!("key omits column {column} of {}.{}", info.decl.name, index.name), format!("name each prefix column once: {}", index.columns.iter().take(keys.len()).cloned().collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", "))));
            };
            let expected = self.key_ty(info, index, column);
            if !keys[position].2.comparable(&expected) {
                return Err(type_error(&index.site, format!("{column} is {}; {}.{column} is {}", keys[position].2.name(), info.decl.name, expected.name()), format!("use {} for {column}", expected.name())));
            }
            ordered.push(keys[position].1.clone());
        }
        Ok(ordered)
    }

    fn walk(&mut self, table: &str, seed: &A, column: &str, depth: u32, site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(Recurse, Ty)> {
        let info = self.table(table, site)?;
        let Some(next) = info.columns.get(column) else {
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{table} has no column {column}"), name_rewrite(column, info.columns.keys().map(String::as_str), None)));
        };
            }
        }
    }

    /// `t[row]` where `row` is a present row of `t`: the same read as `t[row.id]`.
    fn row_as_key(&self, read: &ReadExpr) -> Option<ReadExpr> {
        if read.keys.len() != 1 || read.keys[0].column.is_some() || read.range.is_some() || read.filter.is_some() || read.matching.is_some() || !read.seeks.is_empty() || read.index.is_some() || !read.order_keys.is_empty() { return None; }
        let key = &read.keys[0].value;
        if !matches!(key, A::Name { .. }) { return None; }
        let (_, ty) = self.clone().expr(key, &mut setup::Body::default()).ok()?;
        if !matches!(ty, Ty::Row(ref table) | Ty::WriteRow(ref table) if *table == read.table) { return None; }
        let mut by_id = read.clone();
        by_id.keys[0].value = A::Member { base: Box::new(key.clone()), name: "id".into(), site: key.site().clone() };
        Some(by_id)
    }

    fn write_key(&mut self, info: &'a TableInfo, keys: &[ast::KeyPart], site: &Site, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<WriteKey> {
        if keys.is_empty() {
            return Err(unknown(site, format!("{} needs a key: which row?", info.decl.name), format!("{}[id]", info.decl.name)));
        }
        // A row value as the key addresses the row by its id.
        if keys.len() == 1 && keys[0].column.is_none() {
            let mut row_setup = setup::Body::default();
            let (e, t) = self.expr(&keys[0].value, &mut row_setup)?;
            if let Ty::Row(table) | Ty::WriteRow(table) = t.required()
                && table == info.decl.name
            {
                out.extend(row_setup);
                let id = self.temp("key");
                out.push(Stmt::Let { name: id.clone(), value: ir::field(e, "id") });
                return Ok(WriteKey { index: "byId".into(), key: vec![ir::var(&id)], alternate: false });
            }
        }
        let read = ReadExpr { cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), table: info.decl.name.clone(), keys: keys.to_vec(), index: None, range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new(), site: site.clone() };
        let (bound, index, _) = self.select_index(info, &read, ReadMode::Point, out)?;
        super::point_key::check(info, &index, &read, &bound.iter().map(|(column, _, ty)|
            (column.as_deref(), !ty.is_optional() && *ty != Ty::Unknown)).collect::<Vec<_>>(), false)?;
        let key = self.order_keys(info, &index, bound)?;

### 5.2 Composition: helpers with bodies, jobs with preludes

**8. `fn` takes a body.** Today `fn` is one expression and `proc` is a
block that returns nothing, so the 0.1.0 builder wrote the receipt
fan-out three times (`send`, `sendMedia`, `sendPoll`) and the 0.2.6
builder wrote its receipt state as three nested one-line `fn`s. LLP
2000.000 §6 named the split as pricing residue. Proposed: one `fn`,
declared with an expression or with a block ending in `return`, callable
in expression position anywhere; expanded inline as helpers are today
(`bind/program/control.rs`), so a write inside a body is admitted exactly
when the caller may write. **Loop placement also inherits the caller's
enclosing loops:** a helper inside a job's recipient sweep performs ordinary
per-recipient loops; a helper at the job's top level still receives the
automatic-sweep and stable-domain checks. `break` and `continue` remain
lexically scoped to loops inside the helper. A query calling a writing `fn`
is a placement error at the call site, with the site named. `proc` is deleted with the
rewrite `fn` (rules: delete, don't deprecate).

```quarry
fn receiptState(cid: conversations, sender: principal, seq: int):
  unread = 0
  undelivered = 0
  for m in memberships[cid] first 50 by byConversationPrincipal:
    if m.principal = sender or m.joinedSequence >= seq:
      continue
    if m.readSequence < seq:
      unread = unread + 1
    if m.deliveredSequence < seq:
      undelivered = undelivered + 1
  return 'read' if unread = 0 else ('delivered' if undelivered = 0 else 'sent')
```

**2026-09-27 — repeated pure calls (0.4.5 lane QE, calibration #22).**
A null test narrows a repeated `fn` call like a field path of a lexical
snapshot when the expanded helper reads no table, writes nothing and reads
neither `now` nor another changing input (the commit/read clock, a minted id
or connection state). Every argument must be a lexical name, a field path
of a lexical snapshot, a literal, or another such pure call. For example,
`now + windows.ttl(c.disappearing) if windows.ttl(c.disappearing) != null else null`
compiles. A database read used directly as an argument does not qualify;

    /// The fixed charge a delete of a `parent` row reserves before it probes,
    /// cascades or clears any row, over every meter: the closure's rows and
    /// their identities, every restrict probe (one seek per closure row, plus
    /// the closure rows it skips), every set-null row, and one engine write of
    /// each, priced from the source-write plan the engine already exports. A
    /// function of the schema only — caps, the reference graph, each table's
    /// indexes and row size, and the exported plans — so a meter, an `E_BOUND`
    /// abort or the physical allowance never depends on how many (hidden) rows
    /// exist, and every host charges alike: a device's projection keeps every
    /// table that can carry an action and every exported price. `None` when the
    /// closure is unbounded (a cycle or a missing cap) or a written table has no
    /// fixed write price. Heap is the closure's identity scratch plus the largest
    /// single row's transient work, plus event images retained until commit.
    // @ref LLP 3000.039#5.1 — the reservation is schema-fixed; LLP 3000.015 §2's law for work shaped by hidden rows.
    pub fn delete_reservation(&self, parent: &str) -> Option<Price> {
        let bounds = self.closure_bounds(parent)?;
        let mut price = [0_u64; 7];
        let mut heap = 0_u64;
        let mut identities = 0_u64;
        let mut gathered = 0_u64;
        let rows_of = |name: &str| bounds.iter().find(|(t, _)| t == name).map_or(0, |(_, n)| *n);

            if table != parent {

                heap = heap.max(unit[4]);

                    // rows that name it (each names one target per reference), then stops

        // delete, with the root's and one page of child ids.

that could conceal a model edit. Explicit `SNAPBACK4_LEAN_ORACLE` retains its
caller-vouched bypass. A stale/corrupt local build is repaired before validating
the fix; no model or engine semantics change.

**The proofs:**

- determinism;
- the value order is total and agrees with the key encoding;
- no read discloses a row its rules hide;
- no write succeeds without write authority;
- `complete: true` is never reported for a read that omitted a qualifying
  row.

**Independence.** The model's author works from the language card
(`snapback4 guide language`), LLP 3000.007 and the value-model LLPs, and
does not read the binder or the interpreter. Underspecified points go to an
`OPEN.md` beside the model and are resolved there, with the engine's
behaviour stated, before they are encoded.

**The emitter and the comparison.** A Lean emitter in `snapback4-lang`
(beside `emit/rust.rs`) embeds programs in the core fragment, and reports
any other program as unsupported. The Stage 1 harness gains the Lean model
as a further oracle on that fragment. The model is not normative: when it
and the engine disagree, the disagreement is classified as a Rust defect, a
model defect, or an open language question, and an open question comes to
Charlie with both behaviours stated.
**Status (2026-10-08): the emitter and the Lean oracle are built.**

- **The emitter** (`snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean.rs`) translates a checked
  program's surface tree, not the binder's IR, so the binder stays under
  test. It takes the few types it needs (literal typing beside ids,
  positional keys) from the declarations the checker accepted. Anything
  outside the fragment is a named unsupported reason, never a partial
  translation. `snapback4 check --emit-lean <file.q>` prints one file.
- **The runner** is `quarry-oracle` (`snapback4/lean/Oracle.lean`): one JSON
  case per line in, one tagged outcome per line out, one process per worker.
  The model's semantics were not changed for I/O.
- **The oracle** is `snapback4-difftest run-lean`. It compares the engine
  (SQLite, default ceilings) with the model on kind, refusal code, tagged
  data, `complete` and `next` presence. Each side then continues from its own
  cursor, and a mutation's `observe` reread runs on each side's resulting
  store. Meter fields and sites are not compared. Lean-only generator
  variants (`lean::generate`) aim at `OPEN.md`: per-effect and named update
  rules, a probe target hidden by its read rule, nullable and id orders, and
  residual filters.
- **The nightly runner** builds `quarry-oracle` before its campaigns and

//! Complete source-write reservation plans. Only prices survive projection.
// @ref LLP 3000.022#14.3 — one calculation for the compiler and authoritative writer.
use super::*;
use crate::engine::row_copy_ceiling;

// steps, indexes, examined, written rows, transient heap, output, rule probes.
pub type Price = [u64; 7];
pub const COLUMNS: usize = 0;
pub const ASSETS: usize = 1;
pub const AGGREGATES: usize = 2;
pub const ROW_SOURCE: usize = 3;
pub const ROW_MEMBER: usize = 4;
pub const ALLOCATION: usize = 5;
pub const COUNTER: usize = 6;
pub const CAPTURE: usize = 7;
pub const BIRTH: usize = 8;
pub const VIEW_READ: usize = 9;
#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct ActionEvents {
    pub heap_per_write: u64,
    pub steps_per_write: u64,
    pub tables: Vec<String>,
}
#[derive(Clone, Debug, Default, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]
pub struct WriteReservation {
    pub prices: [Price; 8],
    /// v2 adds owner initialization; absence identifies a legacy v1 plan.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub birth: Option<Price>,
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub view_read: Option<Price>,
    /// Complete event price/envelope survives projection of private maintains.
    #[serde(default, skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none")]
    pub action_events: Option<ActionEvents>,
    /// Data-dependent skipped work cannot be admitted from a fixed price.
    pub server_only: bool,
}
impl WriteReservation {
    pub fn price(&self, phase: usize) -> Price {
        if phase == BIRTH { self.birth.unwrap_or_default() } else if phase == VIEW_READ { self.view_read.unwrap_or_default() } else { self.prices[phase] }
    }
    #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
    fn is_zero(&self) -> bool {
        self.prices == [[0; 7]; 8] && self.birth.unwrap_or_default() == [0; 7]
            && self.view_read.unwrap_or_default() == [0; 7] && self.action_events.is_none() && !self.server_only
    }
}
fn add(to: &mut Price, price: Price) {
    for (to, price) in to.iter_mut().zip(price) { *to = to.saturating_add(price); }
}
pub fn slots(kind: &ColumnType, in_list: bool) -> u64 {
    match kind {
        ColumnType::Optional(inner) => slots(inner, in_list),
        ColumnType::List { of, max } | ColumnType::ListRange { of, max, .. } => slots(of, true).saturating_mul(u64::from(*max)),
        ColumnType::Image { .. } | ColumnType::Video { .. } | ColumnType::File { .. } if in_list => 1,
        _ => 0,
    }
}
pub fn asset_price(table: &Table) -> Price {
    let n = table.columns.values().map(|k| slots(k, false)).fold(0_u64, u64::saturating_add);
    [n.saturating_mul(8), 0, n.saturating_mul(4), n.saturating_mul(2), n.saturating_mul(16_384), 0, 0]
}
pub fn counter_price(scope: &Table) -> Price {
    let mut price = [2, 0, 0, 1, row_copy_ceiling(scope)*4, 0, 0];
    add(&mut price, asset_price(scope));
    price
}

    let parent = &schema.tables[&through.parent];
    [through.fields.len() as u64+2, 0, 1, 0,
     row_copy_ceiling(parent)*2+row_copy_ceiling(table), 0,

}

fn row_price(schema: &Schema, m: &Maintain, recipients: u64) -> Price {

}

    /// A heap-bound allocation can be written; the row validator checks its
    /// actual length. It cannot claim a smaller bound inside an inline helper.
    pub(crate) fn writable_to(&self, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
        match (self, expected) {
            (Ty::Optional(a), Ty::Optional(b)) => a.writable_to(b),
            (a, Ty::Optional(b)) => a.writable_to(b) || *a == Ty::Null,
            (Ty::Text(u32::MAX), Ty::Text(_)) => true,
            (Ty::List(a, am), Ty::List(b, bm)) => a.writable_to(b) && (*am == u32::MAX || am <= bm),
            _ => self.assignable_to(expected),
        }
    }

    /// Whether a value of `self` may be stored where `expected` is declared.
    pub fn assignable_to(&self, expected: &Ty) -> bool {
        if self == expected {
            return true;
        }
        match (self, expected) {
            (Ty::Null, Ty::Optional(_)) => true,
            (Ty::Optional(inner), Ty::Optional(target)) => inner.assignable_to(target),
            (value, Ty::Optional(target)) => value.assignable_to(target),
            (Ty::Text(a), Ty::Text(b)) => a <= b,

            // checks membership where it knows the literal.

            (Ty::Id, Ty::Ref(_)) | (Ty::Ref(_), Ty::Id) | (Ty::Id, Ty::Principal) | (Ty::Principal, Ty::Id) => true,
            (Ty::Ref(a), Ty::Ref(b)) => a == b,

//! Admission of replica-independent unique point keys. Populations and
//! maintained null groups have their own semantics.
use super::catalog::TableInfo;
use crate::{ast::{IndexDecl, ReadExpr}, Diagnostic};

// Arguments remain in authored order, with the binder's normalized column
// names and presence facts (including control-flow narrowing).
pub(super) fn check(table: &TableInfo, index: &IndexDecl, read: &ReadExpr, arguments: &[(Option<&str>, bool)], rule: bool) -> Result<(), Diagnostic> {
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — the complete maintained group is unique even with nulls.
    // Extra predicates (such as value = 1) may use a secondary access index.
    // A partial group or a value-only key must still pass ordinary admission.
    if table.maintained.is_some() && table.indexes.iter().any(|key| key.name == "byGroup"
        && key.columns.iter().all(|column| index.columns.contains(column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column))) {
        return Ok(());
    }
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — a fully pinned superset of a required unique key is itself unique.
    // Preserve role-bearing rule/postcondition indexes without adding a
    // redundant uniqueness constraint or changing their physical selection.
    let unique = index.unique || table.indexes.iter().any(|key| table.total_unique(key)
        && key.columns.iter().all(|column| index.columns.contains(column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column)));
    if !unique {
        let repair = if rule {
            "declare a non-null unique key for this probe; table rules cannot scan a population, so move population work into a query or mutation before storing the fact the rule tests"
        } else {
            "use a bounded population read (`where … first N`) or declare a non-null unique key"
        };
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_POINT_KEY",
            format!("{}.{} is not unique: a point probe can choose different tied rows across replicas, even with non-null arguments", read.table, index.name),
            repair).guide("indexes"));
    }
    if !index.unique || table.maintained.is_some() { return Ok(()); }
    let named = arguments.iter().all(|(column, _)| column.is_some());
    let mut guards = Vec::new();
    let mut nulls = Vec::new();
    for (position, (column, present)) in arguments.iter().enumerate() {
        let column = if named { column.unwrap() } else { index.columns[position].as_str() };
        if index.columns.iter().any(|key| key == column) && !index.computed.contains_key(column)
            && table.columns.get(column).is_some_and(|ty| ty.optional) && !present {
            if matches!(&read.keys[position].value, crate::ast::Expr::Literal { value: crate::ast::Literal::Null, .. }) { nulls.push(column); }
            else { guards.push(column); }
        }
    }
    if guards.is_empty() && nulls.is_empty() { return Ok(()); }
    // @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — null ties have no replica-independent point;
    // require proof of presence without changing total population equality.
    let mut advice = Vec::new();
    if !nulls.is_empty() { advice.push(format!("replace literal null with a non-null value for {}", nulls.join(", "))); }
    if !guards.is_empty() {
        advice.push(format!("prove non-null arguments for {} with required types or presence guards", guards.join(", ")));
    }
    // Recovery choices are design advice. Do not print a source edit without
    // a context proof (a population can need a different index or bound).
    Err(Diagnostic::new(&read.site, "E_QUARRY_NULL_KEY",
        format!("{}.{} is a nullable unique key: a null argument can tie several rows; a point is ambiguous across replicas because each device may hold different tied rows. {}; alternatively read a bounded population or key the row by id (guide indexes)", read.table, index.name, advice.join("; ")),
        "").guide("indexes"))
}

# E_UNIQUE

E_UNIQUE means a caller-readable row already holds this declared secondary unique key.
The refusal names `unique principal` (or the declared columns), never the
planner's internal index name. Update the existing row or choose a new key.
Declare `else CODE 'message'` on the key to use an application refusal instead.
`insert … if absent` returns null on a readable collision when doing nothing
is the intended operation. Every refused write rolls back the whole mutation.
An intrinsic id clash remains E_CONSTRAINT; a hidden clash remains the ambiguous
E_RULE (or NOT_FOUND for an unreadable write target). Keep private keys private.
See `guide writes` for constraints that govern every writer across hidden rows.

codex
The kept-parent guard is ineffective after schema lowering, so it can incorrectly suppress the unreadable-probe warning. I also found that required-row projection skips nullable reference key columns; I’m checking the explicit `.id` path to confirm this is a lowering defect.

//! A positional unique key followed by named tests is named-key sugar.
// @ref LLP 3000.013#2.2 — only declared unique keys resolve positions; extra equalities test one row.
use super::{catalog::{Catalog, TableInfo}, intent_walk as walk};
use crate::{ast::*, diag::{Diagnostic, Fallible, Site}, print};
use std::{borrow::Cow, collections::BTreeMap};

pub(super) fn keys<'a>(table: &TableInfo, parts: &'a [KeyPart], index: Option<&str>, site: &Site, synthesized: &std::collections::BTreeSet<(String, String)>, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, [KeyPart]>> {
    if !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_some()) || !parts.iter().any(|key| key.column.is_none()) { return Ok(Cow::Borrowed(parts)); }
    let prefix = parts.iter().take_while(|key| key.column.is_none()).count();
    if let Some(late) = parts[prefix..].iter().find(|key| key.column.is_none()) {
        let ordered = parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_none()).chain(parts.iter().filter(|key| key.column.is_some())).map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ");
        return Err(Diagnostic::new(&late.site, "E_QUARRY_KEY_ORDER", "positional key components come before named column tests",
            format!("{}[{ordered}]", table.decl.name)).guide("reads"));
    }
    let declared: Vec<_> = table.indexes.iter().filter(|key| key.unique
        && !synthesized.contains(&(table.decl.name.clone(), key.name.clone()))).collect();
    let candidates: Vec<_> = if let Some(types) = types {
        let read = ReadExpr { table: table.decl.name.clone(), keys: parts[..prefix].to_vec(), index: index.map(str::to_owned), site: site.clone(),
            cursor_prefix: None, computed: Default::default(), order_keys: Vec::new(), range: None, matching: None, filter: None, seeks: Vec::new() };
        super::positional_keys::candidates(table, &read, types, synthesized).into_iter()
            .filter(|key| key.unique && key.columns.len() == prefix).collect()
    } else {
        // Before types are known, do not silently turn `roles[viewer,
        // role = 'staff']` into an id lookup merely because byId has the
        // positional prefix's arity. A declared composite key covered by the
        // positional prefix plus named suffix must reach the typed pass, which
        // prints the fully named repair.
        let named: Vec<&str> = parts[prefix..].iter().filter_map(|part| part.column.as_deref()).collect();
        let partial_composite = declared.iter().any(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix && key.columns.len() <= parts.len()
            && key.columns[prefix..].iter().all(|column| named.contains(&column.as_str()))
            && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name));
        if partial_composite { Vec::new() }
        else { declared.iter().copied().filter(|key| key.columns.len() == prefix && index.is_none_or(|name| name == key.name)).collect() }
    };
    let chosen = match candidates.as_slice() {
        [chosen] if table.total_unique(chosen) => *chosen,
        _ => {
            let suggested = candidates.first().copied().or_else(|| declared.iter().copied().find(|key| key.columns.len() > prefix)).or_else(|| declared.first().copied());
            let descriptions = declared.iter().map(|key| format!("({})", key.columns.join(", "))).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(" or ");
            let rewrite = if let Some(key) = suggested {
                let mut named = key.columns.iter().enumerate().map(|(at, column)| {
                    let value = parts.get(at).filter(|_| at < prefix).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value))
                        .or_else(|| parts.iter().find(|part| part.column.as_ref() == Some(column)).map(|part| print::expression(&part.value)))
                        .unwrap_or_else(|| format!("<{column}>"));
                    format!("{column} = {value}")
                }).collect::<Vec<_>>();
                named.extend(parts[prefix..].iter().filter(|part| !key.columns.contains(part.column.as_ref().unwrap())).map(spelling));
                format!("{}[{}]", table.decl.name, named.join(", "))
            } else { format!("{}[id = <id>, {}]", table.decl.name, parts[prefix..].iter().map(spelling).collect::<Vec<_>>().join(", ")) };
            return Err(Diagnostic::new(site, "E_QUARRY_POSITIONAL_SCAN",
                format!("{} needs one full declared unique key with non-null columns before named tests; {prefix} positional components match {} keys; declared keys: {descriptions}", table.decl.name, candidates.len()), rewrite.clone())
                .source_rewrite(crate::diag::SourceRewriteKind::MixedKey, rewrite).guide("reads"));
        }
    };
    let mut named = parts.to_vec();
    for (part, column) in named.iter_mut().zip(&chosen.columns) { part.column = Some(column.clone()); }
    Ok(Cow::Owned(named))
}

fn spelling(part: &KeyPart) -> String {
    match &part.column { Some(column) => format!("{column} = {}", print::expression(&part.value)), None => print::expression(&part.value) }
}

pub(super) fn read<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    read_typed(table, read, catalog, None)
}

pub(super) fn read_typed<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    Ok(match keys(table, &read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site, &catalog.synthesized, types)? {
        Cow::Borrowed(_) => Cow::Borrowed(read),
        Cow::Owned(keys) => {
            let index = types.and_then(|types| super::positional_keys::candidates(table, read, types, &catalog.synthesized)
                .first().map(|index| index.name.clone())).or_else(|| read.index.clone());
            Cow::Owned(ReadExpr { keys, index, ..read.clone() })
        },
    })
}

/// Normalize before planning and sync inference, including helpers and shapes.
/// Invalid keys remain for the ordinary binder to diagnose at their use site.
pub(super) fn normalize(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    let tables: BTreeMap<_, _> = catalog.tables.clone();
    let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| {
        let (name, parts, index, site) = match value {
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => (&read.table, &mut read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site),
            Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } => (&*table, keys, None, &*site),
            _ => return,
        };
        if let Some(table) = tables.get(name)
            && let Ok(Cow::Owned(named)) = keys(table, parts, index, site, &catalog.synthesized, None) { *parts = named; }
    };
    for table in catalog.tables.values_mut() {
        for rule in &mut table.decl.rules { walk::expression(&mut rule.value, &mut visit); }
        for (value, _) in &mut table.decl.final_exists { walk::expression(value, &mut visit); }
    }
    for doc in &mut catalog.documents {
        walk::document(doc, &mut visit);
        for view in &mut doc.views { for branch in &mut view.branches {
            if let Some(predicate) = &mut branch.predicate { walk::expression(predicate, &mut visit); }
            walk::shape(&mut branch.fields, &mut visit);
        } }
    }
    catalog.shapes = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.shapes.iter().cloned().map(|shape| (shape.name.clone(), shape))).collect();
    catalog.functions = catalog.documents.iter().flat_map(|doc| doc.functions.iter().cloned().map(|function| (function.name.clone(), function))).collect();
}
//! Referential actions on delete (LLP 3000.039 §3): before a delete removes
//! its row, reserve a schema-fixed charge, close over the `cascade`
//! references, refuse while a `restrict` reference still holds a closure row
//! from outside the closure, clear `set null` references outside it, and
//! remove the closure children first. The caller then removes the row itself.
// @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — closure first; probes exclude the closure; deletion dominates set null.
use crate::engine::runner::Runner;
use crate::{
    error::{MeterKind, RuntimeError},
    meter::{Ceilings, Cut, Meter},
    rows::{Bounds, Dir},
    schema::{Effect, references::{OnDelete, Referencing}},
    value::Value,
};
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

impl Runner<'_> {
    pub(super) fn apply_reference_actions(&mut self, table_name: &str, old: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        if !schema.is_referenced(table_name) { return Ok(()); }
        let Some(price) = schema.delete_reservation(table_name) else {
            // A device without a reached child's exported price cannot charge
            // what the server charges; the server always has every price.
            if self.device { return Err(RuntimeError::Prediction(format!("deleting {table_name} needs every referencing table's write price; queued without a local delete"))); }
            return Err(RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{table_name}'s delete actions have no reservation: a cascading reference lacks its cap or its child's write price (guide writes)")));
        };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#4.2 — what the commit publishes is fixed by the schema, not by the rows the actions reach.
        let closure = schema.action_tables(table_name);
        for table in &closure.written {
            if schema.visible(table) { self.written_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
            self.action_tables.insert(table.clone());
        }
        for table in &closure.read { self.read_tables.insert(table.clone()); }
        if !closure.written.is_empty() { self.tx.widen_change_evidence(); }
        for (kind, amount) in [(MeterKind::Steps, price[0]), (MeterKind::IndexEntries, price[1]), (MeterKind::Examined, price[2]), (MeterKind::RowsWritten, price[3]), (MeterKind::OutputBytes, price[5]), (MeterKind::RuleProbes, price[6])] {
            if amount > 0 { self.meter.charge(kind, amount)?; }
        }
        // Pending event images survive the closure and subsequent statements.
        // Keep their schema-fixed reservation until the request ends.
        let events = schema.delete_event_heap(table_name).unwrap_or(0);
        self.meter.heap(events)?;
        let mark = self.meter.mark();
        self.meter.heap(price[4].saturating_sub(events))?;
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — draw the reservation's rows from the request's physical allowance before the first seek.
        if !self.context.is_system() { self.tx.draw(table_name, price[2])?; }
        let meter = std::mem::replace(&mut self.meter, Meter::new(Ceilings::unbounded(), Cut::Abort));
        let prepaid = self.tx.reference_prepaid(true);
        let action = std::mem::replace(&mut self.engine_action, true);
        let result = self.reference_closure(table_name, old);
        self.engine_action = action;
        self.tx.reference_prepaid(prepaid);
        self.meter = meter;
        self.meter.release_heap(mark);
        result
    }

    /// Steps 3–6. The walk holds identities, never images: each child scan
    /// streams `PAGE` rows per seek, and every write rereads its row current.
    fn reference_closure(&mut self, root_table: &str, root: &Value) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        let mut seen: BTreeSet<(String, String)> = BTreeSet::from([(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.as_str()?.to_owned())]);
        // (Vectors, not maps: the web device pays for every new collection type.)
        let mut visits: Vec<(String, Value)> = vec![(root_table.to_owned(), root.field("id")?.clone())];
        // 3. Close over cascades, breadth first. Every reached row's referencing groups must be held on a device.
        let mut next = 0;
        while next < visits.len() {
            let (table, id) = visits[next].clone();
            next += 1;
            for r in schema.referencing(&table) {
                if self.device && !through_reference(&r) { self.prove_reference_held(&r, &id)?; }
                if r.action != OnDelete::Cascade { continue; }
                self.each_child(&r, &id, true, &mut |_, child| {
                    if seen.insert((r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned())) { visits.push((r.child.to_owned(), child)); }
                    Ok(true)
                })?;
            }
        }
        // 4. Restrict: every probe runs, streaming past closure rows (the root
        // included) to the first outside witness; the first occupied reference
        // in schema order is reported. One reference's probes visit each closure
        // row at most once, plus one witness per probe (LLP 3000.039 §5.1).
        let mut occupied: Option<(String, String, String)> = None;
        for (table, id) in &visits {
            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::Restrict || through_reference(&r) { continue; }
                let mut outside = false;
                self.each_child(&r, id, false, &mut |_, child| {
                    outside = !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.as_str()?.to_owned()));
                    Ok(!outside)
                })?;
                let candidate = (r.child.to_owned(), r.column.to_owned(), table.clone());
                if outside && occupied.as_ref().is_none_or(|o| candidate < *o) { occupied = Some(candidate); }
            }
        }
        if let Some((child, column, parent)) = occupied {
            #[cfg(feature = "sqlite")]
            self.refusing_clause(&child, &format!("{column}: {parent} (on delete restrict)"));
            let via = if parent == root_table { String::new() } else { format!(", through a {parent} row this delete cascades to") };
            return Err(RuntimeError::Structural { code: "E_REFERENCED", message: format!("{root_table} at {} is still referenced through {child}.{column} (on delete restrict){via}; delete or retarget those rows first, or declare the reference's action (guide writes)", self.statement_site) });
        }
        // 5. Set null outside the closure. Each group is gathered whole before
        // any of it is cleared: a clear can recount a sibling that shares the
        // reference's index and move it behind a page cursor. Each row is then
        // reread current before its write.
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#3 — gather, then clear: no row moves out of a walk it has not finished.
        for (table, id) in &visits {
            for r in schema.referencing(table) {
                if r.action != OnDelete::SetNull { continue; }
                let mut group: Vec<String> = Vec::new();
                self.each_child(&r, id, true, &mut |_, child| {
                    let child = child.as_str()?.to_owned();
                    if !seen.contains(&(r.child.to_owned(), child.clone())) { group.push(child); }
                    Ok(true)
                })?;
                for child in group {
                    let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, r.child, &child))?;
                    if let Some(current) = current.filter(|row| row.field(r.column).is_ok_and(|v| v == id)) {
                        self.clear_reference(r.child, r.column, current)?;
                    }
                }
            }
        }
        // 6. Delete the closure, children first, each from its current image.
        let reached: Vec<(String, String)> = visits[1..].iter().map(|(t, id)| Ok((t.clone(), id.as_str()?.to_owned()))).collect::<Result<_, RuntimeError>>()?;
        drop(visits);
        for (table, id) in deletion_order(schema, &reached) {
            let current = self.metered_scan(|runner, allowance| runner.tx.get(allowance, &table, &id))?;
            let Some(current) = current else { continue };
            self.delete_reached(&table, current)?;
        }
        Ok(())
    }

    /// Streams the ids of the live rows referencing `id` through `r` to
    /// `visit`, through its index, until `visit` answers false. A capped walk
    /// (cascade, set null) reads `PAGE` rows per seek and holds at most the
    /// reference's cap; a restrict probe reads one row per seek, so it examines
    /// nothing past its witness. Images are dropped before `visit` runs. Seeks
    /// start at the live suffix, so an expired backlog is never visited.
    fn each_child(&mut self, r: &Referencing<'_>, id: &Value, capped: bool, visit: &mut dyn FnMut(&mut Self, Value) -> Result<bool, RuntimeError>) -> Result<(), RuntimeError> {
        let schema = self.schema;
        let index = schema.reference_index(r.child, r.column).ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Schema(format!("{}.{} has no reference index", r.child, r.column)))?.to_owned();
        let (cap, page_rows) = if capped { (schema.reference_cap(r.child, r.column).map_or(u64::MAX, u64::from), PAGE) } else { (u64::MAX, 1) };
        // @ref LLP 3000.039#2.3 — seek on the effective deadline (a read-through child's `$deadline`), so no expired row is visited.
        let ranges = if r.table.expiry_column().is_some() && !through_reference(r) {
            let now = self.context.now.ok_or_else(|| RuntimeError::Input("delete clock is not pinned".into()))?;
            vec![(vec![id.clone(), Value::Null], None), (vec![id.clone()], Some(Value::Int(now)))]
        } else {
            vec![(vec![id.clone()], None)]
        };
 let base="table parents:\n  owner: principal\n  immutable owner\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer\ntable kids:\n  parent: parents on delete keep\n  owner: principal\n  unique parent\n  immutable parent,owner\n  read <- .owner = viewer\n  insert <- .owner = viewer and .parent.owner = viewer\nmutation probe(parent: parents):\n  p = parents[parent] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require not exists kids[parent = parent] else HAS_CHILD\n  return true\n";
 assert!(!checked(base).notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_UNREADABLE_PROBE"));
 for source in [base.replace("  insert <- .owner = viewer\ntable kids:","  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  delete <- .owner = viewer\ntable kids:"),base.replace("  immutable owner\n","  immutable owner\n  until: time\n  index until\n  expire at .until\n"),base.replace("table parents:\n","table roots:\n  public\n  delete <- allow\ntable parents:\n  root: roots on delete cascade\n  cap 1 by root\n")] {
  let b=checked(&source);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);assert!(b.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_UNREADABLE_PROBE"),"{:?}",b.notes);
 }
}

fn nested_read_alternatives_can_reach_the_custom_require(){
 let source="table rows:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- (.owner = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops') and viewer != 'blocked'\nmutation access(row: rows):\n  r = rows[row] ! else NOT_FOUND\n  require r.owner = viewer else NOT_OWNER\n  return r\n";
 let b=checked(source);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN"),"{:?}",b.notes);
 let owner=checked(&source.replace("(.owner = viewer or viewer = 'service:ops')", "(.owner = viewer or .owner = viewer)"));assert!(owner.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_REQUIRE_HIDDEN"));
}

 for source in [base.replace("  insert <- .owner = viewer\ntable kids:","  insert <- .owner = viewer\n  delete <- .owner = viewer\ntable kids:"),base.replace("  immutable owner\n","  immutable owner\n  until: time\n  index until\n  expire at .until\n")] {
  let b=checked(&source);assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(),"{:?}",b.diagnostics);assert!(b.notes.iter().any(|n|n.code=="N_UNREADABLE_PROBE"),"{:?}",b.notes);
 }

pub(super) fn read_typed<'a>(table: &TableInfo, read: &'a ReadExpr, catalog: &Catalog, types: Option<&[(crate::types::Ty, bool)]>) -> Fallible<Cow<'a, ReadExpr>> {
    Ok(match keys(table, &read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site, &catalog.synthesized, types)? {
        Cow::Borrowed(_) => Cow::Borrowed(read),
        Cow::Owned(keys) => {
            let index = types.and_then(|types| super::positional_keys::candidates(table, read, types, &catalog.synthesized)
                .first().map(|index| index.name.clone())).or_else(|| read.index.clone());
            Cow::Owned(ReadExpr { keys, index, ..read.clone() })
        },
    })
}

/// Normalize before planning and sync inference, including helpers and shapes.
/// Invalid keys remain for the ordinary binder to diagnose at their use site.
pub(super) fn normalize(catalog: &mut Catalog) {
    let tables: BTreeMap<_, _> = catalog.tables.clone();
    let mut visit = |value: &mut Expr| {
        let (name, parts, index, site) = match value {
            Expr::Read(read) | Expr::Exists { read, .. } | Expr::Created { read, .. } => (&read.table, &mut read.keys, read.index.as_deref(), &read.site),
            Expr::Write { table, keys, site, .. } => (&*table, keys, None, &*site),
            _ => return,

        if let Some(table) = tables.get(name)
            && let Ok(Cow::Owned(named)) = keys(table, parts, index, site, &catalog.synthesized, None) { *parts = named; }
    };
    for table in catalog.tables.values_mut() {
        for rule in &mut table.decl.rules { walk::expression(&mut rule.value, &mut visit); }
        for (value, _) in &mut table.decl.final_exists { walk::expression(value, &mut visit); }
    }

    /// Every reference into `parent` a delete consults (all but `keep`), in
    /// (child, column) order.
    pub fn referencing<'a>(&'a self, parent: &str) -> Vec<Referencing<'a>> {
        // A plain loop into a vector: iterator adapters monomorphize per call site on the web device.
        let mut out = Vec::new();
        for (child, table) in &self.tables {
            for (column, action) in &table.on_delete {
                if action.acts() && matches!(table.columns.get(column).map(ColumnType::required), Some(ColumnType::Ref { table: target }) if target == parent) {
                    out.push(Referencing { child, table, column, action: *action });
                }
            }
        }
        out
    }
